The SqueezeFS-interpreted ones: `ro` / `rw` (the reader posture — an `-o rw` beside `ro`/`--read-only` refuses), `interception`, `admin_uid=<uid>`, `hostnqn=<nqn>`,`hostid=<id>` (the per-mount NVMe-oF identity — pair-or-neither, wins over `SQUEEZEFS_HOSTNQN`/`SQUEEZEFS_HOSTID`), the per-class kernel TTLs (`attr_timeout`, `entry_timeout`, `dir_entry_timeout`, `negative_timeout`), the `max_background` / `congestion_threshold` INIT overrides, and `direct_device_true` — each documented in its section below. An explicit `writeback` / `writeback_cache` combined with `interception` refuses (KD-11). `default_permissions` is always set by the daemon (kernel-side mode/ACL checks); `allow_other` is the `--allow-other` flag's.
- `--no-writeback`: disable the FUSE writeback cache (enabled by default).
- `--read-only` (= `-o ro`): mount as a coherent **reader** (DLM S5) — no write lease, no `writer_claim`, no NVMe reservation, every plane refused, kernel `MS_RDONLY`, writeback cache off, and kernel/dentry TTLs derived from the writer's checkpoint cadence. Combining it with an explicit `-o rw` refuses loudly. Read the exact consistency guarantee first: [Read-only coherent mounts](#read-only-coherent-mounts--o-ro--one-writer-plus-n-readers).
- `--interception` (= `-o interception` = `SQUEEZEFS_IPC=1`): arm the L4 LD_PRELOAD interception session host for this mount — see [LD_PRELOAD interception](#ld_preload-interception--o-interception--security-posture--unsupported-mixes).
- `--write-verification` (+ `--write-verification-sample <N>`): opt-in read-after-write checksum verification.
- `--dismount-wait <secs>` / `--upload-delay <dur>`: staging drain window on dismount / background upload cadence.

### LD_PRELOAD interception (`-o interception`) — security posture & unsupported mixes

*Hands-on benchmarking walkthrough (build → mount → run → verify engagement): `QUICKSTART.md` → "Benchmarking the LD_PRELOAD Interception Path (Manual)". Measured reference numbers: `.benchmarks/2026-07-19-l4-interception-closing.md`.*

Opt-in at **both** ends (`docs/design-preload-interception.md`, v1 posture): the mount arms the session host (`--interception` / `-o interception` / `SQUEEZEFS_IPC=1`), and each app opts in with `LD_PRELOAD=libsqueezefs_il.so` (built with `cargo build -p squeezefs-preload --profile preload-release --features interposers` — the ONLY sanctioned build; a plain `--release` build refuses at compile time because the root profile's `panic="abort"` would turn shim panics into host-app aborts). Everything the shim cannot serve identically falls through to the real fd via kernel FUSE — **correctness never depends on interception**. Data ops on bound fds ride a shared-memory ring; warm reads serve synchronously from the daemon tiers (measured ~2× kernel-FUSE warm rand-4k on the dev substrate); everything else rides an async handoff into the exact FUSE handler bodies. The per-PR gate is `tests/run_preload_gate.sh` (leg 1 unprivileged; leg 2 root: parity + engagement + dup/close_range/lseek rows + kill-9 and fork-kill-parent soaks).

**KD-11 — interception forces kernel write-through.** `-o interception` flips the mount to `write_back = false` (the same knob `--no-writeback` drives): the default-on kernel writeback cache acks buffered writes before the daemon sees them, and a ring read (direct-to-daemon by construction) would miss them. Combining `-o interception` with an explicit `writeback`/`writeback_cache` request is a contradiction and **refuses the mount loudly**. Cost: buffered kernel-path small writes on interception mounts lose the kernel's dirty-page batching (interception mounts exist to take the ring path for exactly those writes).

**Direct link (`-lsqueezefs_il`) — the supported linked mode (SDK Tier 1, `docs/design-sdk.md` §4).** Applications may link the shim as an ordinary shared library instead of preloading it — same interposers, same fallback ladder, same engagement discipline; the shim is ctor-free (lazy first-call init), so load order needs no ceremony. What linked mode buys: it survives everything that strips `LD_PRELOAD` — **setuid/AT_SECURE binaries** (the dynamic linker ignores untrusted preloads there), `sudo`'s and systemd's environment scrubbing, and container images that never propagate env vars — making interception a property of the *binary*, not of how it was launched. Rules:
- **Link order requirement:** the shim must precede `libc` in the dependency list (automatic — `-lc` is appended last) and should come **first** in the link line so no other library can shadow the interposed symbols. Because modern toolchains default to `--as-needed`, wrap the reference: `cc app.c -Wl,--no-as-needed -lsqueezefs_il -Wl,--as-needed …` — otherwise an app that happens to reference no interposed symbol at static-link time silently drops the DT_NEEDED entry (no interception, no error). The cdylib carries `SONAME libsqueezefs_il.so` (so by-path links record a clean name; provide `-L`/rpath or `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` at run time) and `-z nodelete` (`pthread_atfork` handlers can never be unregistered — a loaded shim is permanent by design; `dlclose` is a structural no-op).
- **Failure-mode difference, stated:** a missing DT_NEEDED library is **fatal at exec** ("error while loading shared libraries"), whereas a missing `LD_PRELOAD` entry is only a loader warning. A linked binary must ship with its `.so`.
- **Same KD-7 rules, verbatim:** linked mode changes delivery, not the version lock — the shim still binds only against the identical `build_commit` + `IPC_ABI` (refusal ⇒ passthrough with the reason line), so a linked app must refresh its `libsqueezefs_il.so` alongside daemon upgrades (the `dist/<target>` same-commit pairing-folder discipline). App-cadence delivery with a compat window is the SDK program's charter (`docs/design-sdk.md` SKD-4), not the shim's.
- **Detection line:** on first contact with an interception-armed mount, a shim that was *not* named in `LD_PRELOAD` prints once per process: `squeezefs-il: active via direct link (DT_NEEDED), not LD_PRELOAD — same KD-7 build pairing applies` (also covers `dlopen` and `/etc/ld.so.preload` loads; a setuid binary whose ignored environment still names the shim misreports as preloaded — the line is diagnostic, never a correctness input). The gate battery pins linked-mode parity, ctor-context I/O, scope occupancy, and this line (`tests/run_preload_gate.sh` legs 1e / 2b-linked).

**Security posture (the §5.2 daemon fd screen is the boundary):**
- The bind credential is a **real open fd** passed over an abstract AF_UNIX socket (`SCM_RIGHTS`). The daemon re-derives everything from the received fd itself: `O_PATH` descriptions are refused outright (obtainable with search-only permission — accepting one would grant reads without read permission), non-regular files refuse, `st_dev` must match the mount, and per-op rights come strictly from the description's access mode **in both directions** (an `O_WRONLY` binding cannot ring-read; an `O_RDONLY` binding cannot ring-write — both surface as `EBADF`). `O_APPEND`, `O_SYNC`/`O_DSYNC`, and `O_TMPFILE`-class (unnamed regular file, `st_nlink == 0` — which also conservatively refuses open-then-unlinked fds; passthrough serves them) refuse at bind.
- **Version lock (forward-only):** sessions bind only between identical builds (`build_commit` equality + `IPC_ABI`). Degenerate identities — `unknown` (no-git tarball) or `-dirty` — refuse on *either* side; `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_ALLOW_DEV=1` is the dev-box override, **counted** in `.stats` `ipc_binds_dev_override` (nonzero outside dev boxes is a fleet-hygiene alarm).
- **Multi-user (`--allow-other`) posture:** any uid that can open files on the mount can establish sessions. Sessions are per-process, arenas are private mappings (no cross-process payload visibility), per-uid session caps and the R5 `ipc_session_arenas` budget component bound resource use (shed = refuse-new-sessions, never tearing live ones). The trust model is exactly POSIX-fd trust plus resource caps. `SO_PEERCRED` labels accounting and is defense-in-depth, not the authorizer.
- **Observability:** `.stats` carries the refusal ledger (`ipc_bind_refused_{version,nonce,flags,mode,budget,peercred}`), lifecycle gauges (`ipc_sessions_{active,total}`, `ipc_arena_bytes`, `ipc_binds`, `ipc_admission_refusals`) and two **must-stay-0 tripwires**: `ipc_descriptor_rejects` and `ipc_sessions_poisoned` — nonzero means a client bug or an attack (one loud log line per event).

**Unsupported mixes (documented contract, not detected):**
- **Concurrent cross-process `MAP_SHARED` mmap-writers + ring writers on the same file** — page-granularity writeback can clobber ring-written bytes (lost updates). Same-process mmap is fully handled (POSIX-7): the shim unbinds *all* in-process bindings on the mapped inode AND **poisons** the inode, so a later `open()` of the same file — or an `mmap()` that preceded the first open — can never re-arm the ring underneath a live mapping; that fd stays kernel-served for the process's lifetime (the poison set is fail-safe: if it ever fills, the whole mount degrades to kernel-served rather than forgetting an entry). Cross-process remains declared unsupported — a peer's mapping is invisible to this shim and the kernel never tells the daemon about mappings; run such workloads without the shim.
- **Cross-process buffered/mmap readers** can observe a bounded staleness window on ring-written data (same class as attr-TTL staleness); the daemon's `notify_inval_inode` handoff bounds it — fired on bind and rate-limited per `(ino, window)` on ring writes (`SQUEEZEFS_IPC_INVAL_WINDOW_MS`, default 1000; `.stats` `ipc_inval_{notifies,suppressed}`), delivered over the classical sideband even on armed over-uring sessions. **Size coherence is exempt from that window (POSIX-8):** a ring write that GROWS the file always fires an attrs-only invalidation (`.stats` `ipc_inval_attrs_only`) — no page-cache work — so `lseek(SEEK_END)` and `stat` never read a stale `i_size` and an append can never land at a stale offset; the last unbind of an inode fires one whole-inode shootdown so nothing the window suppressed outlives the bindings.
- **Mixed-ABI fd lifecycles**: apps that close *and* recreate fds exclusively through raw `syscall(2)`/io_uring (invisible to the shim) and then issue libc data calls on the reused number are unsupported under the shim (the per-op `(st_dev, st_ino)` revalidation the design discusses as a triage knob was never built — one `fstat` per op would re-spend the syscall budget the shim exists to delete; triage such an app by running it without the shim).
- **Containers with their own network namespace** *(solved in v1.1 — OQ-6)*: the abstract-socket rendezvous is per-netns, so pre-v1.1 such apps silently stayed on kernel FUSE. Since v1.1 the daemon **also binds a filesystem-path ctl socket** and advertises it in the bootstrap blob; the shim's connect ladder tries abstract first (same-netns fast path), then the path. **Operator contract for container fleets:** bind-mount the socket runtime dir into the container alongside the filesystem — default `/run/squeezefs` (root mounts) or `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/squeezefs`, else `/tmp/squeezefs-il-<uid>` (user mounts); override with `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_SOCKET_DIR=<dir>` (`none` disables, restoring the v1 zero-residue posture). The socket file is mode 0666 **because connecting is not a credential** — `SO_PEERCRED` + the daemon fd screen remain the security boundary, identical over both rendezvous. Note the user-mount default under `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` is a 0700 dir: other uids cannot reach it (user mounts serve same-uid apps; point `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_SOCKET_DIR` at a shared dir if you need more). A failed path bind degrades loudly to abstract-only and never fails the mount; the file is unlinked at daemon shutdown (zero residue restored), and a same-name stale file from a crash is replaced at the next spawn (names embed pid+random, so a collision is always our own residue).

*Env knobs:* `SQUEEZEFS_IPC=1` (arm), `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_ARENA_MB` (per-session payload arena, explicit MiB wins verbatim; default derived = `max(64 MiB, DMA-aligned admission-cap ÷ max(128, cpus × 8))` — the population-derived fraction, 2026-08-04: the divisor is the session population the cap is sized to hold (`cpus × 8` = the matched-inflight client-fleet slope; the retired fixed /128 admitted only half of a 256-process matched-inflight fleet on a 32-CPU/176 GiB-budget client — `ipc_admission_refusals`, engagement-INVALID rows), 64 MiB is the shipped floor and boxes with `cpus × 8 < 128` keep the old cap/128 arithmetic exactly), `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_MAX_OP_BYTES` (default 1 MiB), `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_MEM_PCT` (session-shm admission cap as a percent of the resolved memory budget, clamp (0,100] — the preferred spelling), `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_MEM_MAX` (MiB; absolute session-shm admission cap, explicit-wins-verbatim — compat spelling), default with neither set = 12.5 % of the budget (`budget/8`, no fixed ceiling; precedence absolute > percentage > derived), `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_ALLOW_DEV=1` (counted dev-build skew override), `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_SERVICE_THREADS` (service-thread ceiling; default = the drain-LANE derivation `clamp(3×cpus/8, 2, 64)` — the counted 2026-08-06 field width sweep, `.benchmarks/2026-08-06-dd-width-slope.md`: one lane = svc submitter + direct-drive reaper = 2 OS threads, sized so the lane-pair population takes ¾ of the core budget; it dominates the shim's `clamp(cpus/4, 2, 16)` session default at every machine size, so every default session still owns a drain thread and spawn-on-bind never parks a spare — threads spawn on session admission), `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_DD_SHARDS` (direct-drive uring shards — one ring + pinned reaper per shard, one lane per service thread; default = the SAME drain-lane derivation — the pair moves together: a shard set wider than the ceiling is production-dark — override/measurement lever clamp 1..=64), `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_DD_INLINE_REAP` (reaper/drain fusion, default ON — the owning service thread drains its lane's direct-drive CQ inline, zero syscall; `0` = the A/B lever, and kernels without `IORING_ENTER_EXT_ARG` auto-disarm it loudly; engagement gauge `.stats` `ipc_direct_inline_reaps`; shim-iops campaign 2026-08-07), `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_DD_EAGER_FLUSH` (mid-sweep direct-drive enter once a lane's unflushed SQE count reaches K; **absent = the `CadenceCore`-governed threshold** — probed downward from the measured sweep claim size and adopted only on live delivery response, gauges `ipc_dd_cadence_{k,probe_ups,probe_backoffs}`; an explicit K > 0 pins it (measurement lever, `.benchmarks/2026-08-07-shim-iops.md` §2.1); `0` = end-of-sweep only with the governor ignored, the pre-governor M3 posture and the A/B control), `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_DD_LANE_FLUSH` (default ON — a service thread's sweep-end flush enters only its OWN direct-drive lane's ring; the r3 drain-funnel fix, `.benchmarks/2026-08-08-shim-drain-funnel-r3.md`: the flush-ALL sweep serialized every svc thread on every shard's kernel uring_lock — 31 % of svc cycles spinning, the field's ring-ingress pool; `0` = the pre-r3 flush-all A/B lever; dd rings also build with `IORING_SETUP_COOP_TASKRUN` where the kernel offers it, degrading loudly pre-5.19), `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_IDLE_SECS` (idle-session reap, default 300, 0 = off; `.stats` `ipc_sessions_reaped`), `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_INVAL_WINDOW_MS` (W1 invalidation rate window, default 1000), `SQUEEZEFS_IPC_SOCKET_DIR` (path-socket runtime dir, `none` = abstract-only — see the container-netns entry above). Client side: `SQUEEZEFS_IL_OP_TIMEOUT_MS` (per-op ring deadline; timeout poisons the session → passthrough), `SQUEEZEFS_IL_REAP_QUANTUM_US` (the libaio reap's deep-regime quantum, default the shipped 50 µs, clamp 1..=1000 — since the reap-fanin campaign, 2026-08-08, it is the batch-threshold doorbell park's AGE BOUND rather than a blind sleep: the k-th completion cuts the wait short (k = `clamp(pending/4, 2, pending)` derived per session, never a knob) and the daemon pays ≤ 1 wake per k completions; worst case identical to the retired sleep — `.benchmarks/2026-08-08-shim-reap-fanin.md`). The daemon-side dequeue exports **`ipc_ingress_ns`** (always-on histogram): the MEASURED client-publish→daemon-dequeue ring-ingress residence via the v5 slot ingress stamp — an il row is INVALID unless its count delta accounts for the row's ring ops.

*Data-plane observability:* `ipc_ops_{read,write}` / `ipc_bytes_{in,out}` are the **engagement instrument** — an interception benchmark row is only valid if their deltas account for the row's ops (silent passthrough measuring kernel FUSE is the failure mode the check exists for); `ipc_fast_path_serves` vs `ipc_async_handoffs` + the `ipc_fast_path_{lock,miss}_demotions` split are the fast-path health signal.

### Multi-user mounts — the single-tenant resource posture

**Stated posture (VAL-7d, pre-RC engineering spec §3): one SqueezeFS mount is a SINGLE resource tenant. There is no per-uid quota, no per-uid rate limit, and no per-uid accounting anywhere in the FUSE data path — by design, not by omission.** An operator sharing one mount between untrusted workloads must not expect resource isolation from SqueezeFS; use one mount per tenant, or a cgroup/container boundary around each workload, and give each its own volume set.

What this means concretely:

- **Every R5 memory-budget component is process-global.** The gauges (`mem_budget_components{…}`) aggregate the whole daemon: staged payloads, parked extents, transport payload arenas, IPC session arenas and severed buffers, write-pipeline in-flight bytes, job copy buffers, read-lane holds. One workload that drives the budget to **Red** therefore pauses maintenance jobs, tier publishes and dehydration *for every user of that mount*. That is the designed Red semantics (`docs/design-read-path.md` §5.7 — converge by completion, never OOM); it is not partitioned by uid, and the pause is fleet-visible in `mem_budget_{level,red_events}`, `job_paused_mem_pressure` and `read_tier_publishes_paused`.
- **Nothing rate-limits a uid's op stream.** The bounds that exist are *global* and structural — the transport's `queues × q_depth` in-flight ceiling, the write-pipeline BDP admission target (`write_pipeline_admission_waits` is the honest-backpressure gauge), the bounded writeback queue, the reclaim queue — so a heavy workload's backpressure is felt by everyone on the mount. Fairness between uids is the kernel's I/O scheduler and cgroup v2's job, not the daemon's.
- **What IS per-uid.** Exactly two things, both on the interception control plane, both admission caps rather than accounting: `per_uid_session_cap` (concurrent IPC sessions per uid — derived, `clamp(session-shm admission cap ÷ per-session arena, 64, 4096)`, i.e. the session population the budget can actually hold; `mem_budget::ipc_per_uid_session_cap`, no knob of its own — the budget knobs are the levers) and the `ipc_session_arenas` R5 component's shed = refuse-new-sessions behavior. Neither bounds the FUSE path.
- **Access control is separate and IS enforced.** `-o default_permissions` puts POSIX mode/ACL checks in the kernel, POSIX advisory locks are kernel-arbitrated per mount (see the DLM section in [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md)), the reserved-xattr screen keeps daemon records invisible through FUSE (`fuse_reserved_xattr_refusals`), the `.stats`/`.config` inodes are `0400` owned by the mount uid with their key census opt-in (`SQUEEZEFS_STATS_KEY_CENSUS=1`), staging/read-cache trees are `0700`/`0600`, and the ADMIN lane admits only uid 0 or `-o admin_uid=N`. **Confidentiality and integrity are multi-user-safe; resource consumption is not partitioned.**
- **Why this is the honest answer for the design target.** The 15,000+-node target (ruling D1) scales by NODES: each node runs its own daemon over its own mount, so cluster multi-tenancy is a *scheduler* property (one mount per training job / per container), and per-uid accounting inside a single daemon would buy nothing for it while adding a per-op charge to the hot path. If a future deployment genuinely needs several untrusted uids sharing one daemon's budget, that is a new program — per-uid R5 sub-budgets plus a per-uid admission gate — and it must arrive with its own measured cost, not as a claim made here.

### Fleet-share sizing (co-located daemon fleets)

**Every resource derivation in the tree reads the WHOLE machine** — the R5
memory budget, transport payload arenas, drain-lane ceilings, conveyor
batches, blocking pools. That is correct at one daemon per host and wrong by
N× the moment N daemons share a machine (same-machine multi-mount clients
are a product surface — each mount is its own client identity,
`docs/design-full-multi-writer.md` §5). The sizing law (KD-MW-14):

| Knob | Default | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `SQUEEZEFS_FLEET_SHARE` | `1` (= today's whole-machine posture) | Integer ≥ 1: a divisor applied **once, at the root inputs of the derivation tree** — the effective memory budget and effective CPU count become `ceil(system / share)`, and every downstream derived value scales through the existing formulas untouched. Set it to the number of co-located daemons, per daemon (the fleet rig exports it automatically; it is never auto-detected — daemons discovering each other to divide a machine would be a coordination plane where a knob suffices) |

The rules an operator must know:

- **It modifies the derived tier only.** Explicit absolute knobs still win
  verbatim; percentage knobs apply to the shared budget
  (`absolute > percentage > derived`, unchanged).
- **Floors are never divided.** Physical minima and never-regress-below-
  shipped floors hold per daemon regardless of the share; a share that
  cannot satisfy its floors **refuses loudly at startup, naming the
  arithmetic** — never a silent clamp below a floor.
- **Kernel-mandated geometry is exempt** (and pinned by a tie test): the
  FUSE-over-io_uring queue COUNT is one queue per kernel possible CPU per
  mount — a session with fewer never becomes ready — so N daemons always
  hold N × possible-CPUs queues. The *memory* behind those queues DOES
  scale: per-queue depth degrades to fit the divided payload-buffer cap.
- **The cgroup-unreclaimable pressure arm divides through the same root**:
  in a shared cage the arm reads the CAGE's residue, so without the divisor
  all N quiet daemons read each other's anon as their own pressure — the
  proven failure was all 32 daemons in Red with tier publishes paused
  fleet-wide (`.benchmarks/2026-08-16-mw-s6-arm.md`, finding #4). Per-process
  RSS stays undivided, so each daemon's own balloon is still policed at
  full strength.

Proven at N=32 on one 32-CPU box (the S6-a row: R5-pressure columns flat
fleet-wide — `mem_budget_red_events` 0, `hard_backstops` 0,
`parked_gate_timeouts` 0 on all 32 daemons). Tie tests:
`tests/derivation_sweep_tests.rs` (share=4 quarters every divisible derived
cap; the exemption list is pinned to exactly the kernel-mandated set).

### Cache/staging paths (`squeezefs config`)

Changing cache/staging directories is an admin op, guarded like `format` (refused while any client has the volume mounted); it rewrites the format config and wipes the new directories so the next mount stamps a fresh staging generation.

```bash
squeezefs config set-cache-paths sqmeta://<meta_dev> <path> [<path>...]
squeezefs config get-cache-paths sqmeta://<meta_dev>
```

### Transparent compression & encryption

Optional per-volume transforms declared at format: `--compression lz4|zstd` and `--encrypt-algo aes256gcm|chacha20`, applied across all three write layouts. Compression is **best-effort per block**: an incompressible block is stored raw (frame-flagged, counted as `compress_stored_raw` in `.stats`) instead of expanding — and transformed volumes reserve per-chunk headroom at format so worst-case images always fit (see [Breaking changes](#breaking-changes--migration-notes)).

**The encryption key never touches the volume it protects, and never rides `argv`** (design: `docs/design-key-handling.md`). Generate one, then keep it somewhere the daemon can read and nobody else can:

```bash
head -c 32 /dev/urandom | base64 > /etc/squeezefs/keys/volume.key
chmod 600 /etc/squeezefs/keys/volume.key

squeezefs format sqmeta://<meta> sqdata://<data> \
  --encrypt-algo aes256gcm --encrypt-key /etc/squeezefs/keys/volume.key
```

- The file must hold **at least 32 bytes of key material** (not a passphrase — the derivation has no work factor), be a regular file, mode `0600`, owned by the invoking user. It is opened `O_NOFOLLOW` and checked on the fd; anything else refuses loudly. `--encrypt-key -` reads the material from **stdin** instead.
- `format` prints a **key id** and persists only that plus a derivation salt in the volume's format config. Losing the key file loses the data: nothing on the volume can reconstruct it.
- **Mounting** resolves the key, in order, from `mount --encrypt-key <path>`, `SQUEEZEFS_ENCRYPT_KEY_FILE=<path>`, then `/etc/squeezefs/keys/<key_id>.key` — so a key file parked at the default path needs no flag at all. A missing key refuses the mount naming all three sources; a *wrong* key refuses naming the expected and observed key ids (never a mount that fails every read).
- Data keys are wrapped with AES-256-GCM/ChaCha20-Poly1305 under an HKDF-SHA-256-derived key-encryption key (the volume's own record cipher). A process holding key material is set undumpable with core dumps disabled.


### Read-path tuning (mount env; design `docs/design-read-path.md`)

Defaults are the measured sweet spot — override only with a live-counter reason (the `.stats` inode exposes every family):

- `SQUEEZEFS_READ_TIER_ADMISSION` (`second-touch` default | `always` | `never`): NVMe read-tier admission for >256 KiB fills. `second-touch` kills the streaming publish tax (a cold 16 GiB pass writes ~0 instead of ~16.9 GiB to the tier) while re-read heat still converges to the tier; `always` restores unconditional first-touch publishes (A/B escape hatch).
- `SQUEEZEFS_READ_HOT_BLOCK_CACHE_MB`: RAM hot-block tier budget for >256 KiB blocks (default derived from the read-mem cache; `0` disables the tier and admission auto-degrades to `always`).
- `SQUEEZEFS_READ_PREFETCH_WINDOW` (default **derived**, `0` disables): per-stream prefetch pipeline depth cap in blocks. Unset, the cap is the prefetch share of the hot-tier budget in blocks — `clamp(SHARE_PCT × hot_budget / 100 / block_size, 4, 4096)` (`routing::derived_prefetch_window_cap`; the fixed 16 was retired by the 2026-07-29 read-saturation campaign's no-constants rule) — and an explicit value wins verbatim. The window is adaptive (2→cap, AIMD) and contention-scaled; the cap is a ceiling, not a target.
- `SQUEEZEFS_READ_PREFETCH_SHARE_PCT` (default `50`): the prefetch pipeline's share of the hot-tier budget in the contention-scaling formula — lower it if concurrent stream count routinely exceeds hot-tier capacity.
- `SQUEEZEFS_READ_RANGED_THRESHOLD` (default `262144`, `0` disables): reads at or under this size on passthrough (uncompressed/unencrypted) volumes fetch only their 4 KiB-aligned device window instead of the whole block — the rand-4k amplification kill (≈1000× → ~1.0×). Compressed/encrypted volumes always fetch whole blocks (decode requirement).
- `SQUEEZEFS_READ_DEST_LEASE` (default on, `0` = the A/B control): the READ **dest-window lease** (copy-elimination phase 1, 2026-08-06). Cold, 4 KiB-aligned, sub-block kernel reads on passthrough volumes DMA device bytes **straight into the reply's registered payload window** — the per-byte serve copy (1.00 CPU passes/byte, ~1/2.7 of the read CPU budget on a CPU-walled client) is deleted, and the speculative fill machinery stands down for such traffic (a pooled ahead fill for a window the demand read DMAs itself is a double-fetch). Trade stated honestly: lease-served bytes are device-true — re-read-heavy cold loops pay device fetches instead of tier warmth (fitting warm sets still serve from tiers other traffic populated). `0` restores the pre-campaign fill+serve-copy shape byte-identically. Engagement: `read_dest_lease_bytes` in `.stats` (subset of `read_dest_dma_bytes`).

### Hybrid I/O for O_DIRECT reads (default) and the device-true escape

**Hybrid I/O (default, user directive 2026-07-15):** O_DIRECT reads get the best of both worlds — they keep bypassing the *kernel page cache* (the kernel's side of O_DIRECT, unchanged) while serving from and admitting into *SqueezeFS's own read tiers* exactly like buffered reads. Tier hits serve from RAM (binding-validated — the ~536–558 k IOPS class on tier-resident data, `.benchmarks/2026-07-15-hybrid-io.md` + the RW5 close §3b); misses use **evidence-based admission** — first touch of a block reads the device (device-true, nothing admitted: streaming/scan pollution protection), a **second touch within the ghost window** admits the block (one whole-block fetch → RAM hot tier + NVMe read tier), so re-read-heavy O_DIRECT workloads (rand-4k databases, repeated scans) converge to RAM speed after one warm-up pass. Admission pauses under memory-budget Red. Watch `read_odirect_tier_serves` / `ranged_read_ghost_escalations` in `.stats`.

- **`-o direct_device_true`** (mount option) / **`SQUEEZEFS_DIRECT_DEVICE_TRUE=1`** (daemon env): the **measurement/diagnostic escape** — O_DIRECT reads become strictly device-true (no tier serve, no admission, no ghost recording, no prefetch classification; every O_DIRECT read is a validated device read of exactly its aligned window). This is the posture for device-path benchmarking and the `.benchmarks` amplification methodology (`squeezefs bench --direct` prints which posture the mount carries by sniffing `.stats`). Buffered traffic on the same mount keeps full hybrid behavior. Mode visible as `"direct_device_true"` in `.stats`; adoption counted by `read_device_true_reads`.
- `--mem-budget <size>` (mount flag) / `SQUEEZEFS_MEM_BUDGET_MB`: the daemon's joint memory budget. Unset, the budget follows cgroup v2 `memory.max` × 0.8 (re-read every second — a runtime-lowered cage tightens the budget live), else 70 % of RAM. Under pressure the daemon sheds (early flushes, cache clamps, prefetch pause) instead of OOMing; watch `mem_budget_level`/`mem_budget_red_events` in `.stats`.
- `SQUEEZEFS_TRANSPORT_MEM_MAX` (MiB, absolute) / `SQUEEZEFS_TRANSPORT_MEM_PCT` (percent of the budget): the FUSE-over-io_uring payload-arena cap. Default with neither set = budget/8, **no fixed ceiling** (derivation sweep 2026-08-04 — the retired 2 GiB ceiling degraded ring depth on > 64-CPU big-RAM boxes; `SQUEEZEFS_TRANSPORT_MEM_MAX=2048` restores it verbatim as the A0 lever). Precedence absolute > percentage > derived.
- `SQUEEZEFS_PARKED_BUFFERS` (count, absolute): the parked-write buffer budget (buffers' worth of block size). Explicit wins verbatim; default derived = `max(256, budget/16 ÷ block_size)` (derivation sweep 2026-08-04 — 256 is the shipped never-regress floor; the R5 authority gauges and sheds these bytes, and Red halves the effective cap).
- `SQUEEZEFS_URING_FS_WORKERS` (count, clamp 1..=64): the io_uring file-worker pool for ad-hoc local file I/O. Default derived = `clamp(cpus/4, 4, 64)` of process parallelism (derivation sweep 2026-08-04 — the retired `clamp(nproc, 4, 8)` pinned every ≥ 8-CPU box at 8 with no basis).

### Random-small-write path (sole-owner patch + extent overlay; design `docs/design-random-small-writes.md`)

Small random overwrites of striped files no longer pay a whole-block read-modify-write. Two levers, both default-on (program Implemented 2026-07; closing evidence `.benchmarks/2026-07-17-rand-write-program-closing.md`):

- **Sole-owner extent patch (W1)**: an isolated, LBA-aligned, non-extending small write to an exclusively-owned, passthrough, whole-block-mapped striped block becomes **one in-place sub-block DMA** — zero reads, zero metadata commits, zero staging (354–397 → 61–67 k IOPS on the 4 KiB random-write shape; device amplification ~1× writes). Sequential streams are predicate-excluded (adjacency guard) and keep the whole-block write-through economy.
- **Extent overlay + batched fold (W2)**: patch-ineligible shapes (compressed/encrypted volumes, refcount-shared blocks post-clone, holes, unaligned) park 4 KiB-class extents instead of 4 MiB buffers, spill as checksummed staging *extent records* (never a seed read at spill), and fold into blocks lazily — compressed-volume rand-write amplification ~2,500× → 15–26×.
- **Torn-extent durability note (the v1 aligned-only contract)**: a patch rewrites **only device sectors wholly inside the application's own write range** — bytes the application never wrote are never rewritten, so a crash can never perturb foreign data. The residual exposure is a per-sector old/new mix *strictly inside an un-fsynced in-flight write* (POSIX-legal; fsync acks only after DMA completion — the write ACK on this shape is *stronger* than before, since data reaches the device before ACK instead of a parked buffer).
- **The device overlay's length floor (finding 47, 2026-09-02)**: the same cap is the device overlay's (`docs/design-overlay-overwrite.md` §5.1) minimum segment length — a sub-cap aligned write is this program's whatever its block's state (hole, clone-shared, decorated, co-writer-refused, stream-adjacent), never an overlay store that would mint a whole CoW dest per touched block and read + re-seed the whole old image at settle. `overlay_ineligible_sub_cap` counts the floor engaging (≈ per sub-cap aligned write on overlay-armed mounts by design); `overlay_gap_seed_old_bytes` growing on a rand-4k row while it stays flat is predicate rot.
- **The WRITE handler's per-op economy (W-6, 2026-09-08, `.benchmarks/2026-09-08-w6-write-handler-economy.md`)**: the W1 patch write went 14.6 → 3.06 allocations per op (the three left are the device layer's: the DMA payload's `Bytes::from_owner` box, the uring worker's completion oneshot and its per-request record) and its twenty shared-line per-op RMWs (five latency histograms, the queue-depth sample, four counters) record into per-thread stripes — every stats key keeps its meaning (a read folds the stripes). The budget is pinned at 4/op by `tests/kernel_op_economy_tests.rs`; `SQZ_ALLOC_TRACE=1` names the sites.
- **Ranged gap seed (W-6)**: the device overlay's settle sources a partial overwrite's uncovered ranges by ranged reads of exactly the gap's bytes instead of the whole old block image — `SQUEEZEFS_GAP_SEED_RANGED` (default on; `0` = the whole read, A/B), engagement `overlay_gap_seed_ranged_bytes`, amplification face `overlay_gap_seed_read_bytes` (device bytes read per seed; `read ÷ old ≈ 1` engaged, = block window ÷ Σ gaps on the control). Decorated bindings and transformed volumes keep the whole read by construction.
- Knobs (acceptance/diagnostic, not operational escape hatches): `SQUEEZEFS_PATCH_MAX_BYTES` (default derived = block_size/8 — 512 KiB on the shipped 4 MiB block; explicit wins verbatim, `0` disables the patch path — A/B lever — and, by the same act, empties the overlay's sub-cap class so every aligned length is overlay-eligible), `SQUEEZEFS_FOLD_MAX_EXTENTS` / `SQUEEZEFS_FOLD_MAX_BYTES` (fold triggers, default 64 / block_size/4 = 1 MiB on the 4 MiB block).
- Watch in `.stats`: `patch_writes` ≈ ops on the patch shape (`patch_ineligible_*` growing there = predicate rot), `patch_edge_rmw_reads` **must stay 0**, `fold_fill` median ≥ 16, `extent_records_{recovered,torn_discarded,future_refused}` on recovery.

### Large files — the block-map tree

A striped file's block map lives inline in its metadata record until the encoded map outgrows the record-value cap — **roughly 6–8 GiB of file at the default 4 MiB block size** (~60 KiB of map). Since 2026-09-01 (`docs/design-kvmap-block-map-tree.md`) a file that crosses that point moves its map into a dedicated metadata tree, **one record per block (with run records for contiguous stretches)**, riding the same journal, checkpoint and node cache as every other metadata record. Nothing is configured: the crossing happens on the publish that outgrows the inline cap, once per file, and announces itself in the mount log (`kvmap crossing: ino N entered the block-map tree …` — `grep kvmap <log>`). Below the crossing the on-disk bytes are pinned identical to before.

- **Ceiling.** Block indices are 32-bit, so a file may hold at most `2³² − 1` blocks: **`(2³² − 1) × block_size` = 16 PiB − one block at 4 MiB**, `4 PiB` at 1 MiB. The practical bound is metadata capacity — on the order of 8 GiB of map records plus 13 GiB of block-reference records per PiB of file at 4 MiB blocks, and run records collapse the map side by the run length for sequential data. (The previous scheme, a single indirect blob, capped a file at ≈ `block_size² / 30` ≈ 545 GiB at 4 MiB; a volume still carrying such a legacy blob converts it on the file's first publish under a current binary.)
- **RAM.** A tree-mapped file's map is read on demand through the node cache, not rehydrated whole. Files whose *write* map would exceed the derived share (`kvmap_write_map_budget_bytes` = memory budget ÷ 16) take the **partial store** — a dirty overlay plus bounded warm windows with tree read-through — gauged as `kvmap_partial_inos`; `kvmap_write_map_bytes` is the live write-map footprint, `block_map_window_{bytes,hits,fills,evictions}` the read-window tier (an R5 component with floor 0).
- **Truncate and unlink of a tree-mapped file are O(1) at the call**: the size (or the unlink) commits immediately together with a durable per-file sweep cursor, and the record deletes, block-reference releases and device reclaims run as a **background job** in chunks — resumable from the cursor across crashes and remounts, regenerated at mount for every cursor found. `squeezefs job list <mountpoint>` shows them as `kvmap_sweep` jobs; the ledger is `map_sweep_{jobs,chunks,records,resumed}`. Reads clamp to the new size, so shadowed records are unreadable the moment the truncate returns.
- **Crossing ledger.** `map_migrate_{inos,records,resumed}` count files that crossed, records written and crash-resumed crossings; `publish_map_record_bytes` is the per-publish journal cost on tree-mapped files, and `publish_indirect_blob_bytes` / `layout_indirect_map_reads` must trend to 0 on a converted volume. The per-volume tree counters are `meta_kv_block_map_{puts,deletes,run_puts,lookup_exact,lookup_range,lookup_floor,range_records}`.
- **fsck class C11 — map-plane consistency (report-only).** Orphan map records (a crossing that crashed mid-train, a dead inode), heads with no records, and run-vs-point coverage anomalies are detected and reported, never auto-repaired: `fsck_map_orphan_records` **must stay 0**, alongside `fsck_map_empty_heads` and `fsck_map_run_foreign_shadows`; `fsck_crossing_exempted` counts files whose crossing was in flight during the pass (an incomplete pass records no verdict). A crashed crossing's residue is invisible to reads and is deleted by the next crossing's sweep prologue.
- **Format face.** The first crossing on a volume stamps incompat bit 16 (`KV_BLOCK_MAP_TREE`) — never on untouched volumes; binaries older than 2026-09-01 refuse such a volume loudly ([Breaking changes](#breaking-changes--migration-notes)).
- **Knobs** (measurement levers, not operational settings): `SQUEEZEFS_KVMAP` (default on; `0` = new crossings take the legacy blob arm — never disables resolution of an existing tree head), `SQUEEZEFS_KVMAP_OVERLAY` (default on; `0` = whole map in RAM at any size), `SQUEEZEFS_MAP_MIGRATE_CHUNK` (crossing ops per transaction, default 512, range 64–1024).

### Symmetric metadata program — the slot-tree forest (PR 1, DARK)

The symmetric shared-disk metadata program (`docs/design-symmetric-metadata.md`, Rev 7 — every mount an equal metadata authority) lands in rungs, every one **dark** until the PR-14 flip. PR 1 (2026-09-13, `.benchmarks/2026-09-13-sym-forest.md`) is the on-disk **forest**: under incompat **bit 17** (`KV_SYMMETRIC_FOREST`) a metadata volume holds ONE mixed-kind KV tree per routing slot instead of one tree per record kind, plus a control tree ("tree 0", id 8) whose `slot_state` records name every non-native slot tree's root; the fixed ledger names tree 0 and the native slot tree. Every content key carries its kind at offset 8 (inode 9 B / dentry 17 B / xattr 17 B / block-map 13 B; block references under the `0x06` prefix, 29 B), so a file's inode, its `layout` and its block map are adjacent in one leaf, and a record's slot is read from its key.

- **What changes for an operator today: nothing.** No format stamps bit 17 (`format --symmetric` and the offline conversion verb are PR 11's); a volume without the bit mounts exactly as before — the shipped trees, the shipped codecs, the shipped code path. The only way a volume carries bit 17 is the test seam `SQUEEZEFS_TEST_STAMP_SYMMETRIC=1` at format, which the forest suites use and nothing else may. A binary older than 2026-09-13 refuses a stamped volume loudly at the superblock gate ([Breaking changes](#breaking-changes--migration-notes)).
- **What the forest does on a stamped volume.** Slot trees are minted lazily — a slot with no records owns no extent; every guest slot tree's root is published into tree 0 by the checkpoint task as one checkpoint-class journal entry before the flush pass, with the images the entry names barriered first. A guest root that has MOVED and is not yet published is a **floor**: the cycle's replay tail never passes the journal position of the mint or root swap until tree 0 names the new root, so a publication the ring's checkpoint reserve defers (a routine event under ring pressure) leaves every record under that root inside the replay window — the same law the flush pass's skipped SMOs obey — and a publication whose entry fails to write is the journal-failure class (fail-stop), never a warning. Mount replays tree 0 first, opens every guest tree it names, then replays the window's slot-tree records by their keys' slots (a slot tree's interior pointer records carry their slot on the journal key — the rightmost separator is the tree maximum every slot tree shares). Block references live in the referencing ino's slot tree, so a block's population is the union over EVERY slot tree: the refs probes walk each tree with its own cursor (`block_ref_count` / the mount-time ledger seed / the C8 oracle are exact on a forest). A `-o ro` reader of a forest skips the ledger's per-id root for the guest trees (every slot tree carries header id 0 — the native root is not theirs) and re-reads tree 0 at each epoch step, adopting guests minted after its mount; **a mount that may not write mints nothing at replay either** (a reader, a co-writer, a probe): the window records of a slot tree 0 does not yet name are skipped (`meta_kv_forest_reader_window_skips`) and served at the poll after the writer publishes, while the WRITE mount's replay mints such a tree in the recovery allocation class, so a volume that crashed at `heap_full` with an unpublished mint still mounts. **Such a mount's view is partial BY SLOT and consistent**: it serves the slot trees tree 0 named at its last poll and nothing of the others, and because a dentry lives in its parent's slot while naming a child in the child's, it lists a child in `readdir` iff it holds the child's slot tree — the condition its `lookup` answers — so `readdir` never lists a name `lookup` refuses (`meta_kv_forest_reader_unpublished_children` counts the names withheld); every record of a not-yet-published slot appears together at the poll that names it, inside the published `reader_staleness_bound_ms`. A block reference under a key the codec refuses is REFUSED by every by-block consumer (`block_ref_count`, the ledger seed, the C8 oracle) — the flat layout's law, never a population answered short; only the kind-routed record walks skip. fsck's C1 on a forest is ONE raw walk per slot tree: a record whose key the codec refuses is a record-level finding (`C1RawKey`), repaired in place like any schema violation. The §4.10 digest walk runs over `(kind, legacy key, value)`, so a forest volume and a flat volume holding the same records digest EQUAL — the forest is a relayout, never a rewrite.
- **The parametrized gate.** `tests/run_sym_forest_suites.sh` (= `task check:sym-forest`) runs every pre-forest KV suite twice — flat, then under the seam — and fails on the first red leg: the PR-1 row's "every existing KV contract green on the forest" has an instrument, and the first stamped run of it is what found the round-1 defects (a forest volume could not publish a striped layout in the gate tier; the refs probes saw the native slot only; a deferred publication left no floor). Every suite runs under a per-suite watchdog whose bound DERIVES from the timed table — `max(600 s, 4 × the suite's flat wall)`; `SQZ_SYM_HANG_FACTOR` / `SQZ_SYM_HANG_FLOOR_S` override the terms — and a suite that reaches it is killed (the whole process tree) and reported `=== HUNG: <leg> <suite> … (the last test line: …) ===`, failing the leg loud instead of stalling the matrix for as long as the hung future lives (PR 10 review round 3, Issue 30).
- **Gauges** (`.stats`): `meta_kv_forest_slot_trees_minted` (guest slot trees minted this mount — 0 for the life of every un-stamped mount), `meta_kv_forest_root_publishes` (`slot_state` publications; ≤ checkpoints × slot trees), `meta_kv_forest_key_violations` (**must stay 0** — a record reaching a forest volume's commit or replay path under a key the §5.2.1 codec refuses).
- **Owed by the later rungs** (the PR plan): appender pages and per-appender rings (PR 2 — landed 2026-09-13, [the appender region](#symmetric-metadata-program--the-appender-region-pr-2-dark)), the manager lease (PR 3), slot leases and the `apply_locked` lease gate (PR 4), tokens (PR 5), the fsck ONE-walk census over the mixed leaves for C2–C10 (C1 already walks a forest's slot trees once, raw; the other classes' kind-routed walkers still read them once per kind), `format --symmetric` (PR 11), and the squeeze-test solo re-gate A-B-B-A (the note's §7). The lazy mint still runs one device write on the conveyor pass task (once per slot per volume, before any node lock); design §5.3.3 moves the extent to the appender's grant in PR 3.

### Symmetric metadata program — the appender region (PR 2, DARK)

PR 2 (2026-09-13, `.benchmarks/2026-09-13-sym-pr2-appender-region.md`) lands the **appender region** on top of PR 1's forest, dark behind the same bit 17 and the same seam. Under bit 17 every metadata writer is an **appender**: it owns a 4 KiB **page** — its directory entry AND its ledger record (identity, term, state, ring segments, journal tail, checkpoint seq, extent grant, the slots it leases with their roots) — and a journal ring that is a **segment table** of heap extents. Appender 0's page slots are the fixed journal extent's first four pages and its ring the rest; appenders ≥ 1 live in the **appender directory**, a chain of heap extents `superblock.appender_dir` names (each holds `(node_size/4096 − 1)/2` page pairs and a checksummed header page with `next`). Pages are read newest-valid-wins over four slots (the directory pair + two ring-side pages in the ring's first segment), so a torn newest page falls back twice — under the **directory-first law**: a page naming a ring table the directory pair does not yet fully name (the join onto a fresh ring, a growth, the leave) is written to every such directory slot in one barriered step, and only a page whose table both directory images already name rides the four-slot rotation, so the directory pair alone always finds the region's current ring and a torn table-changing page falls back to the other slot naming the SAME table.

- **What changes for an operator today: nothing.** A bit-17-absent volume has no directory, no pages, no region — sector 0 is byte-identical to the shipped image (the `appender_dir` field lives in its zero padding and is refused at the decoder without the bit), the ring is the whole journal extent as before, and every Appender gauge reads 0. `squeezefs appenders` refuses such a volume naming the seam and PR 11.
- **What a writer does on a stamped volume.** At open it reads the directory and binds its KD-MW-2 identity `(node_token, mount_slot)`: a `Live` page of its OWN NODE means its predecessor died un-recovered and the ring replay just performed IS the recovery (`appender_self_recoveries`) — "own" is judged by the node token alone, because the writer holds the D0 flock at this point and a same-host holder at ANY mount point is therefore dead (a crashed mount remounted at another mount point is the ordinary same-host recovery shape; the `(node, slot)` narrowing arrives with PR 10's death ledger); a `Live` page under a FOREIGN node — or a `Recovering` page of any identity (a recoverer mid-replay; nothing writes the state before PR 10) — REFUSES the writer's JOIN loud, naming the dead-appender recovery driver (PR 10, which owns the operator remedy — no verb for it exists in this binary) — never a silent mount over another node's ring. The refusal fires at the join, AFTER the D0 claim gate has classified the volume's holder: a live foreign writer on a shared non-PR LUN gets D0's own "another process holds the writer claim" refusal, and the non-joining Writer opens — `squeezefs claim clear`, the guarded offline verbs — are never blocked by a page they do not write (a probe or `-o ro` reader lists the page and mounts what tree 0 names). It JOINS after the claim gate — its page goes `Live` with a bumped term in one barriered checkpoint — and every checkpoint thereafter writes the page (one 4 KiB write per region per cycle): tail and checkpoint seq mirroring the fixed ledger, the roots of the slots it holds — never tree 0's root or the membership stamp, which stay the fixed ledger's (KD-SYM-3). A clean unmount first runs the final checkpoint to a fixpoint over EVERY ring — ring 0 and each declared region's (`rings_uncovered`) — so a leased slot tree's SMO inside the final flush pass (its records in the region's ring, its moved root published to tree 0 by the next cycle) is covered before any page is written (before PR 3's round 3 the fixpoint read ring 0 alone and such a leave lost acked records); then it releases the region in the order pages → barrier → release → bitmap → barrier: the `Free` page (id and term kept — ids are stable for the volume's life) lands in both directory slots, and only once no durable page names a declared region's ring extents are their bits cleared and written before the process exits; a crash between the two barriers leaves them claimed — the C13 orphan-extent class, never a loss. A region whose ring is still UNCOVERED when the leave runs (the fixpoint bound missed) is NOT released: its page stays `Live` with its roots and tail, its ring and grant stay claimed, the unmount logs it loud, and the next mount of the same node recovers it as own residue (`appender_self_recoveries`) — never a `Free` page over an uncovered window.
- **Two appenders on one volume (the declared partition — a seam).** The manager's lease machinery is PR 3's and the slot-lease gate PR 4's, so in PR 2 a second region exists only under `SQUEEZEFS_TEST_SYM_APPENDER_SLOTS`: the declared region gets a page from the directory and a ring from the heap (sized by `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_RING_KB` or the derivation; refused past 8 segments), every transaction journals into the ring of the region that leases its records' slot (a transaction spanning two appenders is PR 6's cross-owner operation and refuses), each region has its OWN checkpoint tail computed from its own ring and its own leaves, its OWN tombstone-elision tail (a record's seq is a position in ITS ring, so a leased slot's leaves elide below their lessee's durable tail and ring 0's leaves below the ledger's — never a MIN across incomparable position spaces), the §4.4 pt 4 compensation of a FAILED window write journals into the window's own ring (content of that region — in the manager's ring it would be the `Lease` violation the next mount refuses on), and a stalled, DRAINED ring grows by one segment per checkpoint (`appender_ring_grows`; growth declines while a pass is inside the region's window OR a stage-B window of it is still in flight — a failed window's compensation reserves on the ring the window holds, so the ring is never swapped under one; growth needs the drained instant, so an appender that saturates its ring without pause stays at its size and every drain rides one cadence tick — `admissible bytes ÷ cadence` per region is the bound, `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_RING_KB` the operator's lever until PR 3's drain-then-grow arm). Structure — interior records, allocator deltas — stays in the manager's ring in PR 2: an appender's own alloc records need PR 3's extent grant to be its own. Mount recovers the writer's OWN `Live` regions (each ring replayed from its page's segments and tail) and refuses a foreign one; the three per-ring partition classes (`Key`, `Lease`, `Extent`) are evaluated over every window before any content is applied and refuse loud on their must-stay-0 gauges.
- **The flush ceiling (KD-SYM-10).** Every dirty leaf of every slot tree a region holds is flushed and barriered within the checkpoint LANDING ceiling of the cadence in force — `CHECKPOINT_MAX_AGE_MS` (1,000 ms, the cadence TRIGGER the tick fires AT) plus the two tick-granularity terms the reader's qualify term already derives: 1,100 ms at the shipped 50 ms flush, published live as `appender_flush_ceiling_ms` — of the record that dirtied it: the audit stamps a slot-tree leaf at its clean → dirty transition and, at each flush pass's covering barrier, counts a region whose OLDEST dirty leaf is older than that ceiling on `appender_flush_ceiling_overruns` (**must stay 0**: a healthy mount under a steady write stream at the default cadence never moves it, nor does a parked cadence — whose landing ceiling is the parked tick's; a parked DEVICE is the legitimate way, and the contract suite does both).
- **The cadence tick reads every region's ring.** §4.6 pt 2's pressure trigger and its "anything to cover" gate read ring 0 AND every declared region's ring: a committer parked at a full region ring (parked at admission, before any node lock — nothing dirty, ring 0 idle) makes the next tick's cycle due with its barrier immediate (`appender_pressure_cycles`), and a region ring with un-reclaimed entries keeps the tick cycling until its tail lands (a cycle later than its flush, like the ledger's). Ring 0 keeps the shipped law verbatim.
- **Gauges** (`.stats`, per volume): the Appender family and the three replay tripwires — see [Observability](#observability). **Verb**: `squeezefs appenders <sqmeta-uri> [--json]`.
- **Owed by the later rungs**: the manager's `JoinAppender` (PR 3 — landed 2026-09-14, [the manager lease](#symmetric-metadata-program--the-manager-lease-pr-3-dark)), extent grants (PR 3 — landed: a declared region's structure journals into ITS ring), slot leases and the `apply_locked` gate (PR 4 — the page's `g` and `slot_tree_extents` are written 0), recovery of a FOREIGN dead appender's ring and the `Recovering` / `Recovered` states (PR 10 — the operator remedy verb with it; until then a stamped volume whose foreign appender died has NO writer-side remedy: `claim clear` clears the D0 claim but the page stays Live and the join refuses); a globally monotone record-seq space (PR 4 — today a slot's records come from exactly one ring per mount, and the fold's per-key LWW compares raw ring positions, which are not comparable across rings once a slot's records move rings at a lease handover); `format --symmetric` / `enable-symmetric` (PR 11).

### Symmetric metadata program — the manager lease (PR 3, DARK)

PR 3 (2026-09-14, `.benchmarks/2026-09-14-sym-pr3-manager.md`) lands the **manager lease** on top of PR 2's appender region, dark behind the same bit 17 and the same seam. Under bit 17 the writer that wins the D0 ladder on a volume IS that volume's **manager** (KD-SYM-3 — an ordinary appender with the `is_manager` bit; the fixed ledger is role-bound to it): it carves **extent grants** for every other appender (`extent_grant:{appender}` in tree 0, the unclaimed remainder on the appender's page), takes them back in `ReturnExtents` batches at the checkpoint cadence, answers `JoinAppender` over the S8 owner service, and — on a PR substrate — holds the metadata namespace under **Write Exclusive – Registrants Only** with every other appender a registrant. With a grant, a declared region's structure — its interior flips, its allocator deltas — journals into **its own ring** (PR 2's "structure stays the manager's ring" is reversed; PR 2's `Lease` / `Extent` replay classes are the tripwires that prove it).

- **What changes for an operator today: two things, both shipped-bug fixes.** (1) **The Reservation Report is read SIZED BY REGCTL** on every namespace class (design §5.8.1, KD-SYM-18): the shipped fixed 4 KiB buffer silently truncated the report at the 63rd extended registrant, so a fleet wider than that read a short registrant set at every guard decision; the read is now two steps (a header, then `header + 64 × REGCTL`, re-read once if the count grew between them) and `pr_registrants_per_namespace` / `pr_report_bytes` publish what it saw. (2) **A replayed in-window `free` of a LIVE tree root is DROPPED at mount** (`meta_kv_replay_root_frees_dropped`, one WARN per drop) — the root-swap carve-out's hole, found by PR 3's C13 crash window on the FLAT leg: a root swap journals no pointer record, so a kill between the swap (a D4 compaction nudge, a merge sweep's root collapse, a threshold-maintenance root swap) and the next ledger record left the mount replaying through the predecessor — live again — while the swap's `free(old)` parked and the bring-up cover's tail then released the live root's extent to the next claim. The allocator load now defers every replayed park until the trees are open and drops the frees of mounted roots; the swap's successor image stays claimed-and-unrouted (the bounded flat leak the design states; fsck C13's class on a forest grant). Everything else is unchanged for a bit-17-absent volume: no grant, no manager, no verb; `manager_lease` reads `vacant` and every Manager gauge 0.
- **KD-SYM-13 — the plane refuses to arm without PR.** A declared partition or a join on a substrate whose metadata namespace advertises no Persistent Reservations (a file-backed or loop volume) is REFUSED at open naming `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_ALLOW_NON_PR=1` — the LOUD opt-in for lab use, announced at every mount that uses it, under which fencing is detection-grade only (design §5.8.2 names the acked-loss class a zombie can reach). A solo forest mount (no partition, no join) arms nothing, needs no opt-in and keeps the shipped rtype-1 reservation posture verbatim.
- **WERO on the metadata namespace (§5.8.1).** With the plane armed on a PR substrate, `SQUEEZEFS_META_PR_WERO=1` (the default) makes the manager's hold **rtype 3** — `meta_pr_wero` = 1, the `data_custody` standing hold one namespace class over — and every joining appender a **registrant** under it (`join_wero_as_registrant`), so a dead appender is fenced by preempting exactly its registration; `=0` keeps the shipped rtype 1 (detection-grade for the other appenders) and is refused on a PR-capable substrate without the opt-in. One configured identity per mount (KD-MW-2/3, `SQUEEZEFS_HOSTNQN`/`SQUEEZEFS_HOSTID` pair-or-neither); no fencing groups, no derived identities, no target-ACL probing.
- **The array registrant cap (KD-SYM-18/23).** nvmet — THE target since R-SYM-8 — has no per-namespace registrant cap, so `pr_registrant_cap_refusals` is 0 there by construction. A third-party PR array whose registration table is vendor-bounded is handled without probing it: the cap is DECLARED with `SQUEEZEFS_PR_REGISTRANT_CAP` or LEARNED from the first REGISTER the device refuses with a non-conflict status (`pr_registrant_cap` publishes the cap in force, 0 = unbounded), and a join at or past it refuses LOUD naming the count, the namespace and the remedy (fewer hosts on that namespace, or a target without the cap). The stats face is per namespace: `pr_registrants_per_namespace` and `pr_report_bytes` keyed by device path.
- **Extent grants (§5.3.3).** The manager carves a grant of `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_GRANT_EXTENTS` extents (derived `clamp(2 × ewma_smo_rate × manager_failover_bound_s, 8, free_heap / (4 × appenders))` — the images an appender needs to keep compacting THROUGH a manager failover) from its own bitmap: the alloc deltas and the merged `extent_grant:{appender}` record ride ONE control entry in ring 0, the page names the unclaimed remainder, and the appender claims INSIDE the grant — its `alloc(extent)` in its own ring per image, a `free` gated on its own tail per retirement (`PartitionViolation::Extent` = a delta outside any grant of its own). Refill is proactive at 50 % consumption (USER class, the derived size) and reactive when a flush pass finds the grant empty with the manager reachable (INTERNAL class — the compaction reserve, drawn for exactly ONE SMO's images, `SMO_IMAGES_MAX` = 4, never the derived grant); an appender that exhausts its grant while the manager is unreachable DEFERS its compactions (the records stay in its ring — never lost, never `ENOSPC`) and counts `manager_dependency_stalls` (**must stay 0 at the sized grant**; the bound it is judged against is `manager_dependency_stall_bound_ms` = `manager_failover_bound_ms`), while a user commit that needs the extent refuses EAGAIN-class. `ReturnExtents` clears the returned bits and rewrites the record in one control entry, idempotent against tree 0 (`already` — counted after the frame's runs are coalesced, plus what a concurrent return already took, so `cleared + already ≡ named`); an in-process region's extent that holds a live image (claimed) or whose free is still parked on the region's tail (pending — returnable only once the tail passes it) is REFUSED (`manager_verb_refusals`); the frame's runs are coalesced before the record intersection, so the manager's work is bounded by the frame's own length plus the record, never their product. The closure law `extent_grant_extents ≡ claimed + returned + granted_unclaimed` is published as its four terms. A clean unmount returns the unclaimed remainder before its pages go `Free`.
- **The verbs (§5.3.5, §6.3).** `ManagerCall::{JoinAppender, ExtentGrant, ReturnExtents}` ride the S8 owner service as one verb family (`CLUSTER_WIRE_SCHEMA` 4 → 5 — a same-commit-fleet change, KD-7); every verb is idempotent against DURABLE state — a `JoinAppender` replayed against this manager OR a successor answers `Joined{already}` off the joiner's `Live` page with the page's UNCLAIMED remainder as its grant (a join the manager died inside — page Live, no grant minted — is COMPLETED by the replay, which mints the grant the interrupted join owed), an `ExtentGrant` whose caller's unclaimed remainder already covers the ask answers that remainder VERBATIM (a replayed frame after a lost reply carves nothing; the 50 % refill law reaches a fresh carve because a remainder below half the reference is below the derived size), a return of extents the record no longer grants answers `already` — counted on `manager_verb_replays`; `manager_verb_refusals` (**must stay 0**) counts the durable witness contradicting the caller (appender 0 asked for a grant; a join past `appenders_capacity`, which refuses naming the metadata volume COUNT as the lever; a return naming a LIVE image); `manager_verb_rejected` counts wire-INVALID frames — every wire-carried integer is validated against durable state before anything proportional to it is allocated (a return run must lie inside the volume, `start + len` must not overflow; runs are intersected with the caller's record as intervals; an explicit `want` is clamped to the derivation's cap) — the buggy/hostile-peer class, kept off the must-stay-0 gauge. `JoinAppender` reuses the lowest `Free` directory page or grows the chain by one extent (header written LAST, then the previous header's `next`, each barriered), carves a ring of ≤ 8 coalesced extents — ZEROED before any page names them, and continuing the page's position space past its predecessor incarnation's final head and ring 0's head (a fresh ring restarting at 0 gave this incarnation's records seqs at or below the predecessor's durable ones, and the predecessor's entries still on the device replayed as this ring's: an acked release came back after a crash) — writes the page `Live` into both directory slots (the directory-first law) and mints the initial grant. The manager's control entries admit in the USER class of ring 0: a verb storm competes with user commits for the admissible window and never eats the checkpoint task's reserve. `manager_service_ns` is exact-sum `admit / execute / reply / total`; `manager_load_pct` is MEASURED Σ service ÷ wall; `manager_verbs_per_s` the rate.
- **The successor (§5.9).** The manager role is elected by the D0 ladder, so its failover bound is the ladder's: `manager_failover_bound_ms` = `CLIENT_STALE_TTL_SECS` + the ladder's measured wall + the replay's measured wall, DERIVED and published — the term the grant headroom is sized against, so foreign-homed appenders keep committing on their grant through the window. A successor that wins the ladder over a dead manager's `Live` page 0 adopts that page's newer roots at replay (the ring replay it just performed IS the dead manager's ring — KD-SYM-3's "replay the dead manager's ring BEFORE its first control write") and takes the role; `manager_lease` reads `held` there, `peer:<node token>` on a probe of a volume another node manages, `vacant` on a flat volume. `manager_vol0_unreachable` counts the §5.5.2 releases (a manager that cannot read volume 0's ledger for longer than `T_owner` releases the role — the decision is one function; PR 8/10 wire the roles).
- **fsck C13 — orphan image extent (§5.8.5).** An extent an appender's grant holds CLAIMED that no slot-tree root reaches and no pending-free names: the §5.3.4 unpublished-root-swap window's successor image, or a grant remainder the page could not name. Detected per volume under the volume's SMO + mint serialization (so a live in-window image — an SMO's successor before its route flip, a lazy mint's root before the forest names it — is never a finding), confirmed by one fresh census in the re-check pass, and repaired by `return-orphan-image-extent`: the appender frees the orphan in ITS OWN ring (gated on its tail) and the cadence's `ReturnExtents` clears the bit and rewrites the grant record — nothing is quarantined, since nothing routes to the image. `fsck_repair_classC13` counts the applied returns; 0 on every flat volume.
- **Gauges** (`.stats`, per volume): the Manager and Fencing families — see [Observability](#observability). **Fidelity legs**: `pr-registrants` (≥ 64 registrants REPORTED on a real nvmet namespace — the shipped truncation repro-ported) and `sym-manager-failover` in `tests/run_nvmeof_fidelity.sh`.
- **Owed by the later rungs**: slot leases + `g` + `slot_tree_extents` (PR 4), the appender's persisted commit-rate EWMA as the ring-size input (PR 3's drain-then-grow arm stays owed), foreign-ring recovery and the `Recovering` / `Recovered` states with the operator remedy verb (PR 10 — region 0's page must be consulted BEFORE the fixed-ring replay for §5.8.3 to hold there), the wiring of the `ManagerService` onto the mount path's listener (the service and client exist and are contract-proven on their own listener; PR 4's lease gate is the first mount-path caller). `format --symmetric` / `enable-symmetric` landed in PR 11 (next section).

### Converting an existing set to the symmetric forest (`squeezefs volume enable-symmetric`)

PR 11 (2026-09-14, `.benchmarks/2026-09-14-sym-pr11-convert.md`) lands the two PRODUCT arms that stamp incompat bit 17 — `format --symmetric` ([Format](#format-squeezefs-format)) for a fresh set and the OFFLINE conversion verb for an existing one — beside the test seam. **The program stays DARK**: a plain `format` never stamps the bit, a mount never converts anything, and a volume nobody converts is byte-identical to yesterday's; bit 17 becomes the default (and presence-required) only at the program's PR-14 flip. Convert only a set you intend to run under the symmetric program; there is no downgrade verb (forward-only — `format --force` reformats).

```bash
squeezefs volume enable-symmetric sqmeta://<dev0>,<dev1>,...            # OFFLINE: convert EVERY volume of the set
squeezefs volume enable-symmetric sqmeta://<dev0>,<dev1>,... --dry-run  # print the plan (incl. extents needed vs available) + every refusal; write nothing
squeezefs volume enable-symmetric sqmeta://<dev0>,<dev1>,... --resume   # continue a crashed run from its crash point
squeezefs volume enable-symmetric sqmeta://<dev0>,<dev1>,... --abort    # undo a crashed run on every volume still flat under its marker
```

**Procedure.**

1. **Preflight.** Unmount EVERY mount of the set (writers, `-o ro` readers, co-writers — the verb refuses a heartbeat-fresh client, but an S5 `-o ro` reader registers nothing and is invisible to it: a flat reader left mounted across the flip does NOT serve stale data — once the old trees' extents are freed and reused its cache misses land on recycled extents and its reads **FAIL LOUD** (checksum / seq refusals) until it is remounted). Run `--dry-run` first: it prints, per volume, the record count and bytes that would move, the slot trees, **the heap extents the build claims against the extents the volume can supply** (the capacity preflight — a shortfall shows here as a plan, and as a refusal on the real run), the orphans its census would reclaim, what would be **DROPPED** (below), and every refusal — without writing a byte (pinned: sector 0, the ledger, the ring and the bitmap are byte-identical after a dry run).
2. **The refusals — every one runs BEFORE the first marker, and the marker is the verb's first write** (a refusal this binary can make never strands a set under its markers): the set is **already symmetric** (every volume carries bit 17 and no marker — nothing to do); a **concurrent invocation** or a mount that began after the live-client gate holds the volume's **writer lock** (the verb takes the D0 `flock` on every volume for its duration, released only around its own guarded opens — one invocation converts a set at a time); a volume's newest ledger record carries a **bit-8 NON-SOLO partition suffix** (its last writer was a multi-appender era whose slot arithmetic is not a solo mount's — mount the set solo once, unmount cleanly, re-run; pinned: the solo mount re-checkpoints the ledger in solo form); a volume **not cleanly unmounted** (journal entries past its checkpoint tail — mount the set once and unmount cleanly, re-run); an **open cross-volume intent** on any volume (a half-applied `rename`/`link` — mount once so it rolls forward at open, then re-run); an **in-flight `job:` record** (a paused/queued/running maintenance job would resume onto a layout it did not plan for — let it finish or `squeezefs job cancel`); a **live client**; the **capacity preflight** — the forest is written into FRESH extents while the shared trees stay claimed until the end, so the build's peak claim is `Σ planned nodes (the tree writer's own chunking, one extent each) + the directory extent` against `free + reclaimable orphans − the §4.7 compaction floor`, computed per volume from the write-free inspection; a volume that cannot hold both refuses naming the volume, the extents needed and available, and the remedy (free space — delete files or `squeezefs defrag --meta` — move slots off the volume with `volume migrate-meta-slot`, or rebuild the set on larger metadata volumes; a smaller `--meta-node-kib` lowers the per-slot floor); a **marker without `--resume` or `--abort`** (a crashed run must be acknowledged); `--resume` / `--abort` with nothing to resume / abort.
3. **The run.** The verb writes a `sym_upgrade:` marker on ino 1 of EVERY volume first, then converts the volumes one at a time in canonical member order. From the moment a volume carries its marker, **every writable mount of the set refuses** at the D0 gate — before it takes a claim, so a refused mount writes nothing — naming this verb, `--resume` and `--abort`; read-only mounts and probes keep serving the trees the volume's layout bit names (the old ones until the flip, the forest after). Per volume the durable steps are: quiesce under the D0 ladder (a guarded open, a checkpoint, a clean shutdown — so a live foreign writer is refused by the ladder, never converted under) → read every live record through a probe → reclaim every claimed extent no ledger root reaches, flooring the new nodes' seqs above every stamp those extents' residue carries → write the forest into FRESH extents (one mixed-kind tree per routing slot — an EMPTY one for a hosted slot whose stamp carries a cursor but whose inos were all deleted, so tree 0 is the cursor's durable home — the control tree naming every guest root `Unleased` with its cursor, the appender directory, the fixed ring reset with appender 0's page) → ONE ledger write naming the old roots AND the forest roots → ONE sector write stamping bit 17 + the directory (the layout flips onto a ledger that already describes it) → the old trees' extents returned and folded out of the ledger → the marker removed through the forest's own first mount. Progress prints per volume: records, bytes, slot trees, records/s, MB/s, extents written (and planned / claimable) / freed / reclaimed.
4. **A crash.** Whatever the instant, the set refuses writers (the marker is present on every unconverted volume and, copied into the forest, on a converted-but-unfinished one) and readers keep serving. `--resume` re-runs the verb with the SAME URI: each volume is resumed from its DURABLE state — a flat volume under its marker is rebuilt (a crashed build's fresh extents are reclaimed first, so nothing leaks — pinned: the resumed volume has exactly the free extents of a straight conversion); a stamped volume whose ledger still names the old trees frees them; a stamped volume with only the marker left drops it. `--abort` undoes the run instead, on every volume still FLAT under its marker: the flat trees are verified (every live record of every kind reads — the user kinds, the block references, the block map), the aborted build's extents are reclaimed, one ledger record restates the flat roots with the reclaimed bitmap generation and a node-seq watermark raised above every stamp the reclaimed extents' residue carries (the same law the resume's build obeys — a later flat SMO minted into those extents never shares a seq with a dead frame there), and the marker is removed — the volume is the flat volume it was before the verb (pinned: the pre-verb digest, every claimed extent reachable, writer-mountable). Two refusals, each for the WHOLE set, undoing nothing and naming `--resume` as the only exit: a volume already past its flip (stamped, marker present — the forest is its truth and its old trees are unreferenced or already freed), and a volume whose **redundant superblock copy already carries bit 17 while sector 0 is flat** — the run was killed between the stamp's two sector writes (the copy lands first); the volume IS flat, but an abort would leave the stale stamped copy on the device for a later sector-0 failure to fall back onto, while `--resume` rewrites both copies. The five seams (after the markers, mid-build, after the hybrid ledger, after the stamp, after the free) are each pinned in `tests/sym_convert_tests.rs`, resume and abort alike.
5. **Afterwards.** Mount the set: every volume is a forest (`.stats` `meta_kv_forest_*`, `appender_*` families populate; the old `meta_format_version` stays 3 — the forest is a relayout of the same records: its **post-fold digest over the user kinds (inodes, dentries, xattrs) equals the source's**, and the block-reference and block-map record SETS are equal, both pinned). Run `squeezefs fsck` once; it is clean on a converted set (pinned incl. C8 drift 0 and C13 empty). The `.stats` `appenders_known` reads 1 until PR 4's leases put other appenders on the volume.

**What is DROPPED** (design §7.3 — reported by the verb, decodable, never written under bit 17): a `volume set-owners` assignment (ownership is a lease under the forest, D19 reversed — the claim-set record stays as it was); a SOLO bit-8 partition suffix on the ledger record (the forest's ledger is the manager's, un-suffixed). `volume set-owners` itself stays available until PR 12 retires it; running it on a converted set is meaningless.

**Two things the operator should know.** (1) The conversion holds one volume's live records in RAM while it re-lays them (one volume at a time, ≈ the volume's live record bytes — a 200 K-record volume is ≈ 60 MB); the streaming form is owed (`.benchmarks/2026-09-14-sym-pr11-convert.md` §7). (2) The verb's pre-build census reclaims every claimed extent no ledger root reaches, and prints the count as `orphans reclaimed`. On a volume last unmounted by a binary OLDER than PR 11 that count is NOT zero: every clean unmount of a flat volume used to leak the images its final checkpoint cycle retired (**the PR-11 finding, fixed in the same landing** — the coverage barrier released pending frees after the cycle had written its bitmap pages, and the shutdown fixpoint converged on ring coverage alone, so the release never landed; 6 extents on a 60-file volume, 43 on a 50 K-file one, one more per idle mount cycle, cumulative). A volume this binary has unmounted cleanly reports 0 — the census is also the honest way to see how much an older binary leaked on your volumes before you convert them.

### Symmetric metadata program — slot leases (PR 4, DARK)

PR 4 (2026-09-14, `.benchmarks/2026-09-14-sym-pr4-slot-leases.md`) lands **slot leases** on top of PR 3's manager, dark behind bit 17 AND a knob: **`SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`** on a bit-17 volume arms the plane; **`=0` — the shipped posture until the PR-14 flip — is the PR 1–3 forest EXACTLY** (every slot the mount's, the shared mint rotor, `dlm_mode` = `solo`, no plane, tree 0's `Unleased { g: 0 }` roots), and `=1` on a bit-17-ABSENT volume refuses the writer's open loud naming `squeezefs volume enable-symmetric` (PR 11). Under the plane a **slot is the ownership unit of metadata** (design §5.1.1): the writer that wins the D0 ladder is the volume's manager and LEASES its native slot plus `M` rotor slots through the manager's own `AcquireSlots` (`M = clamp(W / (2 × writers_known), 1, 64)` — 64 on a solo mount; `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_MINT_SLOTS` overrides); every lease is a tree-0 record `Leased { appender_id, g, page_addr, root, cursor, slot_tree_extents }` and a `Live` entry `(slot, g, slot_tree_extents, root, cursor)` on the lessee's appender page — **two durable homes at every instant**, `g` the slot's lease generation, moved ONCE at every grant and never at a release or a replay.

- **What an operator sees on an armed mount.** `dlm_mode` reads **`slot-homed`** (the S4 lock plane's table is installed from the live lease map — every routing slot is local except one a FOREIGN appender leases, whose token server is that holder; on a solo mount nothing is foreign, so `dlm_rpcs` stays 0 by construction while the mode names the plane); `slot_leases_held` = native + rotor + every slot acquired since; `slot_rotor` = `M` in force; `symmetric_meta` = 1.
