New inodes mint by the **bounded parent-slot affinity** (KD-SYM-11/16): a child lands in its parent's slot while that tree is under `A_max(t) = max(used_leaf_bytes / 64, node_size)` (`affinity_a_max_bytes`, soft — a mint is never refused; `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_AFFINITY_MAX_MB` makes it static), else in the rotor slot with the most headroom (`affinity_ceiling_spills`); with every rotor tree STRICTLY over the cap the manager grants one more rotor slot up to `2 × M` (`affinity_ceiling_overflows`), past that the smallest rotor tree takes the mint. The routed create's top-level shape no longer decides the tree layout: a solo mount's load spreads over the 64 rotor trees whatever it is (gate 1's divisibility law).
- **The partition law (§5.4.1) has two faces — the DOOR and the belt.** A commit naming a slot another appender leases refuses **`EAGAIN`** at the door naming the holder (the typed `SlotBusy` — "ship to the holder", the metanode ship itself is PR 6/12's; `slot_door_refusals`); a commit naming a slot MID-HANDOVER **parks** at the door until the handover completes and is re-read (`slot_door_parks` — a bounded wait, never an error: for the cadence's own release the slot is unleased again and the commit re-acquires it first-touch and lands; for a wire requester's accept it is the requester's and refuses `EAGAIN`); a commit naming a slot nobody leases acquires it first-touch (its control entry admitted PARKING like the user commit it is). Every commit holds a door TOKEN per slot to its terminal outcome, and a release drains the slot's tokens after raising `Releasing` and before its flush (a `SeqCst`-fenced Dekker pair, loom-modelled) — so a leaf mutation reaching `apply_locked` past the door is an invariant tripwire: **`meta_kv_leaf_lease_refusals`, must stay 0** (the writer twin of `node_partition_refusals`; the gate's verdict is read UNDER the node's write lock, loom-pinned). Before review round 2 the door passed a `Releasing` slot and every commit on the departing holder during a handover failed `EINVAL` through the belt.
- **Handover = flush-then-transfer (KD-SYM-4, §5.1.4), the one sequence every release runs**: `Releasing` on the gate → the door drained → checkpoint cycles until the departing region's durable tail passes the slot's RECORD FRONTIER and the root is published (a post-condition, bounded at 64 cycles and loud on a stuck tail — the departing ring's window is clear of the slot — one ring per key, judged by PR 2's `Lease` detector at the next open) → the page with the slot `Releasing { root, cursor, g }` + barrier → `ReleaseSlot` (tree 0 `Unleased { root, cursor, g, extents, last_written, tails }`, one control entry) → the page without the slot; the requester's `AcquireSlot` then grants at `g + 1` with the tree's live root. `slot_handover_phase_ns` records `flush / page / tree0 / grant / total` exact-sum; `slot_handovers` counts them. Handovers are serialized per volume (the cadence's releases and an accepted offer recall the same slots). **The mint cursor travels with the lease as a FLOOR** (§5.1.8): a slot released with its top inos deleted and re-acquired never re-mints one of them, in-process or across a remount. **A slot tree's live image extents move with it**: they leave the departing appender's `extent_grant` record with the release and join the requester's with the grant (bits untouched — the images are live), so fsck C13's claimed-unreachable census never names an image its new lessee later retires.
- **Who moves a slot (§5.1.4 — dominance over ONE common window).** The HOLDER decides, at its served ships: it offers a slot to ONE requester iff, over the same `T_idle` window (`SQUEEZEFS_SYM_T_IDLE_MS`, derived `T_owner` = the 45 s membership TTL), that requester's ships ≥ 2 × the holder's own ops AND ≥ `N_floor` (`slot_offer_n_floor` = `max(2, ceil(handover cost / ship cost))`, the handover's own price in ships; cold start = four barriers + three ship RTTs). The consequences are the design's: a live holder is never recalled by a touch; a paused live job keeps its tree (the window still counts its ops); a single touch never moves an idle tree; twelve thousand single-shot creators never move a directory slot (aggregate shipping below `N_floor` each never triggers); ONE dominating requester gets the offer and the crowd does not; a bursty job reclaims its tree from a trickle holder. An offer stands one renewal beat (`min(10 s, T_idle/3)`; `slot_offers`, `_idle` / `_dominated` / `_expired` — closure `offers ≡ handovers + expired + standing`). After a handover the requester-side **cooldown** (the S10 valve, 8 windows over `T_idle`) serves the alternating touches of a pair that keeps swapping a directory instead of re-offering — the pair converges on one holder.
- **Hold and release (§5.1.3).** A page names at most `SLOT_PAGE_BUDGET` = 108 leased slots (35 B per entry — the design's 122 predates `slot_tree_extents`); a region past it releases idle NON-rotor slots least-recently-written first at the cadence (`slot_lru_releases`); when the membership census grows so the derived `M` falls, idle rotor slots beyond it are released (`slot_forced_shrinks`); a declared region whose last slot went and whose ring is drained goes `Free` (`slot_region_releases` — the leave's law for one region). The cadence runs on its OWN task after each checkpoint tick (single-flight — a release waits on admitted commits, and a commit parked at ring admission needs the tick). **A region past the page budget with none of its slots idle** (a burst of first-touch acquires) has roots its page cannot name: they ride tree 0's `Leased` records instead (the page-budget overflow law — before it such a burst pinned the ledger tail and the clause-b audit fail-stopped the volume). **On a JOINED appender the same law rides the wire** (PR 12b round 4): its checkpoint ships the roots its page cannot hold as `PublishRoots` (`slot_roots_shipped`), the manager rewrites the `Leased` records under one durable entry (`slot_roots_published_for_lessees`), and the floors lift at the reply — before it a joiner that first-touched 64 `Unleased` slots inside one `T_idle` (an `rm -rf` of a dead peer's tree) had 20 roots with no durable home, its ring filled behind their floors and D1.b fail-stopped the volume. A clean unmount writes each region's page with every held slot `Releasing` first, then releases every lease to `Unleased` with its final words and tails — moving the released trees' image extents out of the region's grant record in the same control entry — and this volume's contribution to the S4 table is withdrawn (`solo` again once the last armed volume leaves).
- **Crash rows (§5.3.4 rows 5–8) and C14.** A holder dying after its page named `Releasing` and before tree 0 was written has the release completed FROM THE PAGE at its next open (tree 0 `Unleased` at the page's `g`, root and cursor); a holder dying after tree 0's ack and before its page dropped the slot has the stale `Releasing` entry DROPPED — **tree 0 wins**, never a conflict; a requester whose accepted offer died with the manager retries `AcquireSlot` against the successor and is granted (idempotent — the release landed, the grant had not); a requester dying after its page named a slot keeps it (tree 0 `Leased { requester }`; its own re-join answers `already` at the same `g`). **Two `Live` pages attesting ONE slot AT tree 0's `g` refuse the mount loud** naming both appenders and the class (`slot_lease_conflicts`, C14's live face; the remedy is PR 10's `appender clear`); a `Releasing` entry never counts as an attestation, and a `Live` entry BELOW tree 0's `g` for the slot is stale residue (a leave or handover the page never recorded — `g` is strictly monotone per slot and tree 0 is the witness), dropped at the arm and counted `slot_lease_stale_entries`, never a conflict.
- **Reads resolve through tree 0, wire-free.** `SlotHolderCache` (`src/slot_holder_cache.rs`) projects tree 0's lessee map into an `ArcSwap` (rebuilt at the arm and a reader's poll; one entry learnt per grant, forgotten per release); the ship target / token server of a slot is one load. `slot_resolve_rpcs` counts the `ResolveSlot` verbs this manager SERVED — the wire's stale-view fallback (0 on a solo mount; the manager's own resolution never issues one — pinned). The `NotHolder { current }` redirect lands with the ship path that takes it (PR 6/12).
- **The manager's legal answers ride their own gauges** (`manager_verb_refusals` counts witness contradictions ALONE, must-stay-0): `slot_acquire_refusals` = explicit asks for a slot another appender holds (the "ship to the holder" reply), `slot_rotor_cap_refusals` = rotor asks past `2 × M` (the overflow arm's designed past-cap answer; the cap counts ROTOR grants, never every non-native lease), `slot_offers_busy` = a second dominating ship before an accept, `slot_grant_deferrals` = grants of an unleased slot deferred because ring 0's window still held the tree's records after the bounded clearing cycles (a schedule — the requester retries `EAGAIN`; 0 on a healthy cadence).
- **The membership carriage (§5.9).** Every renewal `Grant` carries `slot_leases_ack { prior_epoch, slots: [(slot, g)] }` (the manager's attestation of the member's leases — a slot lease lives as long as the membership lease), `slot_release_notices` (slots the manager recalls from the member: a requester's accepted offer of a slot a WIRE holder holds rides the holder's renewal since a member serves no push channel — `slot_recall_notices`; the holder runs flush-then-transfer + `ReleaseSlot`, the requester retries) and `offered_slots` (offers standing for the member). A member no page names, and every mount without an armed plane, gets an empty carriage.
- **The venue.** The manager verbs (`AcquireSlots`, `AcquireSlot`, `OfferSlot`, `ReleaseSlot`, `ResolveSlot` beside PR 3's three; `MANAGER_SCHEMA` 1 under `CLUSTER_WIRE_SCHEMA` 5 — the program's unreleased wire; the request frame now names its VOLUME ordinal) ride the S8 owner listener's router when the multi-writer arm builds it and any volume of the set armed the plane (`ManagerSetService` dispatches by ordinal); a symmetric mount without the multi-writer arm serves no wire appender; since PR 12 the join ladder stands the listener up on every armed writer, and N daemon processes on one volume is PR 12b's many-writer posture (design row 12b).
- **Gauges** (`.stats`, per volume): the Slot-lease family — see [Observability](#observability); `symmetric_meta` 0, `slot_*` 0, `dlm_mode` `solo` on every unarmed or bit-17-absent mount, pinned.
- **Owed by the later rungs**: tokens over foreign metadata (PR 5 — until then a non-writer's view is PR 1's partial-by-slot projection), the metanode ship for a foreign slot's mutation (PR 6/12), the death ledger and `Recovering` / `Recovered` (PR 10 — the `Live` page of a dead appender is left to it), N daemon processes on one volume and the appender-side dial of the manager (PR 12b — design row 12b, the many-writer posture), `format --symmetric` / `enable-symmetric` (PR 11). **Landed in review round 2**: the record-seq space is monotone across rings — a slot's `seq_floor` (the departing ring's stamp frontier) travels with the lease in tree 0 and on the wire, the receiving ring stamps above it, the offset is durable on the appender page (`seq_offset`); a ring nothing was handed to stamps positions verbatim, so the flat and unarmed paths are byte-identical.
- **Landed in review round 3 — the two seq domains, the tails record, the refill's size.** Every overlay address is a RECORD-SEQ span (`SeqSpan` — a batch member's, a failed window's) and every §4.7 pending-free gate a ring POSITION (`Reservation::record_gate`): on a ring that received a handed-over slot the two differ by `seq_offset`, and before it the two rollback arms removed nothing while a retired extent stayed parked `seq_offset` bytes past its entry (on a quiet ring for ever); the slot's record frontier is RESET to the receiving ring's head at the grant, so a release from the quieter ring clears its window in a cycle instead of storming 64. **The recorded tails ride their own tree-0 record, `slot_tails:{s}`** (written by the release in the same entry as the fixed-size `Unleased` record, left alone by the grant — PR 5's rule 2 reads generation `g`'s tails while the slot is leased at `g + 1`): INLINE while they fit the volume's KV value cap (≈ 5,400 leaves at 256 KiB nodes, 1,386 at 64 KiB), **SPILLED to heap extents the record names above it** — the images written and barriered before the record, the previous release's spill freed by the superseding entry — and a release never pages the tree into the node cache (a resident leaf answers off RAM, an evicted one costs one extent read that is scanned and dropped: the tail is the append walk's result, no header carries it). A tree-0 value past the cap is refused LOUD at the control entry, never discovered at the flush pass; the clean leave's tree-0 batch is chunked by the entry cap (`journal::pack_entries`, each chunk beside its own grant rewrite). A first-touch acquire admits its control entry BEFORE the manager's verb mutex (a park under it deadlocked with the checkpoint task's grant verbs). The appender page carries a LAYOUT version (byte 55 = 1; the PR 2/3 layout refuses, never misreads); a `Free` page names the offset in force; the re-adopted rotor counts against the `2 × M` cap after a crash-remount. Found by the round's large-tree fixtures on PR 3/4 surfaces: the departing holder's own flush (an SMO's moves) passed the `Releasing` gate through `apply_locked_structural` — every tree with pending splits at its handover failed the release before; and a leased leaf's split wider than the one-SMO constant is refilled to ITS need (`GrantExhausted { needed }`, the INTERNAL class unclamped by the steady-state cap) — the constant ask was re-answered verbatim for ever and every commit parked at the region's full ring escalated through D1.b.
- **Landed in review round 4 — the manager maintains the unleased trees; a foreign layout refuses the open.** The SMO task's own moves read the STRUCTURAL verdict: a slot mid-handover is the departing holder's flush, an UNLEASED slot's tree is the MANAGER's to maintain (KD-SYM-2/3 — its root lives in tree 0, its SMOs journal in ring 0), and only a slot another appender leases refuses — before it a released tree with an underfull pair made the must-stay-0 belt count, the merge sweep abort its lap every cadence and the heap-full recovery never pass it; the sweep / heap-full recovery / D4 arm / census walk ONE filtered population and skip a foreign-leased tree on `merge_sweep_foreign_skips`. An appender page of another LAYOUT with a valid checksum is `ForeignLayout`, refused by the four-slot reader and the OPEN (`reformat required`) — never read as an absent page (a torn page still falls back to its predecessor). A release of a COLD slot tree reads every non-resident leaf's extent to learn its tail (≈ 65 k reads at the operating point — the representation's floor until the owed header tail hint); a warm tree, every handover's shape, reads nothing.
- **Landed in review round 5 — a grant to another appender waits for the manager's structural pass on the tree.** A wire appender's grant of an UNLEASED slot took effect in RAM (the `foreign` bit the sweep's filter reads) only AFTER its durable write, so a grant racing the manager's merge sweep on that tree either landed the manager's SMO on a slot tree 0 had just leased elsewhere (its records in ring 0 — the `meta_kv_replay_lease_violations` refusal at the next open; a root published nowhere) or made the must-stay-0 `meta_kv_leaf_lease_refusals` count on a legal schedule. The grant now takes the volume's SMO mutex before the verb mutex (the checkpoint task's own order): no manager SMO on the slot is in flight while the lease moves, ring 0's window is cycled clear of the slot's records before the lease is published (bounded, loud on a stuck tail — a `Corrupt` naming the slots and the tail is a defect report, never a longer wait), and the slot reads as another appender's from the acquire on, rolled back if the write fails. A wire grant may therefore wait out one in-flight structural pass (one cadence tick's work budget at most); nothing else changes for the operator. A release also raises ring 0's record-seq offset above the departing ring's floor — the manager's own maintenance of a released tree stamps above the lessee's records from then on.
- **Landed in review round 6 — every slot word a wire `ReleaseSlot` carries is screened before any effect; the clearing bound is a deferral.** A wire peer's release presents `seq_floor`, `cursor`, `root` and `slot_tree_extents`, and round 5 made the first act on ring 0's seq space immediately: one bad frame (`seq_floor` near `u64::MAX`) would have saturated every later stamp and made the next replay skip every window record. The manager now validates each word against DURABLE / derived state before anything moves — `seq_floor` strictly above the grant's floor and at most the frontier the appender's own page can justify (`seq_offset` + `head_hint` + two ring lengths, capped at half the seq space), `cursor` at least the grant's and inside the slot's namespace, `slot_tree_extents` inside the volume, `root` the grant's or a node inside the appender's extent grant whose header carries the word's stamp (one 4 KiB read) — and REJECTS a frame outside them (`STATUS_REJECTED`, `manager_verb_rejected`, nothing written; `g` stays the durable-witness check's `STATUS_REFUSED`). The frame's `appender_id` (every slot verb) must name a `Live` directory page and never one of this mount's own regions; an offer's `to` must name an appender the manager knows and not the holder. The service answers `STATUS_REJECTED` for every verb's rejection (before round 6 only `ReturnExtents` did). A grant whose window-clearing reaches its bound with the tail still moving is DEFERRED (`slot_grant_deferrals`, `EAGAIN` — the requester retries) instead of failing `Corrupt`; a tail that does not move at all stays the loud class. The four "cycle until the tail covers X" loops share one bound (`COVER_CYCLES_MAX` = eight clause-b rounds). A manager releasing its own slot raises nothing on ring 0.
- **Landed in review round 7 — the root witness reads the slot; a stale page hint never refuses a legitimate release; the deferral is typed on the wire.** A wire release's moved `root` must now hold a node whose records name the RELEASED slot (a holder of two slots cannot release one with the other's root — the check reads the node, one extent per moved-root release). The derived `seq_floor` bound presumes the appender's own checkpoint refreshes its page (`head_hint`, `seq_offset` — met by PR 12: the appender's own checkpoint refreshes them, pinned; PR 12b's wire appender inherits the obligation); a page no appender checkpoint ever wrote (`ckpt_seq` 0 — every PR-4 wire appender's, since only the manager writes it) bounds the floor by the sane cap alone, so a legitimate release is never refused on a stale hint. A grant deferred behind ring 0's window answers the typed `Deferred` reply under its own status (`STATUS_DEFERRED` = 50) — a requester retries without parsing a reason; `slot_grant_deferrals` counts it. Gate stability: the Issue-25 contract now witnesses the TREE under a parked cadence (the sweep's own report, the slot's node count and underfull census) instead of a process-global merge counter a legal earlier pass could satisfy.

### Symmetric metadata program — pack per slot, the refcount probe, the shared-block index and gather mode (PR 7, DARK)

PR 7 (2026-09-15, `.benchmarks/2026-09-15-sym-pr7-refs.md`) lands the data plane's answer to the forest (design §5.4.3 / §5.4.4 / §5.7.5 / §5.8.5 C16; KD-SYM-8), dark behind bit 17 AND `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1` like PR 4: an unarmed forest and every flat mount take the shipped clone, free and pack paths verbatim (pinned), with every gauge below 0.

- **A block reference lives in its OWNER ino's slot tree — on every armed AND unarmed forest volume (a PR-1 defect fixed here).** The forest codec routes a reference by the slot bits of the owner ino at offset 17 (the `(s+1) << 40 | local` LOCAL KEY form), but every product site keyed the owner's GLOBAL ino (the rung-19 law), whose top bits are zero for any realistic ino — so through the routed layer every reference landed in the manager's NATIVE slot tree whatever slot its owner lived in: complete as a census (the union over every tree), wrong for the plane (a publish from any other appender named the manager's slot — the cross-region refusal) and fatal to the pack law's probe. The routed layer, which owns `route_ino`, now rewrites a forest volume's reference owners to their local key form at every entry point; `enable-symmetric` re-keys the references it converts under the stamp's width and native slot; the kind-6 set oracle folds a forest's owners back to the global form so the relayout contracts stay layout-blind. **A flat volume's ledger is byte-identical** (the GLOBAL owner verbatim). Consequence for an operator reading `block_ref_scan` output (fsck's C8 drift log): on a forest the owner reads as the local key ino.
- **Pack per `(writer, slot, data volume)` (§5.4.3 law 1).** On an armed set the packer keys its open packs by the tenant's `(meta volume, forest slot)` (`routing::pack_scope_key` — the NATIVE forest slot is `0` on every meta volume, and a converted set's pre-arm inos sit there on each, so the volume ordinal is what keeps two volumes' natives out of one block): two inos of two slots never share a pack block, so every reference of a pack block lives in ONE slot tree and its refcount there is exact — one range probe, nothing proportional to a handed-over tree. Open packs per data volume ≤ the leased slots being written (up to 64 rotor packs + affinity packs on a solo mount, ≤ 108 with inherited slots — PR 2's page budget) where PK2 has ONE; the seal residue at dismount is therefore ≤ that many partial blocks per data volume (`pack_blocks_sealed_dismount` reports it beside the PK acceptance figure; `pack_open_scopes` is the live population; `defrag --pack` consumes the partials). Unarmed, every tenant's scope is 0 and PK2's one open pack per data volume stands.
- **`refcount(b)` is probed on demand (§5.4.3 law 2)** — `block_ref_probes`: one `06 ‖ vol ‖ blk` range probe in one slot tree (the referencing ino's, which by the pack law holds every reference of an uncloned block), or the shared index. What PR 7 does NOT change: the mount still seeds the RAM refcount map and the free list from ONE by-block scan (the durable ledger's `recover_durable_block_refs`) — **the free list is design §5.5.1's allocation bitmap, PR 8's**; it landed and seeds from this scan's population — the mount-time by-block scan seeds the RAM refcount map on every writer mount on both layouts today, and the bitmap replacing it as the terminal-free engine ("a forest mount's open performs zero by-block census reads") is owed to PR 12/14. The RAM map stays the free-list / cursor / pin engine; the two DECISIONS the design names read the durable truth beside it: the W1 sole-owner patch confirms its RAM verdict with ONE probe of the patching ino's slot tree on an armed set (`DataRouter::sole_owner_durably`, run BEFORE the incarnation word is retired — exactly ONE reference and NONE of them SHARED: a second reference a predecessor lessee committed into a handed-over tree, which this mount's RAM map never saw, or a sole reference under a durable SHARED bit whose RAM mark landed elsewhere, refuses the patch into the CoW fallback; the durable flag is the authority, the RAM mark an accelerator; unarmed, no read), and a SHARED block's terminal free is decided at the index home. An UNSHARED block's terminal free stays the RAM count's verdict (`free_block` carries no ino, so there is no slot to probe there — PR 8's bitmap replaces that decision's input).
- **The shared-block index and the clone protocol (§5.4.4).** A block referenced from two slots arises only through clone/reflink; on an armed set `clone_file` runs the ordered protocol over the entries the dest map SHARES with the source (a staged source's spill under a full staging ring lands a FRESH copy — nothing to share, the protocol never runs over it) and only where two slot trees are involved — the source and dest live in different slots, or the source's reference already carries SHARED (a clone of a clone); a same-slot clone of an unshared block keeps its population in one tree and takes the shipped clone verbatim (no mark, no index, no bit): `MarkShared` at the source's holder through the routed layer's door (`RoutedMetaBackend::mark_block_refs_shared` — the cutover, delegation and S9 ownership gates every mutation of that ino takes; the SHARED bit — bit 1 of the reference value — set on the source's record in ONE tx under its 4a guard; `Gone` when no record exists ⇒ the clone aborts `ENOENT`-class with every pin undone once), `ShareBlock` at the **index home** (one tree-0 `shared_ref:` record per `(block, ino)` for BOTH inos, so C16 can check both sides; idempotent), then the cloner's own publish WITH the bit (a clone of a clone inherits it). **The home is resolved behind ONE function** (`shared_refs::index_home_volume`): in PR 7 volume 0's tree 0 — the manager's control plane, written through its one control-entry writer in ring 0's user class; PR 8 re-points it to the data volume's allocation-lease holder (the design's kind `0x09` in that holder's slot tree), and PR 12 makes a non-manager reach it over the wire (`ManagerCall::{MarkShared, ShareBlock, ReleaseShared}` exist and are served; this mount's own tree 0 answers today). **A terminal free of a SHARED block is never decided locally**: the block's RAM SHARED mark (seeded from the index at the mount's arm, set by `MarkShared`) sends the release to the home (`ReleaseShared`, keyed on the releasing `(ino, block_index)` — the legal same-`off` nested clone+clip class gives one ino two references to one block), which drops that entry, GC's an entry whose ino holds no reference (the "cloner died after `ShareBlock`" window) and answers `Held` (nonterminal — one RAM reference released, never to 0) or `Freed` (the ordinary terminal free runs, the mark clears). Both index writers admit their control entry PARKING before the home's verb mutex (PR 4's door law) — a busy ring 0 delays a clone or a shared free, never fails it; the one remaining failure class (the home's journal failing) KEEPS the reference and the mark, leaves the block allocated and counts `shared_release_failures` (must-stay-0) — a free on this path is never discarded. **A surviving sole owner stays SHARED** after the other side's release (the mark and the bit are never cleared while one holder remains — an un-share needs a cross-process "share in flight" witness the design does not yet name): the survivor's W1 patch takes the CoW path for that block from then on (`patch_ineligible_shared`) — the clone-template-then-diverge shape pays one CoW per first touch of a formerly shared block; priced in the note, owed to PR 12 with the wire protocol. **The W1 in-place patch reads the mark after its `SeqCst` fence and stands down on it whatever the count reads** (`patch_ineligible_shared` — the mark protects the block between step 1 and step 3, when the count still reads 1; loom `shared_ref_core` models the DESIGN's three-word protocol — the product's clone pins before it marks and its free reads the mark before it releases, so the product composition rests on the two-word §5.1 law plus pin-before-publish, stated in the model). A re-description of an existing reference (the passthrough truncate-shrink's `bk:off:len → bk:off:len'`) writes NO reference op — the record survives with its bit (a from-scratch re-Put stripped it and tripped C16 on a legal op). The served wire `ShareBlock` confirms every named `(ino, block_index)` against durable state — exists AND SHARED — before the index moves, rejecting the whole frame otherwise (`manager_verb_rejected`); the served `MarkShared` sets the serving mount's RAM mark beside the durable bit (a served mark that finds a W1 patch in flight on the block — an unstable incarnation word — trips `served_mark_shared_under_patch` on `invariant_tripwires`: the cross-process window PR 12 closes by taking the patch site's guard, detected until then). Gauges: `shared_blocks` (marked population per data volume), `mark_shared_calls`, `share_block_calls`, `release_shared_calls`, `shared_release_failures`.
- **fsck C16 — shared-index drift (report-only).** A SHARED-flagged reference the home does not name (source OR target ino), or an index entry whose ino holds no SHARED reference — both sides, per data volume, confirmed by a second census (verify-before-report), never repaired: the flag and the index are two durable homes of one fact, and the block's next release re-derives the truth from both (the §5.4.4 crash windows: a cloner dying after step 1 leaves a flag C16 reports and the source's release frees the block; dying after step 2 leaves an entry C16 reports and the GC arm drops). `fsck_shared_index_drift` is the gauge; 0 on every healthy set. Rides C8's unsharded pass (the ONE-walk census PR 1 owes is still per kind).
- **Gather mode (§5.7.5, KD-SYM-17) — `setfattr -n user.squeezefs.gather -v 1 <dir>`.** On an armed volume the directory's children then mint into the DIRECTORY's slot whatever the affinity ceiling says (a plain directory's children spill to the rotor past `A_max`), so their inodes live beside its entries and a `stat` of them needs no per-child token — the read-mostly directory's lever, **never automatic** (for the checkpoint-directory shape it would route every creator's later publishes through one holder). The name is the ONE user-settable member of the reserved `user.squeezefs.` family (admitted by name through the FUSE screen; `getfattr`/`setfattr`/`removexattr` work on it; values follow the knob law — `1/true/yes/on`). Cost: one node-cache probe of the parent's xattrs per create on an ARMED volume, nowhere else. `dir_gather_mints` counts them and closes the mint law `affinity_mints + rotor_mints + dir_gather_mints ≡ mints`. An unarmed mount never reads the opt-in.
- **Owed**: the free list as the allocation bitmap and the zero-scan open (PR 8), the index's move to the allocation holder (PR 8), the wire path of the three verbs for a non-manager and the un-share of a surviving sole owner (PR 12b — design row 12b), the S9 publish wire's SHARED bit (`WireBlockRefOp` carries `take` alone — PR 9's custody rewrite), the ONE-walk census (PR 1's), the `ls -l` row with/without gather and the pack ratio on the box (PR 13).
### Symmetric metadata program — cross-owner transactions (PR 6, DARK)

PR 6 (2026-09-15, `.benchmarks/2026-09-15-sym-pr6-cross-owner.md`) lands **cross-owner transactions** on PR 4's slot leases (design §5.6 — ruling **D18 reversed**; §5.6.4 the set-wide directory-rename lock, KD-SYM-14), dark behind bit 17 AND `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`. **`=0` and every bit-17-absent mount are the shipped S3.5 paths exactly** (intents keyed on ino 0, no shipped step, no lock, every Cross-owner gauge 0 — pinned on both layouts). Under the plane a namespace mutation whose objects live in slots OTHER appenders lease — `create`/`mkdir` under a directory another appender holds (the D1 shape: a shared output directory), `unlink`/`rmdir` of its entries, `link` into it, `rename` and `RENAME_EXCHANGE` across holders — is **an ordinary POSIX op**, executed as ONE S3.5 intent whose foreign steps SHIP to their slot holders:

- **The ladder as built.** Plan under the op's 4a guards — **foreign-home guards travel** (below) → `tx0` = STEP 0's records + the intent when step 0 is local, ONE entry in the initiator's ring (the intent keys on local 0 of that step's slot namespace, `xtx:` records in the xattr kind — ino 0's law per slot); when step 0 is another appender's the intent is written ALONE first, its own entry + barrier, into the mount's rotor slot → the initiator's ring barriered → every step in plan order: an own slot's through the local applier, a foreign one's SHIPPED as the S8 verb `xv_step` (`MetaCall::XvStep`, the 0x60 block) to the holder resolved through tree 0's lessee record + the appender→endpoint table (`SlotHolderCache::set_endpoint`, the join ladder's binding — LANDED in PR 12 for every appender Live at the join; a later joiner's arrives with PR 12b's wire `JoinAppender`) — where **`xv_apply_step` is the ONE applier** (the served side is the same function under the holder's guards, lease and ring, idempotent under the recorded `(pre, post)` witness, its reply after its durability lane) → the local participants barriered → the intent retired. **A create in a foreign directory mints the child in the CREATOR's rotor slot** (affinity never follows a foreign parent), so the child's later writes and publishes are local to the creator; only the name lives with the holder. A holder whose witness refuses a live step (the name taken between the plan and the apply) answers the op's errno (`EEXIST` / `ENOENT` / `ESTALE`) and a create's minted child is destroyed before the retirement.
- **A ship that fails never fail-stops.** The S3.5 lattice latch protects the witnesses' premise against a LOCAL mid-plan device error, which a holder that is down does not violate: the op returns the error, the intent stays OPEN, and the set's own **roll-forward cadence** (one task per armed set at the checkpoint landing ceiling; it adopts by the intent register's STATE — an intent an op of this process ABANDONED, or one found with no live owner here — never by elapsed time, so a live op's intent is its own however long it runs; every recovery re-reads the intent under the guards it acquires and applies THAT image, a record gone meanwhile applies nothing) — or the next mount's `recover_open_intents` — completes it once the holder serves again; the holder's successor applies-or-recognizes exactly once under the witness. An open intent no holder serves past the grace window (the 45 s membership TTL — a dead holder's slots are recovered inside it, PR 10) is **`xv_cross_owner_intents_stuck`, must stay 0**; the remedy is PR 10's recovery of the dead holder's slots, after which the cadence completes the intent.
- **Every `rename` whose SOURCE is a directory takes the set-wide `dir_rename` lease** (§5.6.4) — same-slot directory renames too, because a same-slot rename can complete the cycle a cross-slot one is checking against; file renames never take it. The lease is a tree-0 record on VOLUME 0 (`dir_rename → { holder appender, the serving manager's era, the CLOCK_REALTIME take }`) served by volume 0's manager (`ManagerCall::DirRenameLock` / `DirRenameUnlock` — the 0x60 block; `already` to its holder, `busy` naming the holder to anyone else, KD-SYM-7; the wire words SCREENED like every slot verb's — the frame must address volume 0, the id must name a `Live` page and never one of this mount's own regions, an unlock must name the holder — else `manager_verb_rejected`, nothing written), taken OUTERMOST (before any 4a guard — taken after one it would cycle with a peer's shipped step), held for the op, released on every exit. Under it the ancestor check runs on EXACT data and WITHOUT 4a guards (the initiator holds its own exclusive `D{}` guards across the walk; a shared guard on a colliding stripe would park it behind itself with the lease held — the lease IS the read's consistency): the target's parent chain is walked to the root — each hop O(1) off the directory-parent memo (fed by every directory mint and directory rename this mount performs and by every confirmed hop; a miss is a reverse dentry scan, `dir_rename_parent_scans`) — and each link `(parent, name) → child` is confirmed at the parent's slot holder through the guard-free read verb `lookup_exact` (one RPC per foreign link; a link the projection shows and the holder denies, or shows nowhere, is re-read up to three publication ceilings, then `EAGAIN`); a chain reaching the moved directory refuses `EINVAL` — so two nodes cannot rename directories into a cycle. Two directory renames of ONE process run under one record as tickets (the record goes with the last), and the lease is RAII (a dropped op releases it). **The expiry law**: the lock dies with the initiator's membership lease; a crashed holder's record outlives it until the recovery driver releases it (`manager_dir_rename_release_dead` — PR 10's death ledger is the driver; never a TTL on the record). `dir_rename_lock_acquires` / `dir_rename_lock_wait_ns` are its gauges.
- **The travelling guard (§5.6 line 1).** An initiator's 4a guards on objects in a FOREIGN holder's slots are held AT THE HOLDER for the op's duration: one `xv_guards` verb per foreign holder (`MetaCall::XvGuards`, 0x62 — the holder takes ONE canonical `lock_many` in its own table and parks the guards under the op's scope), the steps then ship under that scope and **apply without taking a guard** (a served step never parks on a 4a lock), and `xv_release` (0x63) frees the scope at the op's terminal outcome — the release rides the drop of the op's own guard set, so it obeys the same release law as every local guard. Two laws make the plane deadlock-free: every key whose lock lives in ONE process's table rides that table's one stripe-canonical `lock_many` (the initiator's own slots — and, in the test seam's declared-partition model, its other regions'), and an initiator acquires TABLES in ascending appender-id order (its own at its own id), so a waiter for a table holds only lower-ranked tables and the wait-for graph is acyclic (the hierarchical argument; in the shipped one-appender-per-process shape a holder's id IS its table's rank). **The expiry law**: a parked scope dies with its initiator's LEASE — where this process is the S6 membership authority the sweep keeps a scope exactly while its client is a live member and releases it the sweep after the eviction, never by elapsed time; without an armed plane (the shipped dark posture) the grace window (the 45 s membership TTL the eviction would have fired at) is the belt — `xv_cross_owner_guard_expiries`, **must stay 0** on a healthy set. An initiator that gives up on a holder (a wire timeout, a refusal) ships a fire-and-forget release behind, so a late-parked scope never denies the holder's stripes until the sweep. A two-holder create still costs exactly ONE shipped step plus its guard round trip; a same-process holder costs no round trip at all (pinned). *The first build took the step's guards at the holder while the initiator held its own across the ship — the scoping row's 27th cross-holder directory rename found the holder parked behind the initiator (a stripe collision in one process; across two nodes the two-mutual-initiator cycle), and the travelling guard is the fix.*
- **The D19/D20 arms.** `cross_owner_error` / the M1 pre-check belong to the per-volume-owner recipe (`SQUEEZEFS_MULTI_WRITER` + `volume set-owners`, bit-17-absent volumes); under the symmetric plane they are unreachable — its ownership is the slot lease, not the S8 `OwnerMap` — so **`meta_ship.cross_owner_refusals` reads 0 by construction on every symmetric mount**; the recipe's own refusals stay in force on flat volumes until PR 12/14 retire the recipe (rule: a flat mount's behavior is untouched by a dark rung).
- **Gauges** (`.stats`): the Cross-owner family — `xv_cross_owner_intents_{minted,retired,open}` (closure `minted ≡ retired + open`), `xv_cross_owner_steps_{shipped,served}`, `xv_cross_owner_intents_stuck` (must-stay-0), `xv_cross_owner_phase_ns` (`guard_rtt / plan / intent_barrier / ship_rtt / local_steps / retire / total`), `xv_cross_owner_steps_rejected` (must-stay-0), `xv_cross_owner_guard_rpcs` / `xv_cross_owner_guards_parked` / `xv_cross_owner_guard_expiries` (must-stay-0), `dir_rename_lock_{acquires,wait_ns}`, `dir_rename_parent_scans` — every one 0 on an unarmed or bit-17-absent mount, pinned; the S3.5 `crossvol_tx_*` family keeps its meaning (a shipped step's outcome is counted by the HOLDER).
- **Owed by the later rungs**: tokens over the foreign reads a plan makes (PR 5 — in one process the plan's reads are exact; on the wire they are the S5 projection until tokens land), the join ladder's binding of appender ids to endpoints and the wire initiator's take of the lock through `ManagerClient::dir_rename_lock` (the endpoint binding LANDED in PR 12 — rung 7 binds every Live appender's published endpoint and installs the step shipper; the wire initiator of another daemon is PR 12b's — design row 12b), the death ledger that drives `manager_dir_rename_release_dead` and re-leases a dead holder's slots (PR 10), dentry striping's `StripeDir` / `SupplyStripeIno` / `IsEmpty` / `DestroyStripe` steps (PR 7b — `InsertDentry` / `RemoveDentry` already take the KEY ino, so a stripe ino is another key), the `tar -x` and shared-directory rows (PR 13).
### Symmetric metadata program — the allocation lease, ranged block grants and park-and-reclaim (PR 8, DARK)

PR 8 (2026-09-15, `.benchmarks/2026-09-15-sym-pr8-alloc-lease.md`) lands the **data-plane allocation of the armed plane** and the **membership shard's park** on top of PR 4, dark behind bit 17 AND `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`; **`=0` and a bit-17-absent volume are the shipped posture EXACTLY** — the S9 residue-lane partition below is untouched, no holding, no park posture, every gauge here 0/absent (pinned). Design §5.5 / §5.5.1 / §5.5.2 / §5.5.3, KD-SYM-9/15.

- **Per DATA volume a floating allocation lease.** `alloc_lease:{vol_tag}` in tree 0 of the set's VOLUME 0 (the slot-0 volume — its manager is the set-wide bound, KD-SYM-2) names the holder (the KD-MW-2 identity), its appender id, its HOME metadata volume, its control ino, the bitmap's block count, the lease `term` and the bitmap pages as volume-qualified extents. First-come at term 1 (`AllocLeaseAcquire`); the holder is an ordinary mount. **A live holder's lease moves only by its release** (`AllocLeaseRelease`, the clean leave); everyone else allocates through grants from the holder. **The ordering law**: a DEAD holder (a `dead_member:` record) is succeeded ONLY once `recovered:{holder, home_vol}` exists — the record the manager that recovered the dead holder's home region writes (`RecordRecovered`, idempotent) AFTER its ring replay applied the bitmap deltas to the pages; before it the ask answers **`Deferred`** (the wire's `STATUS_DEFERRED`, EAGAIN — retry), never a grant. The successor then reads the recovered pages through the record's refs, COPIES them into extents of its own grant on ITS home volume and publishes the new refs (`AllocLeaseBitmap`, barriered by volume 0's lane) at `term + 1`; the dead region stays `Recovered` until `alloc_lease:` no longer names its extents (PR 10's release reads the record). `alloc_lease` on `.stats` lists the leases this mount holds (`[]` unarmed).
- **Ranged block grants replace the residue lanes on the armed plane** (KD-SYM-9). The holder carves `BlockGrant { start, len }` from the volume's **data allocation bitmap** — one bit per block, A/B page pairs exactly like the meta heap's bitmap (newest-valid-wins, generation-stamped, checksummed, the volume tag in the header — 32 KiB per TiB at the 4 MiB block), living in extents of the holder's OWN grant on its home volume, the same device as its ring. `G = clamp(2 × ewma_blocks_per_s × T_renewal, 64, capacity / (2 × writers))`: `T_renewal` is the membership beat (the grant is topped up as carriage — never expired), `2 ×` one EWMA window of under-estimate, the floor **64** = one write-pipeline BDP window of 4 MiB blocks, the cap = half the volume spread over the writers (half stays for reuse). **A grant's bits are SET in the bitmap and their deltas journaled in the holder's ring (kind-4 records with a `vol_tag` prefix — a 16-byte key beside the heap's 8-byte extent deltas) and BARRIERED before the grant is answered**: a grant a writer holds is always journaled, so a zombie's DMA into a granted-but-unpublished block is inside a range every successor reads as SET — the recovery floor is the grant's END. A terminal free clears the bit at `finish_free` (after quarantine and grace — a clear bit means "reallocatable now") and its CLEAR delta is journaled AT ONCE, in RAM order with every other delta of the holding (**the ordering law**: every bit mutation and its delta are queued under one per-holding lock and journaled head-first by one drainer at a time, so the recovery's per-key fold reads the order the bits moved — a CLEAR is never journaled after a later re-grant's SET); the dirty pages are written by the holder's checkpoint on its HOME volume only, BEFORE the ledger record that advances the tail past their deltas (the meta bitmap's exact position — a kill between the two never loses a journaled grant). **On a grant-armed allocator the bitmap IS the free list**: the local free list is drained into the bitmap at the arm and is never a mint source again (the window is the one source); a freed block is reallocated only through a grant whose carve SETS its bit again, never off a local free list with its bit clear. **The first hold of a volume is SEEDED from the allocator's derived truth ∪ the set's durable reference ledger** at the arm — every block below the cursor that is not on the free list reads SET, every durably referenced block reads SET, the free list's blocks CLEAR — so arming a POPULATED volume (a set populated before the knob, a converted set, the default flip) re-grants no live block; an allocator whose cursor reads 0 while the ledger names blocks of the volume is un-seeded and the arm refuses loud; every hold is checked against that truth in the LOSS direction and the arm refuses loud (`data_alloc_bitmap_drift`) rather than allocate beside a bitmap that reads a live block CLEAR. The checkpoint's page step never journals on the cycle's path: a delta queue the journaler could not land under ring pressure is re-kicked off the cycle and counted (`data_alloc_bitmap_deltas_deferred`) — the cycle always lands its pages and record. **The arm is the mount path's** (`arm_symmetric_allocation`, after the multi-writer arm): every data volume's lease is acquired first-come through volume 0's manager, held (fresh pages at term 1; after a clean remount or a crash this identity RE-HOLDS its own record — the window's deltas folded onto the pages; a same-node predecessor at another mount point is dead by the D0 flock's proof and is recorded, recovered and succeeded), and the allocator mints from the granted window from then on. **A grant's liveness is the writer's `dead_member` status, never a TTL**: a parked member's granted blocks stay its own until the death ledger names it, whereupon its grants are REVOKED into the S7 quarantine (their bits stay set until the drain proof). The writer's window mints lowest-first and asks for a top-up at 50 % consumption (the extent grant's own law); an empty window asks and retries once, then refuses `StorageFull`-class — never a poison. `MAX_LANES` and the power-of-two stranding table do not apply to a grant-armed writer: the capacity law of the section below becomes **"a writer's reachable supply is its open grants plus the holder's clear bits"**, published as `block_grants` / `block_grant_blocks` / `block_grant_returned` / `block_grant_revoked` and `data_alloc_bitmap_{set,clear}_bits` / `data_alloc_bitmap_population` per held lease.
- **Terminal frees, the free-grace ring, reclaim and quarantine belong to the volume's allocation holder.** A co-writer's displaced frees ship to THAT data volume's holder when the plane names one (`execute_shipped_frees` runs there verbatim; the set authority stays the route for a volume the plane does not name). One grace ring per data volume, one clock: **`free_grace_deferrals ≡ releases + offsets` holds PER VOLUME** (`free_grace_per_volume` lists each ring's terms), and **`free_grace_timeout_deferrals`** is the ring's surviving role under recall-driven qualification (PR 5's arm) — frees released past the routine bound because a reader never acknowledged (the forced release / the tightened deadline; 0 on a healthy fleet).
- **The C6/C8 census stays the ORACLE**: fsck compares the bitmap against `Σ slot trees ∪ shared index ∪ open grants` — a set bit with no reference and no open grant is a LEAK (released), a referenced block with a clear bit is the S1 class, report-only, counted on **`data_alloc_bitmap_drift` (must stay 0)**.
- **The death ledger's RECORDS** (§5.5.2): `dead_member:{node_token, mount_slot} → { epoch, ts }` and `recovered:{…, vol} → { by_term, ts }` in tree 0 of volume 0; `dead_members_recorded` / `dead_members_acted` (closure `acted ≡ recorded × regions held`) / `recovered_records` / `dead_member_propagation_ms`. **PR 8 builds the records and their executors; PR 10's recovery driver is the production writer of `dead_member:`** — the wire's `RecordDeath` verb is RESERVED and refuses loud naming PR 10 until then; `RecordRecovered` is live. **The §5.5.2 vol-0 rule is wired**: a manager whose probe of volume 0's ledger fails for longer than `T_owner` RELEASES its manager role — `manager_lease` reads `vacant`, the coordinator predicate refuses, `manager_vol0_unreachable` counts (the D0 claim itself stays; handing it to a successor is PR 10's ladder; the production prober is PR 10's ledger poll).
- **Membership sharded by home volume (KD-SYM-15).** Volume `v`'s manager is the S6 owner for mounts HOMED on `v` (a symmetric appender renews with its home volume's rendezvous record — volume 0's until PR 12b's joiner chooses a home — design row 12b); an owner serves ONE shard today — its plane (**`membership_shards`** = 1 on an owner, 0 on a member; volume `v`'s manager serving the mounts homed on `v`, V shards over V managers, is PR 12b's owner-role fusion — design row 12b); `squeezefs clients` unions the shards. The membership harness gained the sharded shape (`membership_sim::run_sharded` — the SIM-1 driver PR 13 runs at 12,500 × 64).
- **A manager's death is its home shard's membership outage — the symmetric appender's `T_self` action is PARK-AND-RECLAIM, not poison** (§5.5.3). At `T_self` without a grant the member enters **`Parked`** (posture word **`appender_parked`**): the conveyor stops ADMITTING (a distinct PRE-admission state ahead of ring admission — it **never** feeds `note_journal_failure`, so the D1.b escalation cannot turn a park into a fail-stop: `T_park_max` > `SQUEEZEFS_TIMEOUT` by construction), in-flight entries LAND but their **acks are HELD** on the durability lane and released in journal order on the grant, reads keep serving, token grants and recalls continue (a parked lessee is still the lock master for its slots), DMA to already-granted blocks continues, the **checkpoint/flush task CONTINUES** (the flush ceiling holds through a park — `appender_flush_ceiling_overruns` stays 0), the page stays `Live`, nothing is poisoned; the FUSE op watchdog labels `PARKED` instead of overdue exactly the ops the park can hold — one at or before its publish and no older than the park (a lost reply or an op wedged before the park stays overdue) and the bounded-error timers on the journal barrier are suspended for the park's bound. The member re-asserts as a **reclaim** every beat (one join presenting the epoch it holds — what the successor's grace window admits — against the successor venue the home volume's ledger observation names, paced at the renewal interval; the parked renewal loop keeps ticking and is woken by the observation); an admitted reclaim RELEASES the park (`slot_lease_reclaims`, `appender_park_ns` exact-sum `wait_successor / reclaim_rtt / total`). A clean unmount over a standing park is REFUSED loud on EVERY volume of the set (the refusal latches for the process): the committers parked at the door fail (`EIO` — nothing more is admitted into a region a successor may already have recovered), the landed entries' held acks release, every page stays `Live` for the next open's own-residue recovery. **The park is bounded**: **`t_park_max_ms`** = `manager_failover_bound_ms + grace` (the S6 owner-failover window `LeaseClocks::grace`; derived, published) — past it, or on a reclaim answered "not custody", the member self-fences terminally exactly as before the split (`data_custody::poison`, `EIO` to the parked ops): **`appender_park_expiries` must stay 0 on a healthy failover** — it is the ONE terminal signal. `data_custody::poison` therefore SPLITS **by OBJECT**: the S9 remote-custody client, the lane and publish-era refusals and a not-custody answer poison at `T_self` exactly as shipped (`MemberSession::self_fence`); only the symmetric appender's OWN membership lease parks (`self_fence_as`, the renewal tick its one caller) — poison at `T_self` only for objects whose lease is NOT reclaimable under the S6 grace contract (S9 remote custody); park for slot leases and the appender region. `appender_parks` counts parks, `appender_park_acks_held` the acks a park held.
- **The maintenance coordinator is volume 0's MANAGER; each mount evaluates the inode plane over the slots it LEASES** (the lessee shards — KD-PV-16's owner shards one grain finer). `fsck` / `defrag` / `job` coordinator-class acts on any other armed mount refuse naming the manager (`jobs::maintenance_coordinator_refusal` consults the symmetric predicate ahead of the per-volume-owner map); C9/C10 candidates whose slot another appender leases are left to that lessee (`fsck_inode_plane_foreign_slot_scoped`), the manager judges the UNLEASED slots too, and **`fsck_inode_plane_slots_covered` ≡ leased ∪ unleased** summed over the pass's volumes — every hosted slot on a solo manager or an unarmed volume.
- **Gauges** (`.stats`): the Allocation-lease family — `alloc_lease`, `block_grants`, `block_grant_blocks`, `block_grant_returned`, `block_grant_revoked` (the HOLDER's ledger), `block_grant_topups` / `block_grant_window_remaining` (the WRITER's window, summed over its grant-armed allocators: asks issued and blocks still unconsumed — a write the window covers asks nothing, so a holder's `block_grants` moves only with a top-up; a joiner keeps minting from a grant a DEAD manager carved, the successor's re-hold leaving a live page's remainder alone), `data_alloc_bitmap_set_bits`, `data_alloc_bitmap_clear_bits`, `data_alloc_bitmap_population`, `data_alloc_bitmap_deltas_journaled`, `data_alloc_bitmap_deltas_deferred` (drains the ring's full user window refused — the deltas stayed queued for the next drainer, the checkpoint cycle untouched), `data_alloc_bitmap_drift` (**must stay 0**), `free_grace_timeout_deferrals`, `free_grace_per_volume`, `dead_members_recorded`, `dead_members_acted`, `recovered_records`, `dead_member_propagation_ms`; the park family — `appender_parked`, `appender_parks`, `appender_park_ns`, `appender_park_expiries` (**must stay 0**), `appender_park_acks_held`, `slot_lease_reclaims`, `t_park_max_ms`; `membership_shards`; fsck's `fsck_inode_plane_foreign_slot_scoped` / `fsck_inode_plane_slots_covered`. All 0 / `[]` on every unarmed or bit-17-absent mount, pinned.
- **Owed by the later rungs**: the recovery DRIVER that writes `dead_member:` from a home shard's eviction, runs `replay_data_alloc_deltas` on a foreign home volume and writes `recovered:` (PR 10 — the seam here is the in-process `record_death` + the contracts' own replay call), the wire carriage of the block-grant top-up on the renewal grant and a writer's home volume other than volume 0 (PR 12b — design row 12b), the recall-gating arm of the free path (PR 5), the shared index's home (PR 7), the fleet legs of gates 3/4 (PR 10/13).

### Symmetric metadata program — read tokens (PR 5, DARK)

PR 5 (2026-09-15, `.benchmarks/2026-09-15-sym-pr5-tokens.md`) lands **GPFS-strict read tokens** on PR 4's slot leases — the S10 LOOKUP delegation generalized to EVERY foreign object (design §5.7, R-SYM-4 / KD-SYM-19: GPFS-strict is the ONLY foreign-read method; the bounded-staleness projection of user-visible foreign metadata is retired under the plane) — dark behind bit 17 AND `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`; **`=0` and a bit-17-absent mount are the shipped posture EXACTLY** (no holder, no reader, the free-grace ring's epoch law, v1 frames on flat — pinned).

- **The holder.** An armed writer is every object's token HOLDER for the slots it leases. A grant (`TokenCall::Grant { object, wants }`) CARRIES the records — the inode record, the carried xattrs (`layout`, the symlink target, `user.`/`security.`/`trusted.` names), a directory's dentry set paged by the reply cap with a cookie continuation (the S8 frame law) — so a reader pays **0 RPCs after first touch until recalled**; a re-grant to a holder answers `already`. The token verbs (`Grant` / `Recall` / `RecallAck` / `Release`, `TOKEN_SCHEMA` 1, their own verb block under `CLUSTER_WIRE_SCHEMA` 5) ride the S8 owner listener beside the manager's, dispatched by volume ordinal.
- **Recall before the conflicting commit — one batch per conveyor pass.** At the top of the pass, under the batch's 4a guards and BEFORE ring admission or any node lock (§4.4 pt 5 + the lock law), the UNION of the batch's objects with outstanding tokens is recalled ONCE (`dlm_token_recalls`, `dlm_token_recall_batches`, `dlm_token_recall_fanout` p50/p99); the pass proceeds when every recall is acked OR the reader's membership lease has expired (`dlm_token_recall_expired_with_lease` — closure **`recalls ≡ acks + expired_with_lease`**); a recall unacked by a LIVE member past its lease TTL is **`dlm_token_recall_timeouts_live`, must-stay-0** (the stuck-reader tripwire; the commit proceeds, the token is retired, the free-grace ring opens as the timeout path). `dlm_token_recall_rtt_ns` is exact-sum `send / drain / ack / total` per batch. Control-plane writes (`writer_claim`, jobs, every xattr a grant never carries) recall nothing.
- **The reader** (`-o ro` under the knob = member-reader + token client, §5.7.2): drops the recalled records, drains its in-flight serves on the object (`ServeStamp`), purges its block-key census (the R-6 purge), THEN acks; the next resolve re-fetches — **a foreign create is visible at the reader's NEXT resolve, exact, never bounded**. A reader whose membership lease is past `T_self`, or whose standing recall channel is not fresh, serves NOTHING from its cache (`dlm_token_serve_refusals`). `reader_staleness_bound_ms` reads **0**; every kernel / daemon cache TTL derives to 0 (see [Read-only coherent mounts](#read-only-coherent-mounts--o-ro--one-writer-plus-n-readers)); the S5 poll stays the control plane, reading the predicted 4 KiB ledger slot first (`meta_kv_revalidate_ledger_read_bytes` / `_full_reads`). The mount refuses LOUD without the forest (naming `enable-symmetric`), a membership lease (`SQUEEZEFS_MEMBERSHIP_BIND` on the writer), a holder endpoint it can RESOLVE (since PR 12 the manager's published claim-set endpoint, per-slot holders off tree 0 — no declared authority; `SQUEEZEFS_MW_AUTHORITY` is refused beside the plane; a manager that published no listener refuses the mount naming it) or the cluster secret; and — review round 2 — with `SQUEEZEFS_FREE_GRACE_DRAIN_OBSERVED=0` or `SQUEEZEFS_FREE_GRACE_DRAIN_EPOCH_STAMP=0` (the reader's ack ATTESTS the observed drain and the layout-cache step; no timer stands behind them under tokens, so the levers govern the `=0` reader alone), with an explicit non-zero kernel TTL (`-o attr_timeout=` / `entry_timeout=` / `dir_entry_timeout=` / `negative_timeout=` or their `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_*_TTL_MS` env — a bounded second read method R-SYM-4 forbids; kernel-side invalidation on recall is what would make one legal), and when the holder at the resolved endpoint answers no token verb at the arm's probe (a writer mounted without `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1` — the join ladder stands the listener up — or a down listener — refused at the ARM, never as EIO at the first resolve). Zero device writes across the session, pinned; a clean unmount RELEASES its tokens at the holder.
- **Recall-driven free-grace** (§5.7.3): see [Freed-offset grace period](#freed-offset-grace-period-spec-68-item-3) — a terminal free publishes directly after the recalls (`free_grace_recall_gated_frees`); the ring is the TIMEOUT path (`free_grace_recall_timeout_deferrals`).
- **bset frame v2 + the screen** (§5.8.2, both loaders): under bit 17 every frame carries `(appender_id, g)` before its checksum (32 → 40 B; v1 byte-identical on flat, pinned). A frame is FOREIGN iff (1) `g` > the slot's `g_current` (`appender_fence_breach` under a PR fence — must-stay-0 — else `foreign_frames_screened`), (2) `g` ≤ the recorded tails' generation AND at or past that leaf's recorded tail (PR 4's `slot_tails:{s}` through the ONE accessor PR 10's recovery driver reads too), (3) `g` below an earlier frame's in the same log; a predecessor's frame BEFORE the tail is kept. A screened frame with the current generation's frame behind it is `foreign_frame_overwrite_detected` — acked loss on a non-PR substrate — refused LOUD, never served with a hole.
- **Gauges** (`.stats`, per volume): the Token family + the two free-grace faces + the ledger economy — see [Observability](#observability); every one 0 / `null` on an unarmed mount, pinned.
- **Owed by the later rungs**: the per-object holder → endpoint binding (LANDED in PR 12: the reader binds per slot holder off tree 0 + the published claim-set endpoint) and N daemon processes on one volume (PR 12b — design row 12b; a reader today dials the manager, the one writer,), per-STRIPE tokens for a striped directory (PR 7b — a stripe ino is just another object here), the metanode ship for a foreign slot's mutation (PR 6/12), the death path's tail recorder and `Recovering` / `Recovered` (PR 10), kernel-side dentry invalidation on recall (an economy: the TTL-0 posture is exact but pays a daemon round trip per kernel lookup), the deletion of the S5 projection and the epoch fan-in (PR 14's flip).

### Symmetric metadata program — directory striping (PR 7b, DARK)

PR 7b (2026-09-16, `.benchmarks/2026-09-16-sym-pr7b-dir-stripes.md`) lands **directory STRIPING** on PR 4's slot leases, PR 5's tokens and PR 6's cross-owner intents (design §5.6.5, KD-SYM-20; owner ruling R-SYM-5 — the Lustre DNE-2 shape). Dark behind bit 17 AND `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`; `=0`, a bit-17-absent volume and `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_DIR_STRIPES=1` are the shipped posture exactly (no flip, no map, `user.squeezefs.stripes` a reserved name for the screen, every Striping gauge 0 — pinned).

- **What a striped directory is.** A hot shared directory `D` spreads its dentries over `K` **stripes** — `K` stripe inos `D_0 … D_{K-1}`, ordinary `S_IFDIR` records with no name of their own, each minted in a DIFFERENT node's slot on `D`'s volume — with `stripe(name) = hash54(name) % K` (the volume's seeded dentry hash, so every stripe's entries and the K-way `readdir` merge share ONE order) and stripe `i`'s dentries keyed under `D_i` in `D_i`'s slot tree. Stripe ownership IS slot ownership: a create into `D` is one shipped `InsertDentry` to the name's stripe holder (`dir_stripe_ships`), so `N` creators spread over `K` holders instead of one, and a create recalls only its stripe's readers. **The stripe map is `K + 2` reserved-name dentries in `D`'s own tree** — `\0sqz.stripe:NN → D_i`, `\0sqz.migrating → D_0`, `\0sqz.striped → D_0` (the COMMIT marker; the map is live iff it exists and names stripe 0 — no marker names `D` itself, so the `..` reconnect scan never finds a self-reference) — names whose leading NUL byte no user operation can ever pass (the VFS refuses it), filtered from every listing. The map therefore rides `D`'s token verbatim and C9 sees a stripe as REFERENCED by its map entry; no slot-tree key kind is added (bit 17's kind set stays PR 1 + PR 7's — what this rung adds to the bit's meaning is the reserved NUL name space inside the dentry kind). **A stripe never carries a map of its own**: it is no flip candidate at the trigger, the xattr or the `mkdir` arm.
- **When a directory flips.** (1) **Automatically** at the holder: when the foreign creates it served into `D` over one `T_idle` window (`SQUEEZEFS_SYM_T_IDLE_MS`) exceed `N_floor` (`slot_offer_n_floor`) AND come from MORE THAN ONE creator (the creator is read off the shipped child's slot) — one foreign creator, however many creates, is a HANDOVER candidate, never a striping one. (2) **Explicitly**: `setfattr -n user.squeezefs.stripes -v 64 <dir>` (a COMMAND the holder executes, never a stored value; `getfattr -n` answers the `K` in force on a striped directory, `getfattr -d` omits it; **holder-local until PR 12**: from a mount that does not hold the directory it answers `EAGAIN` naming the holder — run it there). (3) **At `mkdir`** under `-o stripe_dirs` (the DNE-2 default; every new directory on an armed volume). `K` = `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_DIR_STRIPES` (derived 64), never re-striped.
- **The flip (§5.6 intent protocol).** The holder re-checks the commit marker (a concurrent flip that won is found with nothing minted), asks the `K` heaviest foreign creators it observed to each SUPPLY a stripe ino minted in their own slot (`SupplyStripeIno`, `dir_stripe_supply_rpcs` — a creator that declines or is gone is replaced by the next; the remainder is the holder's — asked while the flip holds no guard), takes its 4a guards in ONE canonical acquisition (`D`'s, every remainder stripe's and every marker key — the lock law every op of the daemon obeys: a guard taken while another is held self-deadlocks on a stripe collision), mints the remainder under it, then writes the map as ONE PR 6 intent of `K + 2` marker inserts with the commit marker LAST: a reader sees either no map or the whole map, and a kill at any step rolls forward to the whole map at the slot's next lessee (pinned at every window; a plan that fails names its mints loud — C9's class). From that commit `D`'s EXISTING names re-home **lazily**: `D`'s own tree stays a secondary home every READ consults (a lookup that misses the stripe reads it; where both hold a name the stripe's entry wins — the LWW rule), the holder moves names to their stripes in the background (`dir_stripe_migrated_names` — insert into the stripe first, remove from `D` second), every MUTATION of a name still in `D`'s tree re-homes it first (`dir_stripe_rehomed_on_touch`), the `\0sqz.migrating` flag clears only once `D`'s tree is re-verified empty of user names under the exclusive guard, and a migration the holder died inside resumes at the directory's next access by its slot's lessee.
- **Every operation on a striped directory.** `lookup` / `create` / `unlink` / `link` / `rename` route `(D, name)` to the stripe `hash54(name) % K` names — the ordinary op with the stripe as its key parent (cross-owner through PR 6 when the stripe's holder is another appender; a rename within `D` across two stripes is the two-parent rename). `readdir` is the STREAMED K-way merge of the stripes (and `D`'s own tree) in the shipped `(hash54, coll)` cookie order — each stream refilled only when its head is consumed, so a `readdir` page of `max` reads at most `max + (K + 1) × max/(K + 1)` ≈ `2 × max` raw entries; the cookie of the last entry is the exact continuation, so a name present for the whole scan is listed exactly once under concurrent creates/unlinks (`dir_stripe_readdir_merges`). `stat D` folds the stripes: `nlink = D.nlink + Σ (D_i.nlink − 2)`, `mtime`/`ctime` = the newest — each stripe's record IS the exact delta its own inserts wrote in the same transaction as the dentry, so nothing ships per insert and no delta can be lost; the fold is persisted onto `D`'s record ONCE PER CHECKPOINT CADENCE per directory when a stripe's time passed it (`dir_stripe_time_batches` — never at the `stat` rate: the write recalls `D`'s readers). **`rmdir D`** is ONE intent under ONE guard set — `D`'s exclusive guard, an EXCLUSIVE guard on every stripe (travelling to a foreign holder) and the parent's: `D`'s own tree and every stripe are probed empty under them (exact — every insert that took a stripe's guard has landed, none can be in flight; `ENOTEMPTY` writes nothing), then the intent removes `D`'s name and its markers and marks every stripe and `D` dying (`nlink 2 → 0`); a kill at any step rolls forward whole at the next lessee. An insert that parked behind the rmdir's guards (a create, a link, a rename into `D`) resumes to a parent whose record reads `nlink 0` or is gone and is refused `ENOENT` (`dir_stripe_dying_refusals`) — never landed in a destroyed stripe; the stripes' records are then destroyed as hygiene (`DestroyStripe`; one the process did not reach is an `nlink 0` corpse named by no map — the next mount's sweep reclaims it).
- **What it costs.** A create into a striped directory is one shipped insert (+ the intent's own entry and barrier) unless the name's stripe is this node's (`1/K` of them); `ls` reads ≈ 2 pages' worth of raw entries per `readdir` page across the `K + 1` streams (RAM-resident range reads on the holder; `K + 1` token grants cold on a reader, then none until a recall); `stat D` reads `K` stripe records (RAM); a NEGATIVE lookup in a striped directory pays two probes for ever (the stripe, then `D`'s own tree — the permanent secondary home's read price; a hit pays one); `rmdir D` is `K` probes + one intent of `2K + 3` steps + `K` destroys (rare). The local wave row (7 declared holders, K = 14, 2,000 creates): every foreign create one ship, per-stripe population 113–170 against a mean of 143 at 7 holders (20–47 against 32.3 at 31 holders, K = 62), no handover — the note carries the figures.
- **Crash windows (as built).** A stripe supplied but never recorded (a kill before the intent) is C9's class — an unreferenced `S_IFDIR` record the census cleans. A kill inside the flip's intent rolls forward whole. A kill between a name's stripe insert and its removal from `D` leaves it in both homes — the stripe wins, the resumed migration drops the duplicate. A kill inside an `rmdir`'s intent rolls the removal forward whole — no window leaves `nlink 0` stripes under a live directory.
- **fsck C17 — stripe consistency** (report-only, the C8 posture; `fsck_stripe_findings` **must stay 0**): a stripe named by more than one directory's map, a map entry naming a missing stripe (also C10's dangling-dentry face), a commit marker over an incomplete map (fewer than two entries, a gap, or a marker naming anything but stripe 0), a dentry in stripe `i` whose `hash % K ≠ i`, a user name in `D`'s own tree after `migrating` cleared (a stale-route insert's trace — served by the reads, re-homed at its next mutation). The marker census rides the ONE dentry walk C9/C10 already run; only the striped population is re-read, and the confirm pass judges every C17 suspect against ONE fresh census. **The offline probe writes nothing** — its teardown used to write a checkpoint from a "read-only probe" on every offline `fsck` (content-equivalent on a flat volume, a loss on a forest whose writer died; fixed 2026-09-16, every layout). **On a read-only probe over a volume whose writer died (a `Live` appender page), the inode plane — C9/C10/C17 — records NO verdict** (`inode_plane_volumes_covered` 0): the dead writer's acked records sit in a ring window only its own next open replays; run `fsck` after that open's clean unmount (or PR 10's recovery verb).
- **Gauges** (`.stats`): the Striping family — `dir_striped_dirs`, `dir_stripe_flips`, `dir_stripe_supply_rpcs`, `dir_stripe_ships`, `dir_stripe_readdir_merges`, `dir_stripe_migrated_names`, `dir_stripe_time_batches`, `dir_stripe_dying_refusals`, `dir_stripe_rmdirs`, `dir_stripe_rehomed_on_touch`, `fsck_stripe_findings` — see [Observability](#observability); every one 0 on an unarmed mount, pinned.
- **Owed by the later rungs**: the per-STRIPE token on a wire reader (PR 5's token client fetches a stripe like any object; the reader's `readdir` merge over token pages is PR 12b's — design row 12b), N daemon processes as stripe holders and the shipped explicit flip (PR 12 — the local shape's holders are the declared regions), re-striping (a named follow-on), the `sym-shared-dir` box rows (PR 13).

### Symmetric metadata program — custody by the slot holder (PR 9, DARK)

PR 9 (2026-09-16, `.benchmarks/2026-09-16-sym-pr9-custody.md`) lands **write custody by the SLOT HOLDER** on PR 4's slot leases and PR 5's tokens (design §5.5 the "S9 custody endpoint" row, §5.1.5, §5.1.4, §5.4.3; PR-plan row 9) — dark behind bit 17 AND `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`; **`=0` and a bit-17-absent mount are the S9 paths exactly** (the authority client verbatim, one relaxed load on the acquire path — the S9 suites and `sym_custody_tests`' unarmed pin).

- **Where the server is.** Under the plane the S9 custody protocol (join / renew / release / `T_self` / the custody epoch) is UNCHANGED; its SERVER for an inode object is the appender leasing the object's slot — resolved through tree 0's lessee + the `SlotHolderCache` (PR 6's `step_home` predicate: a declared region's slot is another appender's for every cross-owner decision), never a manager RPC. One JOINed custody client per holder endpoint (its renewal cadence on the `sqz-lease` lane; a second JOIN at one holder would revoke the first's grants, so the dial is single-flight **per holder** — review round 2, Issue 6: each holder's dial runs under its own async mutex, the holder map's lock is a short sync one, and a failed dial is remembered for one renewal beat (finding 15's decline shape), so a holder that accepts and never answers parks its own joiners to the wire's 10 s bound and nobody else's — pinned: a dead holder A delays an acquire against live holder B by nothing); an OWN file's custody is the local arbiter (0 RPCs — gate 1's `dlm_rpcs == 0` on a solo armed mount, pinned); a foreign file costs exactly ONE lock round trip (`dlm_rpcs += 1`, `dlm_custody_via_slot_holder += 1`); a holder whose endpoint is not bound (the join ladder's census binding — PR 12) refuses `EAGAIN` naming it. **Every deterministic refusal is typed** (Issue 11 — `Refused { errno }`, never `LockFailed`, which the POSIX-5 ladder retries for its whole budget): `EAGAIN` where a later attempt can succeed (an unbound holder, a slot mid-handover, a slot moved twice under one acquire, a declined dial), `EIO` where it cannot without an operator (no custody authority armed at the holder, a rejected or foreign frame); `LockFailed` stays the CONFLICT class alone. **The custody generation is PER HOLDER** (Issue 7): a slot holder's client (`WriteCustodyClient::connect_slot_holder`, `CustodyScope::SlotHolder`) folds neither its lease epoch into the process custody generation nor its era into the durable term — a holder's lease-epoch counter is one of N and its term is its volume's — so a grant adopted from a busier holder never voids a DMA authorized under another holder's grant (pinned: two live holders with differing join counters and eras, a DMA captured under A's grant submits after B's grant is adopted, `data_dma_epoch_refusals` flat, the generation and the durable term unmoved); the set authority's client (the co-writer) is byte-identical.
- **The grant carries the token** (§5.7.1, Lustre's intent lock): the acquire rides the token wire as `TokenCall::CustodyGrant` (`TOKEN_SCHEMA` 1 unchanged — the variant appended) and the holder answers `CustodyGranted { grant, records }` in ONE round trip: the S9 arbitration on its custody authority under the caller's lease FIRST (the arbiter's `inode_{ino}` lease keeps the holder's own writes off the file), then the object's records under the grant ∥ pass gate (register before read); the writer adopts the grant exactly as an authority's and installs the records as its token in a per-`(holder, volume)` token plane whose standing recall channel is what lets the holder's later commit on the file RECALL it (the conveyor pass's hook) and the writer re-fetch — exact at the next resolve (`dlm_custody_token_carried`). A `NotHolder` answer (tree 0 moved under the writer's view) re-resolves the holder through the cache's endpoint table once. The arbiter keys on the GLOBAL ino, derived at the holder from the wire's LOCAL key ino — one word, never a second to trust.
- **Custody across a handover** (§5.1.4 — flush-then-transfer; review round 2, Issues 5/9): a slot whose files a writer holds custody of from this holder does NOT move yet — the handover is DEFERRED in the **retryable class** (`KvError::HandoverDeferred` → the wire's `ManagerReply::Deferred` / `STATUS_DEFERRED` / `EAGAIN`; the slot-lease cadence's "not this tick" skip; counted `slot_handover_custody_deferrals` in the Slot-lease family) and the deferral is **BOUNDED by a recall**: the first deferral RECALLS every live grant on the slot through the S9 pull channel — one `RecallNotice { ino, grant_id }` per grant, carried on the renewal, acquire and release replies and on the standing notice poll (`CUSTODY_SCHEMA` 7 → 8; a quiet writer's parked poll receives it at once, a busy one's next carrier within a beat) — and the recall is **re-gathered from state on every carrier** until the release lands (review round 3, Issue 21 — the demotion/shrink notices' own law: a reply lost on the wire re-travels the notice at the next carrier; `dlm_custody_recalled` counts each grant once). **The recall reaches the writer's CACHED lease** (round 3, Issue 20 — the FUSE layer's `active_leases`, held from a file's first write to its last close): the writer's custody client REVOKES it through the recall hooks the mount installs beside the finding-34 release gate (`SqueezefsFilesystem::install_slot_custody_hooks`) — the cached lease is PARKED (removed from the cache, so the ONE accessor `cached_lease_token` refuses it and the next write RE-ACQUIRES through the ladder: `CUSTODY_DEFERRED` at the old holder while the slot is mid-handover, the NEW holder after the move — the direct-drive snapshot refuses a remote lease outright), the ino's in-flight CUSTODY USES drain (every mutating handler — write / setattr / fallocate / copy_file_range / flush / fsync / release — and every detached DMA continuation of a foreign-custody file holds an RAII use from entry to end; a recall's release waits for those that took the token before the revoke, so no handler can merge or DMA under the grant after it left), the parked lease drops and its release is queued GATED (the finding-34 gate flushes the ino's acked bytes — fsync-grade — before the verb departs), and a settle loop at the derived park drives it (`dlm_custody_recalls_absorbed`). The slot is marked **mid-handover** meanwhile, so a re-acquire at the old holder — on the token wire or the custody wire — answers `CUSTODY_DEFERRED` (0x47) instead of undoing the recall (the acquire path parks the derived `renew / 20`, re-resolves the holder through tree 0 and retries inside its per-attempt budget, then answers the CONFLICT class the POSIX-5 ladder retries for the op's budget — a write BLOCKS across the move, never fails; a second file of the slot written inside the window is delayed by the window, never refused; the mark expires after `2 × renew` of the holder's S9 clocks if the requester stops retrying — `slot_handover_recall_bound_ms` — and the next attempt re-arms it).
