The requester's next cadence tick finds the grants gone and the slot moves — with the mark **HELD through the flush-then-transfer** (round 3's ×10 stamped finding: the completing tick had CLEARED the mark before the transfer, so a recalled writer's retry inside that ≈ 10 ms window was GRANTED at the old holder and the grant spanned the move, 1 in 10 runs; now the handover arms its slot's mark BEFORE its grant census and holds it to its terminal outcome — the slot moved or the release aborted — and every grant path (the token wire's `CustodyGrant`, the custody wire's ACQUIRE plain and ranged) reads the mark AGAIN after `owner.grant` and releases the grant `CUSTODY_DEFERRED` when it stands: a Dekker pair through the owner's grant lock and the mark's, so a grant that landed before the census is seen and recalled and one that landed after defers itself; the leave arms a held mark for every held slot before its census, its half of the same pair; pinned by `a_grant_inside_the_flush_then_transfer_is_deferred_to_the_next_holder` with the handover parked mid-transfer. The THIRD window — a request RESOLVED to the old holder before the move and served one RTT after it, when the slot read `Unleased` and the old holder held nothing to grant (the flat ×20's 1-in-20) — is closed at both ends: the served side answers `NotHolder { 0 }` for an unleased slot on a NON-manager (`foreign_slot_holder`'s new arm — the manager maintains an unleased tree, KD-SYM-2/3, so it is the holder of every object in it and the only one that serves it), and the writer RE-RESOLVES through tree 0 after every grant and releases one from an appender tree 0 no longer names as the holder, re-acquiring where it points — `dlm_custody_stale_holder_grants`, ≈ 0): a grant never spans a handover (no write lands under a stale holder's custody — every write after the move runs under a FRESH custody from the new holder, pinned by the fencing token), the recall VOIDS nothing (it is not the `dead_grants` revocation — no DMA refusal, no acked write lost), and the wait is one renewal beat of the recalled writer's release whatever the file's open lifetime (the round-1 build waited for the writer's last close — unbounded under a long-lived open-written file). **The bounds, stated** (round 3, Issue 24): a writer that polls — one beat + the settle; a writer that NEVER polls (dead or partitioned) — its lease expires at `T_owner` and the S9 sweep (`expire_due`, the `mw_custody_sweep` cadence; the leave runs it at its bound) retires its grants, so the handover's worst case is `T_owner`, not a beat; a requester that ABANDONS the handover leaves the recalled writer unable to re-acquire at the old holder for `2 × renew` (every attempt `CUSTODY_DEFERRED`, the write blocking) until the mark expires. Chosen over a derived deferral bound followed by revocation because a revoked live grant costs the work authorized under it (the S9 law's price for a DEAD client, unacceptable for a routine handover); pinned end to end (`a_deferred_handover_recalls_the_custody_and_completes_within_a_beat`, `a_recalled_writers_cached_lease_is_revoked_and_its_writes_reacquire_at_the_new_holder` — a busy FUSE-layer writer across the recall and the move with zero failed writes, `a_lost_recall_carrier_re_travels_the_recall_on_the_next_one`). **The HOLDER's clean leave is a handover to nobody** (round 3, Issue 22): before a covered region's slots go `Unleased`, every live custody grant this holder issued on their files is RECALLED (the same recall) and the leave WAITS for the releases at the derived park up to `T_owner + renew` (the sweep at the bound); a grant that survives keeps its REGION in the uncovered posture WHOLE — its page `Live` with its roots and tail, its ring and grant claimed, its slots leased in tree 0, no `Free` page and no return written for it (review round 4, Issue 28: the round-3 build excluded the blocked region from the slot release alone and its later loops wrote the page `Free` and released the ring over a leased slot — KD-SYM-3's page-home root lost; pinned on the bound-expiry path with a renewing, non-releasing writer: page `Live`, tree 0 `Leased`, the next open's `appender_self_recoveries` 1 and every acked record present) — loud. **The operator's face of that failure mode**: a clean unmount of a holder whose recalled writer keeps renewing but never releases WAITS, per covered region and sequentially, up to `T_owner + renew` (≈ 55 s at the shipped S9 clocks — the writer's own `T_self` is strictly earlier, so a writer that stopped answering is swept sooner) plus the writer's release drain (its ino's fsync-grade flush); the wall is announced at its start by the WARN `PR 9: the clean leave found live custody grants on N slot(s) — recalled; waiting up to <bound> for the writers' releases before the slots are released`, a writer swept at the bound by the WARN `the leave's bound <bound> passed with N recalled writer(s) unreleased — swept at T_owner`, and a grant that survived by the ERROR `the clean leave keeps N slot(s) LEASED — a live custody grant on them survived the recall and the T_owner sweep; the next open recovers them as own residue` (`meta volume …: appender N keeps its leases at the leave … its page stays Live`); a healthy leave prints none of the three and takes one release round trip per recalled grant; the next open recovers them as own residue) rather than let the next lessee grant a file a writer still holds this holder's custody of — pinned (`a_holders_clean_leave_recalls_its_grants_before_its_slots_go_unleased`).
- **A dead holder** (review round 2, Issue 10 — the fence is SCOPED): the writer's lease at it runs the S9 law at its own `T_self` (strictly before the holder's TTL), but a slot holder's client fences ITS OWN custody, never the mount's — every grant from that holder is marked dead (the local handles read released; the next write re-acquires), the process custody generation ADVANCES (the S9 law for a lost lease: DMA in flight under those grants is refused at the device gate), that holder's token planes stop dead (no release travels to a holder that answers nothing; every cached entry dropped — PR 5's `T_self` law for a reader, scoped), and the arm forgets the holder (the next acquire re-dials, meeting the dead holder's refusal or PR 10's re-leased slot; the holder's dial slot is retired under its own mutex, so a dialer racing the fence re-looks the holder up instead of JOINing on an orphan — round 3, Issue 27), and the fence's observable latch is set LAST, after its work (Issue 27). The mount LIVES: nothing is poisoned, nothing parks, holder B's files keep their custody and fresh acquires against B land (pinned, the fence driven by the renewal cadence itself on a manual clock — `dlm_custody_holder_fences`, must-stay-0 on a healthy fleet; `dlm_custody_self_fences` untouched). The set authority's lease (the co-writer) keeps the shipped POISON. **What remains whole-mount**: the `advance`'s retire of DMA in flight under OTHER holders' grants at that instant — the write pipeline's ONE-word epoch carrier cannot tell which holder authorized a permit; the per-object capture is PR 12b's write-path arm (design row 12b; the S7 epoch's scope follows the arbiter). The S11 range-token cache is NOT cleared process-wide by the scoped fence (round 3, Issue 23): the fenced client's grants alone are marked dead, each retiring its own range span (`note_lease_lost`'s `clear_range_grants` stays the set authority's). The holder side — a dead holder's slots re-leased, its death recorded — is PR 10's recovery; until it, an acquire on the dead holder's files answers `EAGAIN` once per renewal beat.
- **W1 declines a foreign-custody file** (§5.4.3 law 2 from the custody path): a file whose custody this mount holds from its slot holder never patches in place — its blocks' references live in the holder's tree, which the durable probe cannot read (another process's RAM in production) — the CoW path, counted on its OWN label **`patch_ineligible_foreign_custody`** (review round 2, Issue 12 — `routing::SoleOwnerVerdict::ForeignCustody`; apart from `patch_ineligible_shared`, PR 7's clause, so the ledger names which clause declined); an own file keeps PR 7's durable probe. **The economy cost is stated**: every sub-block overwrite of a foreign-custody file pays a whole-block CoW rewrite — the random-small-write class W1 exists for (the pre-W1 ≈ 2,500× amplification on the rand-4k shape, `docs/design-random-small-writes.md`) — so growth on this label under a foreign-custody random-write workload is the priced cost, not predicate rot. The owed lever is a per-block SHARED / reference-count summary carried on the custody grant (the holder's tree answers it in the same round trip), or a probe verb at the holder — PR 12.
- **PR 7's owed no-re-Put law at the two backend-side translators**: the owner's compose of a shipped frame (`recompute_refs_against_map`) and the kvmap train's resolve now run `cancel_same_reference_pairs`, so a displaced and an adopted key resolving to ONE reference stage nothing (the record keeps its SHARED bit — C16) and, at the train, the block leaves the post-commit free stream — **a run's start re-adopted as a point at the same block was a LIVE re-description**: the base freed a block the record still held (pinned red-first on both).
- **The PK4 `pack_group` wire STAYS** (§5.4.3 adjudicated against the byte-identical law): the UNARMED co-writer packs through the authority with `pack_group` frames today (`tests/mw_cowriter_pack_tests.rs`, thirteen contracts); an ARMED symmetric mount never issues one (its promotions pack in its own `(meta volume, forest slot)` scope — PR 7; `pack_cowriter_frames` flat, pinned) — the wire retires with the co-writer posture at the default flip (PR 12/14), the D19/D20-arms precedent.
- **The clean leave** (review round 2, Issue 2): the unmount teardown disarms this plane where it disarms the others — right after the co-writer arm, BEFORE the membership leave (`data_grant::disarm_slot_custody`): for every dialed holder its carried tokens are RELEASED (the drain + purge before the holder is told — the token plane's one release path; a plane still inside its recall channel's first round releases too), then EVERY custody grant this mount still holds from it (the FUSE layer's cached leases are alive at unmount) is released and the verb drained, then the renewal loops stop — so the holder's `dlm_custody_held` and `dlm_token_outstanding` read 0 after the writer's unmount and its next commit on those files recalls nobody (pinned; the round-1 build left both to the holder's lease-expiry arm — the dead-client recall-deadline class PR 5 closed for readers). A writer that dies without the leave leaves its grants to each holder's lease-expiry arm (the S9 law). A re-arm replaces the previous arm and stops its loops (Issue 17).
- **The wire word `object` is screened before the arbiter sees it** (review round 2, Issue 3 — PR 3's bounded-execution law): `screen_custody_words` (pure, fuzzed by `token_call_frame`'s service-edge arm + the proptest mirror `custody_grant_screen_is_total_and_accepts_exactly_the_forest`) refuses a slot word past the forest codec's bound (what reached `split_guest_local`'s `debug_assert` — a service-lane panic — and, in release, truncated into an EXCLUSIVE arbiter lease on an ino the peer never named), a control record, a slot this forest never minted, a malformed span; then the durable inode record must exist AND be a REGULAR FILE — write custody is a file's word (WRITE / truncate / fallocate / a dirty handle's flush and fsync; a directory's mutations are the 4a guards'), so a striped directory, any of its stripe inos and the reserved-name markers' targets are REJECTED as custody objects (`custody_object_mode_admissible`, the PR 7b rebase's seam (c); pinned: a file INSIDE a striped directory is granted at its own slot holder, `D` and every `D_i` rejected on `dlm_token_custody_rejected`) (one leaf read). A screened frame is answered `TokenReply::Rejected` / `STATUS_REJECTED` (57) — nothing granted, registered or read — counted on the holder plane's **`dlm_token_custody_rejected`** (the buggy/hostile-peer class, must-stay-0; never a refusal gauge); the writer types it `Refused { EIO }`. Pinned: four screened words, the arbiter's `granted` unmoved.
- **A carried token spanning more than one grant page** (review round 2, Issue 4): a `CustodyGranted` whose xattr page did not end the set (`xattrs_complete == false`) is PAGED to completion from the holder before the install (the `xattr_after` continuation on the grant the holder already registered — `dlm_custody_token_carried_pages`), never installed as a complete token (the round-1 build did, and a `listxattr` off it misread the file); pinned with 40 × 15 KiB xattrs over the 512 KiB page budget. **A carried install that fails keeps the custody** (Issue 8 — `dlm_custody_token_carry_failures`): the caller adopts the lease it was granted, the holder holds exactly that grant, the next serve fetches the token.
- **A `NotHolder` answer re-resolves through tree 0** (Issue 15): the slot may have been handed to THIS mount under the acquire — the acquire answers `HolderAcquire::NowLocal` and the lock manager falls to its local arbiter; a second redirect refuses `EAGAIN`.
- **Gauges** (`.stats`): in the `dlm_custody` object — `dlm_custody_via_slot_holder`, `dlm_custody_token_carried`, `dlm_custody_token_carried_pages`, `dlm_custody_token_carry_failures` (≈ 0), `dlm_custody_holder_fences` (must-stay-0), `dlm_custody_recalls_absorbed` (the writer's absorbed handover recalls), `dlm_custody_recalled` (grants this process's authorities recalled for handovers), `dlm_custody_stale_holder_grants` (round 3 — grants answered by an appender tree 0 no longer names as the slot's holder: a request resolved before a move and served one RTT after it; released at once, re-acquired where tree 0 points; ≈ 0) — 0 unarmed and for every own file, pinned; in the Slot-lease family — `slot_handover_custody_deferrals`, `slot_handover_recall_bound_ms` (the mid-handover mark's bound in force: 2 × the custody authority's renewal beat, 0 unarmed); in the Token family — `dlm_token_custody_rejected` (must-stay-0); in the W1 ledger — `patch_ineligible_foreign_custody`. The other token-side faces (`dlm_token_grants_served`, `dlm_token_recalls`, the reader gauges of the writer's per-holder planes) are PR 5's.
- **Owed by the later rungs**: the writer's read path consuming the carried records on a foreign file (the `KvMetaBackend` read-verb divert exists on `-o ro` mounts only — PR 12b, design row 12b, where every writer is also a token client of the holders it writes to), the per-namespace WERO registrant key on the holder JOIN (LANDED in PR 12: the join carries the key rung 4 registered), the holder-side S9 owner on every armed writer (the mount path arms one under `SQUEEZEFS_MULTI_WRITER=1` today; the join ladder arms it on every writer), the S11 ranged acquire's token carriage (the ranged verb rides the holder's custody wire without records — a range holder reads under the S8 cache the S11 plane already rides), the un-share of a surviving sole owner (PR 12), the box bracket (gate 1 / PR 13-14).

### Symmetric metadata program — dead-appender recovery (PR 10, DARK)

PR 10 (2026-09-16, `.benchmarks/2026-09-16-sym-pr10-recovery.md`) lands **what happens when a node dies** on the armed plane — the death ledger's DRIVER, the §5.9 recovery, C14/C15 and the operator's `appender clear` — dark behind bit 17 AND `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`; **`=0` and a bit-17-absent volume are the shipped posture EXACTLY** (no ledger writer, no poll, no recovery, every gauge here 0 — pinned). Design §5.5.2 / §5.5.3 / §5.8.2 / §5.8.3 / §5.8.4 / §5.8.5 / §5.9 / §6.2 / §11.

- **What happens when a node dies.** Its membership lease expires at the home shard's owner (`T_owner`, 45 s shipped — the S6 plane, the ONE liveness law) and the owner EVICTS it. The eviction is the death ledger's production writer: the owner's death sink ships `RecordDeath { member, epoch, pr_key }` to volume 0's manager (in-process the same call — `dead_member:{node_token, mount_slot} → { epoch, ts, pr_key }` in tree 0 of volume 0, idempotent; the wire verb is served and SCREENED: a live member of the installed owner's census, or the manager's own identity, is `STATUS_REJECTED` on `manager_verb_rejected` — a stale or hostile frame never records a live node dead; **the KEY word is screened too** (review round 2): against the key the census REGISTERED for the member — kept through its departure — this process's own registrant keys and every live member's; a contradiction is `STATUS_REJECTED`, a key for a member no census here knows is DROPPED to 0 and the tail scan is the fence). **The record write is retried until durable**: the admission PARKS on a full ring, and a write that still fails (a device error) is parked in RAM and retried at the next ledger projection — `dead_member_write_deferrals` (**must stay 0** on a healthy manager). **Every manager polls volume 0's ledger as a projection** (`recovery_ledger_polls`; the cadence is the checkpoint landing ceiling — ≈ 1.1 s shipped, so propagation ≈ poll + ceiling ≈ 2 s, published live as `dead_member_propagation_ms`) and, for every `Live` / `Recovering` page of the dead identity on a volume it manages, runs the **§5.9 recovery** in order — **the decision taken UNDER the handover mutex on a fresh page read** (a page the poll snapshotted that moved meanwhile — another actor's act, a rejoin's newer term — is skipped; a member the installed owner lists LIVE again has its record RETIRED, never its region recovered): PREEMPT AND ABORT (RACQA 2 — the design's action) the dead node's registrant key on the metadata namespace and under every data-namespace WERO this process holds (PR substrate — `appender_recovery_preempts`; the fence that stops a zombie; the tail scan below runs unless the metadata preempt of a NON-ZERO key LANDED), page `Live → Recovering { recovered_by_term }` (both directory slots), read the ring past the page's `ledger_tail_seq` (the appender-violation classes judged on the window), **the RAM lease table marks the dead appender's slots `Releasing` — a first-touch acquire at the commit door is refused like a slot mid-handover, and the table goes `Unleased` only after tree 0's records are durable (the handover order; any failure in between aborts the release, leaves the page `Recovering`, and the re-run resumes from the durable state — a re-run past its tree-0 step treats the released slots' records as absorbed; a `Recovered` page whose `recovered:` record is missing has the record completed by the poll before the release law touches it)**, the recoverer's own cached nodes of each slot DROPPED and the page's newer root installed (its RAM tree of a foreign slot is stale at the root it opened with), two-phase replay into the dead appender's slot trees (+ the kind-4 data-bitmap deltas of every allocation lease homed here — PR 8's `replay_data_alloc_deltas`, folded onto the pages), flush every dirty leaf, **record the tails** (EVERY leaf of the slot trees on a non-PR substrate — the full tail scan, `recovery_full_tail_scan_bytes`, one extent read per non-resident leaf, never a materialized node; the resident leaves alone under a device fence, where the preempt is the fence), barrier, tree 0 `Unleased { root (post-flush), cursor per §5.1.8 (max of the recorded, the page's, the window's max minted local + 1 — never lowered), g, tails }` per slot with the grant record moving the trees' images out, the unclaimed grant RETURNED, the orphan images (claimed, reached by no root of the RELEASED slots' trees — walked without loading a leaf — named by no bitmap) returned, page `Recovered { ledger_tail_seq = head }`, `recovered:{X, v}` to volume 0 (idempotent — the record that GATES PR 8's allocation-lease succession), the parked `AcquireSlot`s woken, the dead manager's set-wide `dir_rename` lock released, and — when anything was recovered — the set's open cross-owner intents rolled forward (`recovery_intents_rolled_forward`: a dead holder's slot is the manager's now, so the step applies locally). Serial per manager, parallel across volumes; `appender_recovery_phase_ns` is exact-sum `preempt / read / replay / flush / tails / tree0 / total`, `appender_recoveries` counts regions, and `dead_members_acted ≡ dead_members_recorded × regions held` is the closure (3 volumes ⇒ 3 acts per record; 8 simultaneous deaths ⇒ 8 acts in one projection). **A `Recovered` ring is never replayed** (§5.8.3: whatever a non-PR zombie appended after the recoverer's head read is unreachable, and was never acked). Once no allocation lease names a `Recovered` region's extents the next projection RELEASES it — ring extents back to the heap, page `Free` with its id and term kept (`recovered_regions_released`).
- **Time to reclaim.** `T_owner` (the lease TTL) + propagation (≈ 2 s) + the recovery bound: **`appender_recovery_bound_ms`** (per volume, DERIVED — the largest ring a region of the volume can hold ÷ 230 B per entry, clustered at ≈ 800 files per 256 KiB leaf × 120 µs per cold leaf load, × THREE leaf passes (the flush's cold loads, the tail scan's extent reads, the orphan census's interior reads), + 1 µs of fold per entry, + one checkpoint landing ceiling — the ceiling resolved at OPEN, never an env read per stats serve; ≈ 1.31 s for a 32 MiB ring at 256 KiB nodes under the shipped cadence) — the number the measured `total` phase is read against. A **manager's** death adds the D0 ladder's wall: the successor wins the claim (the dead manager's `Live` page 0 adopted with the role, its fixed ring replayed — PR 3), opens GRACE for its home shard, and only the members that did NOT reclaim in grace are recorded dead when grace ends (`the_owners_eviction_and_the_grace_deadline_write_the_ledger_never_a_reclaimer`) — a reclaiming appender's `Live` page is LIVE, `g` unchanged, never recovered; a parked member's reclaim venue is the successor the home volume's rendezvous record names (an owner at a newer era than the one it joined under — `membership::observe_successor`, read at every parked beat). A manager that cannot read volume 0's ledger for > `T_owner` RELEASES its role and stops refreshing its writer claim; the claim ages past the TTL and the D0 ladder re-elects a successor that can (PR 10's successor ladder IS the D0 ladder).
- **A `Recovering` page of YOUR OWN node** (a recoverer died inside your predecessor's recovery, or your own `appender clear`) is own residue: the next mount of that node replays the ring exactly as it would a `Live` one (the partial recovery folded the same records, idempotent by seq) and joins over it. **A foreign `Live`/`Recovering` page whose id the declared test partition claims** is the one join refusal left (the seam would stand a ring up over a joined appender's page or race its recoverer).
- **`squeezefs appender clear <sqmeta-uri> <appender_id> [--volume N]`** — the attested OFFLINE remedy for a death the membership plane never recorded (the plane was off, or the owner died with it): the `claim clear` law for an appender page. It takes the D0 flock on the volume and on volume 0 and REFUSES loud on a live-mounted volume, **a heartbeat-fresh `writer_claim` on ANY volume it touches (the volume's or volume 0's — a live manager anywhere in the set, this host's or a foreign one; read from a probe before anything of the verb's is opened; a KILLED writer's claim stays fresh for the TTL, so the verb waits the TTL out exactly like `claim clear`)**, a fresh `client:` heartbeat of the page's node (it may be alive), this node's OWN residue (your next mount recovers it itself), and — since PR 12b's review round 1 (Issue 4) — **a JOINED appender's page inside `T_park_max` of the manager's claim going stale**: a joined writer writes no `client:` heartbeat (PR 13), and on a manager-less set a live one may be PARKED at `T_self` reclaiming against the successor for up to `T_park_max` (derived — `manager_failover_bound_ms` + the membership grace; past it the parked writer fences itself, `appender_park_expiries`), so the verb refuses inside that window naming the bound (mount the successor instead: its death ledger recovers what is dead and never needs the guess); otherwise it writes `dead_member:` to volume 0's ledger (epoch 0, no registrant key — attest only what you know — and the EARLY recorder class) and marks the page `Recovering` in both directory slots; the next writer mount's gate recovers the window BEFORE it serves. **The custody quarantine (Issue 34)**: the verb is an EARLY recorder — unlike the plane's own (the S6 eviction at `T_owner`, after which every writer holding custody from the dead holder has self-fenced at `T_self < T_owner`), it can run inside a surviving writer's `T_self` — so the recovery refuses FRESH custody grants on the recovered slots' files until `T_owner + 2 × skew_max` has elapsed since the record (the OWNER-side bound — the instant past which a custody lease may be re-granted, plus the recorder's wall-clock skew against the manager's; `slot_custody_quarantine_refusals`, the retryable `EAGAIN` / `CUSTODY_DEFERRED` class naming the quarantine; ≈ 45 s at the shipped S9 clocks) — a write into one of those files inside that window BLOCKS on the POSIX-5 ladder, never fails; the quarantine is DURABLE (a `custody_quarantine:` record in the volume's tree 0, re-derived at the next mount's gate), so a manager restart inside the window keeps it; PR 8's same-node takeover writes the same class. Idempotent (`Cleared { was: Recovering }` the second time); audited on `appender_clear_runs`. It is also **C14's remedy**.
- **fsck C14 — slot custody conflict** (report-only, **`fsck_slot_custody_conflicts` must stay 0**): one forest slot attested `Live` on two appenders' pages at tree 0's generation, or `Live` on a page while tree 0 leases it to another appender at that generation — a shape no grant or release writes (a stale entry below tree 0's `g` is residue, never a conflict). **The mount REFUSES on it** naming the verb; fsck reports the pair once per slot and plans `report-only` — which page is dead is the operator's attestation, `appender clear` on the dead one, after which the next mount recovers the slot and drops the forged entry. **C15 — un-recovered appender** (`fsck_unrecovered_appenders`, 0 once every ledgered death has been acted on): a `Live` / `Recovering` page whose identity volume 0's ledger names with acked entries in its ring window or slots still leased to it; the plan is `recover-dead-appender` — applied ONLINE by the volume's manager (`fsck_repair_classC15`), refused offline naming the mount path (a probe holds no ring and no lease to recover under; the next writer mount's gate recovers before serving, as does the live manager's next poll). **The inode plane (C9/C10) SCOPES OUT the inos a foreign appender's un-replayed ring window names** (`fsck_inode_plane_window_scoped` — each foreign ring read once per census) and judges every other — a cross-owner create's dentry rides the parent holder's ring while the child's record is the creator's, so the referenced set is incomplete over exactly those inos and complete over the rest; a live fleet is never skipped whole (an unreadable ring = no verdict); C15 names a dead window, its recovery completes the set. **On an N-writer set the DENTRY pass takes NO verdict over a volume with a slot LEASED to another LIVE appender** (PR 12b review round 1, Issue 1 — `fsck_inode_plane_foreign_dentry_scoped`, one per volume per census; LIVE = the installed S6 owner lists the lessee's member id inside `T_owner` — a lessee NOT known live is PR 10's frozen-tree class above: judged, its window scoped; **the raw C1 walk takes the same law** — PR 12b round 4: a live lessee's slot trees are skipped, counted `fsck_c1_projection_slots_scoped`, because the lessee moves their roots and rewrites their images under grants the manager handed out, so the manager's raw walk from ITS root word read a routing loop (`root-seq` restarts to the traversal budget) and reported `C1Torn` over a healthy tree): that slot's tree is a PROJECTION at the censusing mount whose staleness the census cannot bound (the `sym-storm` leg's 442 false C10 "dangling" names were exactly the children of slots a live joiner had just released — read fresh — named by a directory tree it still leased — read stale); the inode side stays lessee-scoped as before, so `fsck_inode_plane_volumes_covered` excludes such a volume and the finding classes there read 0 by construction, never by luck. The verdict returns once every foreign lessee has left (or the census runs at each lessee for its own slots — PR 13's shipped census). The `sym-storm` / `sym-crash` legs snapshot every daemon's `.stats` BEFORE the fsck verdict, run the fsck under a hard bound (`HUNG` is a red, never a stall) and assert the "deleted stays deleted" arm through every RW mount. **C6 on a grant-armed allocator runs the BITMAP ORACLE** (PR 8's `DataAllocBitmap::drift` at fsck time): the allocation bitmap against the census's tracked population with the holder's open grants and the in-flight registry as the live-owner shield — LOSS (referenced ∧ clear) is the C6 finding, LEAK candidates are counted on `fsck_alloc_bitmap_leak_candidates` (the (re-)hold releases them); the single-writer `highest − free_list` arithmetic is never run there.
- **A `-o ro` reader, a probe, a co-writer and a peer-owned open SCREEN frames off tree 0** (§5.8.2, both loaders): with no lease plane to answer for a slot, `slot_state`'s `(g, lessee)` and `slot_tails:{s}` are the screen's inputs, cached per slot against the control tree's root — before it, offline fsck's raw C1 walk folded a zombie's frame every writer screened and reported it as damage on a volume a writer reads clean (the non-PR zombie contract found it: `a_zombie_frame_on_an_untouched_leaf_is_screened`). **A non-writer applies rules 2 and 3 ONLY** (the recorded tails, the in-log order): its tree 0 lags the writer's by the S5 poll, so a frame the current lessee wrote at `g + 1` since the reader's last resync would land on rule 1 — the breach class — and rule 4 off a stale lessee; **on a reader, `appender_fence_breach` / `foreign_frame_overwrite_detected` therefore never move, and `foreign_frames_screened` counts rule-2/3 frames alone**; the generation-comparing rules are the writer's, whose lease table is the truth.
- **A crashed incarnation's block-grant window is RELEASED at the re-hold** (found by the `sym-crash` leg's first round): the grant window is RAM, so a kill leaves its granted-but-unminted tail and its minted-but-unpublished blocks SET in the allocation bitmap with no reference and no open grant — the bitmap oracle's LEAK half (PR 8's `DataAllocBitmap::drift`, stated but not wired). The symmetric allocation arm now CLEARS every such bit at the (re-)hold with its delta journaled in order (`data_alloc_bitmap_leaks_released` — 0 on a fleet that never crashed, ≈ the window remainder per crash), and fsck's **C6 runs the bitmap oracle on a grant-armed allocator** (the `highest − free_list` arithmetic is single-writer and the bitmap IS the free list there — one crash read as a 386-vs-385 finding; the first build DECLINED, losing the fsck-time census — restored in review round 2 as the oracle, above); a grant-armed allocator whose holding this process does not keep (a wire writer's) still declines on `fsck_foreign_lane_exempted`. **The LEAK release at the hold is sound only while no PEER holds a grant window on the same volume** (block grants are not term-bound): since PR 12b's review round 1 (Issue 2) the (re-)hold DEFERS it while any other appender page of the home volume is `Live` — a live joiner's window remainder is RAM at the joiner and reads exactly like a dead incarnation's, and clearing it re-carved blocks the joiner was still minting from (a double allocation, pinned) — counting the candidates on `data_alloc_bitmap_leaks_deferred` (a bounded leak: at most one window remainder per writer per failover, fsck C6's `fsck_alloc_bitmap_leak_candidates` names them; released by the next re-hold with no live peer). A dead writer's minted-but-unpublished blocks on a NON-PR substrate bypass S7's quarantine by this release (the zombie's later DMA into a re-granted block is the detection-grade class the D0 row already states).
- **Gauges** (`.stats`): the Recovery family — `appender_recoveries`, `appender_recovery_phase_ns` (exact-sum `preempt / read / replay / flush / tails / tree0 / total`), `appender_recovery_bound_ms` (per volume, derived), `recovery_full_tail_scan_bytes`, `appender_recovery_preempts`, `recovered_regions_released`, `appender_clear_runs`, `recovery_ledger_polls`, `recovery_intents_rolled_forward`, `data_alloc_bitmap_leaks_released`, `data_alloc_bitmap_leaks_deferred` (leak candidates left SET at a re-hold beside a LIVE peer — PR 12b), `dead_member_write_deferrals` (**must stay 0**), `dead_member_records_retired` (a rejoin's or the sweep's retirements), `dead_members_quarantined` (the record's arm on an allocation HOLDER — split off `dead_members_acted`, whose closure is per region recovered); fsck's `fsck_slot_custody_conflicts` (**must stay 0**), `fsck_unrecovered_appenders`, `fsck_repair_classC15`, `fsck_inode_plane_window_scoped`, `fsck_inode_plane_foreign_dentry_scoped` / `fsck_c1_projection_slots_scoped` (PR 12b — the dentry pass's per-volume no-verdict and the raw C1 walk's skipped slot trees under a LIVE lessee), `fsck_alloc_bitmap_leak_candidates`; PR 8's `dead_members_recorded` / `dead_members_acted` / `recovered_records` / `dead_member_propagation_ms` now driven by the plane. All 0 on every unarmed or bit-17-absent mount, pinned (`a_flat_volume_and_an_unarmed_forest_arm_no_recovery_driver`).
- **The fleet leg** (LOCAL, the tcp devsub): `sudo tests/mw_fleet.sh create N=2 --symmetric --lease-ttl-ms=15000` (bit 17 + the armed manager; implies `--membership`) then `sudo tests/run_mw_matrix.sh sym-crash [--rounds=10]` — kill -9 the manager ×N under load; every round asserts **the acked-writes ORACLE** (a client's ledger of names whose per-file `fsync(2)` RETURNED before the kill — `dd conv=fsync`, never `sync -f`: that is `syncfs(2)`, which the FUSE fork does not serve, so the kernel pushes writeback and returns with no daemon barrier behind it; every ledgered name must resolve on the successor with its content, and a lost or altered name is the row's RED), the successor's own-residue recovery, the manager lease `held`, every symmetric must-stay-0 gauge 0 and the online fsck (C14/C15 riding it) clean. A FOREIGN appender's recovery over the wire needs N daemons on one volume — PR 12b's venue (design row 12b); the in-process matrix is `tests/sym_crash_matrix_tests.rs`.
- **A dead INITIATOR's open cross-owner intent** (PR 6's owed item, the other half): whoever recovers the initiator's ring adopts its intents — the manager's poll rolls every open intent forward after any recovery (`recovery_intents_rolled_forward`), and a dead initiator's intent at ino 0 of its slot is in the ring the recovery replays, so it is covered by the same arm; the row is pinnable only on PR 12b's many-writer venue — design row 12b (in-process the initiator IS the manager).
- **The in-process fixture for another daemon** (review round 2): the crash matrix's `two_backends` stands the recoverer up as a DIFFERENT `KvMetaBackend` than the dead lessee — the lessee's region image (its ring, its page slots, its grant's extents) captured at two checkpoints and re-applied under a foreign identity while the recoverer is open, so its RAM tree of the slot is stale against the device exactly as another node's would be. It found the round-1 defects the one-backend fixtures could not reach, and a shipped one beside them: a PROBE's `shutdown` ran a checkpoint (a ledger record and a tree-0 publication written by a mount holding no lock) — every non-writer door now writes nothing at teardown.
- **Owed by the later rungs**: N daemon processes on one volume and the wire death path end to end (PR 12b — design row 12b; the ledger poll, the preempt and the verbs are contract-proven in-process and on the wire's own listener), **the cross-shard rejoin's retirement** (PR 12b: a member homed on another shard that rejoins as a non-manager appender is retired by none of the three retirement arms today — its fresh `Live` page matches the standing `dead_member:` record for up to `2 × T_owner` and a manager's poll would recover the live rejoiner's region while it writes; the join ladder must retire the record at volume 0's manager before the page goes `Live` and carry the join epoch on the page — design row 10), the busy-appender-across-a-manager-failover row (PR 12b's venue), PR 2's drain-then-grow, the SIM-1 death/recovery leg at 12,500 × 64 (PR 13), the box bracket (PR 13/14).

### Symmetric metadata program — the mount posture: every RW mount is a writer (PR 12, DARK)

PR 12 (2026-09-16, `.benchmarks/2026-09-16-sym-pr12-mount-posture.md`; `src/sym_join.rs`) lands **ONE mount shape** on an armed set — dark behind bit 17 AND `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`; **`=0` and a bit-17-absent volume are the shipped postures EXACTLY** (every row above them in this document verbatim, pinned). Design §7.3 / §6.1 / §6.2 / §5.1.6 / §5.7.2 / §11.

- **The join ladder.** A plain `mount` of an armed set walks: **1 declaration** — `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1` with NONE of the retired posture knobs beside it (below); **2 bits** — every volume carries the whole multi-writer class (7/9/10/11/13/14/15/16) AND 17, else the refusal names the volume, the bit and the act that stamps it (`enable-symmetric` / `format --symmetric` / `enable-multi-writer`); **3 membership** — the mount is a member of its home shard; the manager is the shard's S6 owner, and the ladder ARMS the plane at `auto` when `SQUEEZEFS_MEMBERSHIP_BIND` is unset (an explicit bind wins verbatim; an explicit `off` is refused — the death ledger's writer is the S6 eviction); **4 registrant** — WERO on the METADATA namespaces (PR 3's `meta_pr_wero`, taken at the open) AND the DATA namespaces (S7's standing hold, joined here; the registrant cap probed — PR 3), or, on a substrate without reservations, the DETECTION-GRADE lab posture under `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_ALLOW_NON_PR=1` (announced in the report, refused without it); **5 `JoinAppender`** and **6 `AcquireSlots`** — the open's (PR 2/4); **7 the planes** — the custody owner, the publish / meta / manager / token services on ONE listener (`SQUEEZEFS_MW_BIND`: `auto` = D2's posture, an addr:port, never `off`), the ownership plane and the cadence — what a declared multi-writer authority used to arm and what a solo mount never stood up — and the writer's listener PUBLISHED into its own claim-set entry on every volume, the durable binding every mount resolves an appender's endpoint through (page identity → member id → the entry's endpoint). Every refusal names its rung. **There are no roles**: `mount_posture` reads `writer` on every RW mount; `manager_lease` / `alloc_lease` / `slot_leases_held` / `membership_mode` say what the writer HOLDS; `symmetric_join` publishes the rungs walked, the data namespaces registered, the detection-grade word and the listener. Gate 1's law holds by construction: a solo armed writer's ownership map is all-local, `dlm_rpcs == 0`.
- **Retired forward-only — refusing, not yet deleted.** On an armed process `SQUEEZEFS_MULTI_WRITER` (at an ON spelling — `0` / `false` / `no` / `off` is the ABSENT knob by the ENG-10 law and is admitted, the refusal saying so), `SQUEEZEFS_MW_ROLE`, `SQUEEZEFS_MW_AUTHORITY` and `SQUEEZEFS_MW_MEMBERS` are RETIRED spellings (the `Kind::Enum { retired }` law PR 16 introduced, applied beside `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`): the startup gate refuses the process naming every offender and its successor — the plane — in the registry's own form. `squeezefs volume set-owners` is refused on a bit-17 set (ownership is a slot LEASE — first-writer-takes-it, handed over, recovered; `get-owners` / `locate` still read the set). On an UNARMED mount every one of them keeps its shipped meaning until the PR-14 flip deletes the postures (the co-writer, set-authority and partial-authority rows above are the flat recipe's, unreachable on an armed set by construction). `SQUEEZEFS_MW_BIND` is NOT retired: every writer serves, and the bind is where.
- **`-o ro` = member-reader + token client, bound PER SLOT HOLDER.** A read-only mount of an armed set resolves every object's HOLDER through its own tree 0 (§5.1.6): an object in a slot the manager holds (or nobody does — the manager maintains every unleased tree, KD-SYM-2/3) is served by the manager's plane; an object in a slot another appender leases is served by a plane dialed to THAT holder's published listener at the first resolve (one plane per LISTENER — a holder the manager's daemon also serves rides the manager's plane and its one recall channel). An object whose holder has published no endpoint REFUSES loud (`EAGAIN`, naming the holder; `dlm_token_reader_unbound_holders`) — never the bounded-staleness projection (KD-SYM-19). The reader dials no declared authority: the manager's endpoint is what its join ladder published. `reader_staleness_bound_ms` stays 0.
- **The ownership-accounting gate keys on the held LEASE.** On a grant-armed allocator (the armed writer's fresh mint, PR 8) `plane_gate` passes iff this mount holds the data volume's ALLOCATION LEASE (or runs the holder's own served-free act) — the bitmap, the terminal free, the grace ring and the quarantine live with the holder, a non-holder's frees SHIP to it (`block_grant::free_target_for`); `alloc_plane_gate` passes on the grant window. W1, the recovery walk, reclaim and quarantine therefore run on every mount for what it holds; a refusal names the lease and the volume (`cowriter_accounting_refusals`, the same class one lease finer). Every unarmed allocator takes the posture-word arms verbatim.
- **Two PR-7/PR-4 windows closed.** The served `MarkShared` runs UNDER the source file's block guard (`BLOCK_FLUSH_LOCKS(global ino, block index)` — the guard the local W1 patch site holds from its predicate through its DMA), so a foreign cloner's mark lands wholly before a patch's fenced mark load or wholly after its DMA (`served_mark_shared_under_patch` stays as the must-stay-0 belt). The manager rewrites a wire joiner's page ONLY while no checkpoint of the joiner's own has written it (`ckpt_seq == 0`) — one writer per page — so an appender's own checkpoint's `head_hint` / `seq_offset` / `ckpt_seq` are never regressed and the release screen's DERIVED `seq_floor` bound is tight (a > 2-lap release lands where the join-time hint would have refused it).
- **Found on the real PR target (the fidelity tier's `sym-join-ladder` leg, quick tier).** (a) The knob-armed manager held the shipped rtype 1 on its METADATA namespace — the WERO posture (PR 3) keyed on the declared test partition alone, so no second host's appender could ever have registered to write its own ring; the product shape now holds rtype 3 (pinned red-first). (b) **A shipped S9 defect, every layout**: a clean unmount of a `SQUEEZEFS_MULTI_WRITER=1` authority (and of the ladder's writer, which shares the arm) left the mount's OWN registrant on every data namespace — the custody sweep held a clone of the WERO hold across its 10 s cadence, and the release ioctl runs in the hold's drop, which died with the process. The sweep now observes the hold weakly and the disarm's drop is the release; an operator who saw `nvme resv-report` list a departed writer's key after a clean unmount was seeing this (the next mount's register ladder recovered it as own-stale — no fence was lost, the residue was the symptom). The leg's law is zero residue on BOTH namespaces after the clean leave, traced by key at every phase.
- **What this rung did NOT contain — PR 12b, the MANY-writer posture (LANDED 2026-09-17, the section below).** N RW daemons on one set, **N unbounded by design** (the owner's ruling): every non-manager RW daemon — the wire joiner whose `JoinAppender` / `AcquireSlots` travel to a manager in another process and whose backend commits into a region it does not manage: its own ring, its own page writes and checkpoint task, its own listener, tree 0 as a projection, the third gate state — joins as a full writer; nothing bounds N (per-writer pages + rings with no ledger bound, per-slot leases over 2^16 slots × 64 rotors, membership sharded by home volume — 12.5 k writers per shard by the design's arithmetic; 15 k is the target). "The second daemon" is only the first pin. **Design PR-plan row 12b**, serial after PR 12; the ladder's rungs 5–6 over the wire still meet the D0 gate's `FreshForeign` refusal, which on an armed process NAMES the posture ("symmetric plane armed: a second or later RW mount joins as a WRITER once PR 12b — the many-writer posture, N unbounded by design — lands; until then the D0 guard applies to RW mounts and `-o ro` readers join as token clients"). The writer side of the census binding this rung DID land: rung 7 installs the cross-owner step shipper and binds every appender Live at its join off their published endpoints (a later joiner's arrives with PR 12b's wire `JoinAppender`). With it stay: appender homes ≠ volume 0 and two membership shards on a 2-volume set, the wire holder's flush-then-transfer on a recall notice, the `shared_ref:` migration at a home change, the allocation bitmap replacing the by-block scan as the terminal-free engine (zero census reads at open), the writer's read divert to its per-holder token planes, the remote holder's `IsEmpty` under the striped rmdir's scope, PR 9's per-object epoch capture and the owner-side grace at the production arm, PR 10's cross-shard rejoin retirement, PR 2's drain-then-grow, PR 7's un-share of a surviving sole owner (it needs the "share in flight" witness across the owner and the index home). The note's seam ledger names each with its reason.

### Symmetric metadata program — the N-daemon posture: every non-manager RW mount is a full writer (PR 12b, DARK)

PR 12b (2026-09-17, `.benchmarks/2026-09-17-sym-pr12b-n-daemon.md`; `src/meta_backend/kv/backend/joined.rs`, `sym_join::arm_joined`) lands the piece PR 12 left standing — dark behind bit 17 AND `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1`; **`=0` and a bit-17-absent volume are the shipped postures EXACTLY** (a plain mount's open is the shipped one verbatim — `symmetric_join_target` answers nothing without the knob). Design §7.3 / §5.1 / §5.3 / §5.9 / §11, PR-plan row 12b. **N is unbounded by design**: every non-manager RW daemon on a volume joins as a full writer; "the second daemon" is the first pin, the third the second.

- **The join ladder's terminal decision.** Under the plane a plain `mount` of a set with a LIVE manager — a local holder of the writer lock on this host, or a heartbeat-fresh `writer_claim` of another host's writer — JOINS it instead of claiming: rungs 5–6 over the wire (`JoinAppender` → a region of its own, `AcquireSlots` → its rotor), rung 3 as a WRITER MEMBER of the manager's membership shard (the manager is the S6 owner; a joiner is never a second owner), rung 4 the same registrant join, rung 7 the same planes on ONE listener (its slots' tokens, custody, shipped steps — never the manager verbs, which are page 0's daemon's) with the listener PUBLISHED through the manager (`PublishEndpoint` → the joiner's claim-set entry on every volume + the manager's holder table — every mount resolves it the PR-12 way). A set with no live manager: the D0 ladder runs and this mount becomes the manager; two mounts racing for an unclaimed set: the D0 loser re-reads the target and joins the winner. `mount_posture` reads `writer` on the joiner too; `manager_lease` reads `peer:<node>`, `writer_guard_mode` reads `joined-appender` (its own guarantee row: no lock, no claim, no metadata reservation of its own — a REGISTRANT under the manager's WERO on PR substrates), `appenders_live` 1, `appender_id ≠ 0`.
- **What a joined appender owns and what it never touches.** Its ring, its page (written by ITS checkpoint alone from the first instant — `ckpt_seq ≥ 1` at the join, so the manager's slot-only page rewrites yield), its slot trees (RAM-authoritative for the slots it leases), its extent grant (refilled over the wire — the page's grant word is kept current around every ask), its listener, its custody owner and token holder. Tree 0, the ledger, the bitmap, page 0 and every manager verb are the manager's: a control write reaching a joiner refuses loud and counts on **`joined_control_refusals` (must stay 0)**. Its commit door: an own slot passes, an unleased slot is first-touched over the wire (`AcquireSlot`), a slot another appender holds refuses `EAGAIN` (`slot_door_refusals` — "ship to the holder": the cross-owner op ships its step there, PR 6). Its reads of a slot it does not hold are TOKEN reads at that slot's holder (the manager for an unleased slot) through PR 9's per-holder planes — exact at the next resolve, never a stale projection. Its terminal frees SHIP to the data volume's allocation holder (the manager in this rung's one-shard shape) and its fresh blocks mint from that holder's ranged grants over the wire — a joined open performs ZERO by-block census reads. Its ring never grows (`joined_ring_grow_declined`; it drains at `admissible ÷ cadence` per tick — PR 2's bound). **Across a manager failover** a live joiner keeps writing into its own ring; its next manager verb fails once at the dead listener, re-dials the successor's (resolved off the projection — page 0's identity → its claim-set entry) and lands; its membership lease re-asserts against the successor the rendezvous names at the cadence law, its holder plane toward appender 0 re-points, and its reads of the manager's slot resume once the successor arms (`joined_wire_redials` counts each). It acknowledges every freed-offset label its renewal grant carries at once — its foreign bindings are token-governed — so it never holds the manager's freed offsets in the grace ring. **N joined writers on one box**: the fleet rig mounts them with `mw_fleet.sh create N=2 --symmetric --writers=K` (`SQZ_MWFLEET_OSS_GB=16` for the kill legs' 16 GiB load); each daemon registers the shipped transport geometry (32 × 32 × 1 MiB kmbuf rings), so the rig compacts memory before each joined mount.
- **Death and return.** A joiner killed −9 that mounts again at the SAME mount point presents its `Live` page's identity, the manager answers `already`, and its ring replays as own residue (the covered AND the windowed records; its grant pre-sized over the wire before the replay mints). A joiner named dead by the ledger (the S6 eviction of its membership lease, or the operator's `squeezefs appender clear`) is recovered by the manager's poll exactly as PR 10 says; a third daemon then takes its slots at `g + 1`. **A same-node page is own residue on PAGE 0 alone**: the manager's remount adopts its own page 0 (the flock holder's) and lists every joiner's page as another daemon's — alive or dead, the ledger decides; PR 2's "any same-node page" law would have adopted a live joiner's ring. A joiner's clean unmount hands every slot back over the wire (flush-then-transfer to nobody: the released root exact at the manager) and `LeaveAppender` frees its page and returns its ring and remainder to the heap.
- **The transfer's token half.** A slot that moves — a wire grant, a handover, a release — RECALLS every token the departing holder granted on its objects and waits for the acks before the move completes; the new lessee's later commits recall at its own plane. A recall notice the manager carries on a joiner's renewal grant (`slot_release_notices`, PR 4) makes the joiner run the same flush-then-transfer; an offer standing for it (`offered_slots`) is taken with `AcquireSlot`.
- **Gauges** (`.stats`, per volume; `null` on the manager and on every unarmed mount): the Joined family — `joined_appender_id`, `joined_manager_endpoint`, `joined_wire_verbs`, `joined_wire_acquires`, `joined_wire_releases`, `joined_wire_extent_grants`, `joined_wire_extent_returns`, `joined_wire_failures` (a wire verb that could not complete — the caller's retry class), `joined_wire_redials` (a transport-class failure absorbed by re-dialing the manager's CURRENT listener off durable state and running the idempotent verb once more — one per manager failover a live joiner rode through; never a failure), `joined_control_refusals` (**must stay 0** — a control write reached a joined appender: a site that assumed "this mount is the manager"), `joined_ring_grow_declined`, `joined_registrant_posture` (`adopted` — a co-located joiner shares the manager's PR association and registered nothing, KD-SYM-22; `registrant` — a remote joiner registered under the set's one key on both namespace classes; `detection` — a non-PR substrate under `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_ALLOW_NON_PR=1`), `joined_meta_hold_standing` (rung 4's metadata-namespace hold is standing — 1 for the mount's life; the leave drops it last). Process-wide beside them: `sym_holder_binds_on_demand` (holders bound at their first foreign act — appenders that joined after this mount's ladder ran, and a holder that MOVED its listener: a successor at the same identity), `dlm_slot_lease_foreign_reads` (fencing reads of a lease-foreign object with no live custody, served the owner era's base — a joiner reading the manager's directory; never the S8 miss tripwire), `membership_reclaim_refusals` (the member's re-asserted reclaims a venue refused before `T_self` — see the membership fields). Beside them `writer_guard_mode == joined-appender`, `manager_lease == peer:<node>`, `appenders_live == 1`, `appender_id ≠ 0`, `mount_posture == writer`; the manager's `appenders_known` counts the joiners' Live pages and its `leaves` their clean departures.
- **The two-PROCESS legs ran** (the note's §6): the fidelity tier's `sym_n_daemon_leg` — three real daemons on kernel nvmet, quick tier PASS=118 — and the fleet's `sym-crash` / `sym-storm` (a manager + a `-o ro` reader + THREE joined writers, `mw_fleet.sh create N=2 --symmetric --writers=3`, the per-file-fsync acked-writes oracle, `sym-crash` 5/5 green); rows 16(a)–(n) of the note are what they found, each fixed red-first at its venue. **Not in this rung — PR 13 / PR 14.** A second membership SHARD (a home ≠ volume 0, the `shared_ref:` migration, the cross-shard rejoin retirement); the counted box rows; the MANAGER's zero-census open (PR 14's terminal-free engine swap); PR 7's un-share (PR 14). The note's §2 ledger names every item with its reason. **Known gaps with a bound (review round 1, Issue 4):** a joined writer writes no durable `client:` heartbeat (PR 13), so on a MANAGER-LESS set `squeezefs appender clear` cannot tell a live PARKED joiner from a dead one — the verb REFUSES inside `T_park_max` of the manager's claim going stale (a parked joiner fences itself past it: `appender_park_expiries`), and the successor's mount path (its death ledger) is the remedy that never needs the guess; and the successor's S9 custody owner opens NO grace window at the production arm (PR 9's owner-side grace, PR 13), so a recovered slot's file can be granted afresh up to ONE renewal beat (`membership_renew_cadence_ms`) before the old writer learns its lease is unknown — the DMA of that beat is still fenced by the custody epoch (`data_dma_epoch_refusals`) on a PR substrate.

### FUSE transport in-flight concurrency (defaults are the L1 policy; knobs are overrides)

Random-4k iodepth workloads are gated by two multiplicative kernel-side limits: the FUSE-over-io_uring per-queue ring depth and the INIT-negotiated `max_background`. Opening both measured **44k → 316k IOPS (7.2×, device-true)** on `elbencho --rand -t 16 -b 4k --iodepth 16 --direct` (`.benchmarks/2026-07-15-iops-parity-decomposition.md`); since L1 that class is the **default** — no knobs required:

- **Per-queue depth** (`SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_OVER_IO_URING_Q_DEPTH`, clamp 1..32): default is **32 degraded to the payload-buffer cap** — `mem-budget/8`, no fixed ceiling since the 2026-08-04 derivation sweep (`SQUEEZEFS_TRANSPORT_MEM_MAX` MiB absolute / `SQUEEZEFS_TRANSPORT_MEM_PCT` override it; the pinned-arena bound is the geometry's structural demand cap queues × 32 × payload) — with floor 4 (the pre-L1 posture — small-RAM boxes keep yesterday's footprint). An explicit value wins verbatim over the cap. Payload arenas cost `queues × depth × ~1 MiB` of registered anon memory — gauged as `transport_payload_buffer_bytes` in `.stats` and attributed to the memory budget as the `transport_payload_buffers` component.
- **Queues** (`SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_OVER_IO_URING_QUEUES`, testing only): pinned to kernel **possible CPUs** — registering fewer never becomes ready (kernel readiness requirement).
- **`-o max_background=N` / `-o congestion_threshold=N`**: INIT-reply overrides; defaults `clamp(queues × depth, 64, u16::MAX)` (the delivered ring capacity — the fixed 256 ceiling was retired by the 2026-08-04 derivation sweep; `-o max_background=256` is the A0 lever restoring the old posture) and ¾ of it. Also runtime-writable per live connection via fusectl: `echo 256 | sudo tee /sys/fs/fuse/connections/<minor>/max_background`.
- **FIND-L1-A (≥ 13-writer O_DIRECT convoy): FIXED 2026-07-17.** The convoy was a write-path completion-trigger defect (one write's end as a proxy for block completeness — kernel-split out-of-order WRITE segments misfired it), not a transport trade; the coverage-union trigger cured it (`t16` default/mb12 = 1.006–1.026, t16 ≥ 1.10× t8, both cells *rose*). No `max_background` throttle is needed or recommended anymore. Forensics + fix: `.benchmarks/2026-07-17-rw3-find-l1a-forensics.md`, `.benchmarks/2026-07-17-rw3b-write-through-coverage-fix.md`.

### FUSE max_write geometry (block-size writes; sysctl-gated)

The INIT `max_write`/`max_pages` pair is **negotiated per mount** (2026-08-04 geometry campaign; `.benchmarks/2026-08-04-fuse3-zc-adoption.md`): the daemon desires the **volume block size** (floored at the historical 1 MiB), and the fuse3 transport gates that desire by the kernel's `fs.fuse.max_pages_limit` sysctl and the payload budget, then advertises `max_pages = ceil(max_write/page)` — exactly what the plan stands on, so the kernel's fuse-over-uring REGISTER bound (`ring->max_payload_sz`) always equals the registered ents. Negotiated values are gauged in `.stats` as `transport_max_write` / `transport_max_pages`.

- **sqz-host posture: `fs.fuse.max_pages_limit=1024`** (`sysctl -w fs.fuse.max_pages_limit=1024`, persist in `/etc/sysctl.d/90-squeezefs.conf`). With the default 4 MiB block size a whole block then arrives as **one** FUSE_WRITE / one payload lease / one merge instead of 4 kernel-split 1 MiB segments — per-request fixed costs (dispatch, lock discipline, commit batching, wake economy) quarter on ≥ block-size sequential shapes. Reads scale the same way (readahead gathers to `max_pages`).
- **Fleet kernels without the sysctl (or at the 256 default) stay at their advertised cap gracefully**: negotiation lands on 1 MiB — byte-identical to the pre-campaign shape, and the mount always succeeds (pre-fix, a raised sysctl made every REGISTER refuse and the mount **fail**; the geometry law makes that structurally impossible).
- **Payload-arena cost scales with the negotiated ent size**: `queues × depth × max_write` registered anon bytes under the `mem-budget/8` cap (no fixed ceiling since the 2026-08-04 derivation sweep; `SQUEEZEFS_TRANSPORT_MEM_MAX`/`_PCT` override) — the depth leg degrades 32→4 first, and only past that does `max_write` itself degrade back toward the 1 MiB base (never below it). Field shape 32 queues × 4 MiB ⇒ depth 16 at a 2 GiB cap (e.g. `SQUEEZEFS_TRANSPORT_MEM_MAX=2048` or a 16 GiB budget).
- `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_MAX_WRITE=<bytes>`: overrides the daemon's desire verbatim (still sysctl-gated) — the A/B lever for depth-16×4 MiB vs depth-32×1 MiB brackets; not an operational escape.

### FUSE zero-copy serve integration (sqz kernel; opt-in)

On the sqz kernel series (`docker/kernel-sqz/` — the kmbuf bufring + `FUSE_URING_ZERO_COPY` transplant), the FUSE-over-io_uring transport can register each request's pages in a per-queue sparse buffer table and serve eligible traffic **without daemon copies**: cold aligned reads DMA device→caller-pages (`READ_FIXED`), eligible WRITE payloads DMA caller-pages→device (`WRITE_FIXED` — the D14 write-side leg, W1 patch class), everything else bridges through a per-queue memfd bounce. Design `crates/fuse3/src/raw/connection/zc.rs`; adjudications `.benchmarks/2026-08-06-fuse-zc-write-side.md` + `.benchmarks/2026-08-07-zc-bridge-cqe-wedge.md`.

- `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_ZC` (**default on** — ruling D16, 2026-08-07, superseding the earlier 0.97× all-write-rows flip rule; rc-manifest §3f): arms where the surface admits it — requires the kmbuf surface, the kmbuf lever, and CAP_SYS_ADMIN (the kernel's own zc REGISTER gate); every decline is loud, and a kernel that refuses the zc REGISTER (any stock kernel) degrades to the bufring path byte-identically. Armed wins: reads +40–75 % (device DMA → app pages, the K1 kill), durable/seq writes ≥ par. **Known caveat**: UN-shimmed kernel-lane rand-4k writes paid ~20 % (the extraction round trip). **Handler/worker fusion LANDED 2026-08-07** (`.benchmarks/2026-08-07-zc-write-fusion.md` — small armed WRITEs run their handler on the queue worker's own fused lane, zero cross-thread wakes per op; local tcp-devsub A-B-B-A: fusion +12 %/+22 % on the rand rows at −27..29 % daemon CPU/byte, armed-vs-unarmed recovered to 0.973×/1.000× at median): the caveat SUNSET awaits the field bracket (rc-manifest §3f rule: armed rand-4k ≥ 0.97× unarmed on squeeze-test — the sunset row spec is in the fusion note §5). Until then small-op-heavy workloads may still prefer the interception shim (whose ring lane never paid it). `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_ZC=0` is the escape / A-B lever. Engagement: `fuse3_zc_negotiated` (0/1), `fuse3_zc_replies`, the write-vehicle pair `fuse3_zc_write_directs`/`fuse3_zc_write_extractions` (+ byte faces), the fusion trio `fuse3_zc_write_fusions`/`_bytes`/`_demotions`, `read_zc_serve_bytes`.
- `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_ZC_WRITE_FUSION` (**default on** — RESOLVED 2026-08-08, fused-lane-predicate fix: the field falsification (armed rand-4k 0.45× at ~300 µs fabric RTT) was the shape-only hold PREDICATE, not the lane — W1-ineligible writes were held/fused and then paid hold + fused poll + LATE extraction serialized at fabric RTT (the both-vehicles signature `fuse3_zc_write_fusions` ≈ ops ∧ `fuse3_zc_write_extractions` ≈ ops). The hold now gates on the filesystem's W1-eligibility probe (`Filesystem::zc_write_hold_eligible` — the `try_sole_owner_patch` ladder's lock-free read-only mirror) and ineligible shapes extract AT DELIVERY on the classic dispatch; acceptance A-B-B-A on the fabric-emulated venue (fused ≥ fusion-off on the field shape and the eligible shape; armed ≥ 0.97× unarmed on both) and un-emulated (the W1-shape +11.6 % win, both bracket orders) — `.benchmarks/2026-08-08-fused-lane-predicate.md`): handler/worker fusion for small armed WRITEs — W1-ELIGIBLE hold-candidate deliveries at or under the fusion ceiling run their handler future on the queue worker's own fused lane, deleting the bridge round trip's cross-thread wakes. `0` = the A/B control. Engagement: `fuse3_zc_write_fusions`/`_bytes`; `fuse3_zc_write_fusion_demotions` ≈ 0 steady-state; **`fuse3_zc_write_lazy_extractions` ≈ 0 is the hold gate's staleness law** (growth = the delivery probe drifting from the W1 ladder).
- `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_ZC_READ_FUSION` (**default off** — e2e perf audit R-3, read board #3's zc-leg form; `.benchmarks/2026-09-03-r3-fill-issue-economy.md` §9: the composition verdict): handler/worker fusion for armed FUSE READs at or under the fusion ceiling — the READ handler runs on the reaping queue worker's fused lane, so the zc direct leg's fetch message, its CQE resume and its prefilled COMMIT are same-thread channel ops instead of three cross-thread wakes (the 4k-random attribution measured them at 35–47 µs each around a 40 µs DMA: `zc_bridge_phase_ns` `msg_hop` / `device_cq` − device / `wake_hop`). Composed with R-2's READ fast-dispatch as ONE law at the delivery CQE: (a) a warm READ is served inline by the reap thread's try-only probe; (b) else a zc-eligible READ at or under the ceiling is fused onto the worker's lane; (c) else the minted handler is handed to the `fuse3-tpc` lane homed on the queue's CPU — `transport_fast_dispatch_serves + fuse3_zc_read_fusions + transport_fast_dispatch_demotes ≡ READs`, every READ exactly one arm. Reads above the ceiling (`SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_ZC_FUSION_MAX`, the same bound the write lane uses — a serve the probe declined may still copy the request's size on the worker) take arm (c). Standing alone against the pre-R-2 dispatch it won (field kern rand-4k 24×8 +15.5 %, local +17 %, `msg_hop` 32 → 0.6 µs, `transport_wake_writes` 1.57 → 0.62/op); COMPOSED behind R-2's fast dispatch it measured −6.5 % IOPS vs R-2 alone (481 k vs 514 k; p50 +70 µs, p99 −66 %) — the two levers converge on the reap thread's per-op serialization (the fused pass's run-queue waits replace the lane hop and the bridge's two worker hops at a net loss), so the default is OFF and a demoted READ ships on arm (c). `1` = fuse (the tail-shaped A/B posture). Engagement: `fuse3_zc_read_fusions` ≈ every cold kernel READ on an armed session (the 1 MiB stream rows read 0 by the ceiling); `fuse3_zc_read_fusion_demotions` ≈ 0 steady-state.
- `SQUEEZEFS_READ_ZC_SERVE` (**default on** since the 2026-09-05 field bracket (daemon CPU/GiB −24 % cold / −25 % warm on unaligned reads, A-B-B-A; `0` = the A/B control) — e2e perf audit R-4, read board #4/#5; `.benchmarks/2026-09-05-r4-read-zc-serve.md`): the fd-SOURCE zc READ serve. On a zero-copy-armed session every out-paged reply body reaches the request's pages by `READ_FIXED(fd → slot)` — an fd-SOURCED primitive (the device fd on the direct leg, the per-ent bounce memfd otherwise), and no io_uring op copies an anonymous VA into a fixed buffer — so a warm (hot / read-lane hold) or cold-slice serve paid the daemon's tier → bounce copy AND the kernel's bounce → folios pass (2.0 passes/byte warm, 2.69 cold whole-block). `1` builds the whole-block READ fill pool as ONE `MAP_SHARED` memfd slab (the `ZcBounce` two-way-reachability law applied to the fill pool): fills, hot-tier entries and read-lane holds become fd-addressable, the router's hot/hold/fill-slice arms and the R-2 fast probe hand the transport the tier buffer's own `(fd, offset)` instead of copying, and the queue worker bridges THAT into the folios — 1.0 warm, 1.69 cold-slice, no kernel change (the bridge op is the one the bounce already rides), the write path's pool untouched (a separate pool — the A/B moves the READ serve only). Non-pool sources (NVMe read-cache mmap, transform decode output, over-capacity fresh backings) keep the copy path, counted. The lever landed off (2026-09-03) and flipped on when the field bracket adjudicated it (§7 of the note: throughput A ≥ B on all four pairs, the transport-worker class −37…−46 % CPU, `fuse3_zc_fd_body_fallbacks` 0 across 440 k fd-source replies; the zc-capability gate green under both postures). Engagement: `read_zc_pool_serve_bytes` (a closure term beside `read_copy_dest_bytes`/`read_copy_bounce_bytes`/`read_dest_dma_bytes`/`read_zc_serve_bytes`) + `read_zc_pool_serve_warm_bytes` (the hot/hold subset), `fuse3_zc_fd_body_replies` (the transport vehicle count); `fuse3_zc_fd_body_fallbacks` (an fd-source bridge that failed and re-staged through the bounce — the reply is never lost) must stay ≈ 0.
- `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_ZC_FUSION_MAX` (default derived payload/8 — 128 KiB at the shipped 1 MiB transport geometry; range 4096–1073741824): the fused-dispatch payload ceiling — bounds the handler work the queue worker's drain loop runs inline (never move payload-scale memcpys onto the worker). The default brackets the measured hop-vs-inline-copy crossover; explicit values win verbatim.
- `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_KMBUF` (default on; `0` = the A/B control): the kernel-managed reply-buffer negotiation zc rides on (zc is bufring-plus).
- `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_IO_URING_SPIN_US` (default **off** = `0`; range 0–100000; e2e perf audit R-4, `.benchmarks/2026-09-03-r4-reap-thread-economy.md`): the spin-before-park CAP, µs, for the FUSE-over-io_uring queue workers (`f3-ur*`). With a nonzero cap, where a worker would block in `submit_and_wait` it first spins a bounded window watching `IORING_SQ_TASKRUN` (the ring's "deferred task work landed" flag — rings are built with `IORING_SETUP_TASKRUN_FLAG`), the CQ tail, the wake coalescer's armed flag and the shutdown word, so a device CQE or a lane message landing inside the window is reaped without a park and the scheduler's wake latency. The window is ADAPTIVE: 2 × the worker's own park-gap EWMA (a reaction clock — every event it waits for echoes something it just did) capped by this value and the 200 µs rail, engaged only while that worker's queues hold ops in flight (a delivered-not-committed slot or a bridge pend — an idle queue never spins, so an idle mount burns nothing), and refused past the box's queueing knee (whole-box busy > 80 %, the Erlang-C wait onset for c ≥ 16 servers; `/proc/stat` sampled once per 100 ms across the pool). **Measured NOT fat at the field and shipped off**: on the kern rand-4k 24×8 row (cap 200, the rail) the governor absorbed 0.50 parks/op — 69 % of the worker's parks, 0.76 → 0.24/op — and `zc_bridge_phase_ns` `msg_hop` / `device_cq` / `wake_hop` did not move by a µs (27.1 → 27.6 / 97.5 → 96.2 / 36.6 → 38.8), at +5.5 % CPU/op (+10 % on the workers) and a −2.7 % / +0.6 % IOPS bracket with p99.9 +5–21 %: the parks the window covered were the ≤ 16 µs class the kernel wait already returns from without a sleep, and the long class that carries the wake latency sits past any window a 75–80 %-busy box can afford (the ipc theft verdict). Any nonzero value engages the governor with that cap (the registered A/B lever). Engagement (stats inode): `transport_spin_absorbed` (parks the spin deleted — an event caught inside the window), `transport_spin_expired` (windows that ran out and parked), `transport_spin_ns` (the CPU paid — the cost column of any row crediting the lever), `transport_spin_refused_busy` (regime hits the box gauge refused), `transport_spin_window_us` (the last derived window). Closure: `absorbed + expired` ≡ the spins run.
- `SQUEEZEFS_ZC_BRIDGE_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 30000, range 100–600000): the **bounded-outcome law** (zc-bridge-cqe-wedge campaign, 2026-08-07) — every zc bridge op (device fetch/store, bounce bridge, WRITE extraction) in flight past this deadline gets one `AsyncCancel`, and the cancel's own CQE classifies the recovery: found+canceled resolves through the loud fallback ladders; `-ENOENT` with a live pend is a PROVEN lost ring completion and synthesizes its resolution (EIO/fallback — never a silent hang, never an acked byte lost); still-running re-arms the deadline loudly. Tripwires (**must stay 0**): `fuse3_zc_bridge_cancels` (the deadline fired) and `fuse3_zc_bridge_lost` (a completion was lost — kernel-side evidence; report with the mount log). The zcws-9 W4 field wedge (140 requests, 28/32 rings, 96 min, connection-abort recovery) is the class this bounds.

### FUSE io_uring SQPOLL (mount env; measured — leave unset)

- `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_IO_URING_SQPOLL_IDLE_MS` (default unset = **off**): opts every FUSE-side io_uring into kernel submission-queue polling with the given idle timeout — the classical `/dev/fuse` INIT/notify/sideband rings (one poller each) **and** the FUSE-over-io_uring queue rings, which share **one** poller for all queues (qid 0 creates it, the rest attach via `IORING_SETUP_ATTACH_WQ`; a kernel that declines SQPOLL degrades loudly to plain rings, never failing the mount).
- `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_IO_URING_SQPOLL_CPU`: pin that one queue-ring poller (`IORING_SETUP_SQ_AFF`; the leader's pin governs the shared group — per-queue pins do not exist by design).
- **Measured posture (2026-07-15, `.benchmarks/2026-07-15-m10-sqpoll.md`, resolves design OQ 3): not recommended — including for dedicated metadata-heavy nodes.** On the post-M3 transport (one `io_uring_enter` already carries commit+wait), SQPOLL-on measured **+25 % enters/create** (wake-cycle fragmentation; 9.10 → 11.40), **one full core burned by the poller under storm** (idle mounts burn 0.0 % — the idle timeout parks it), and **flat-to-worse paired mdstorm rows** (−1.5 % create … −12.9 % many-dirs unlink) at byte-identical op shape. Consider only on boxes with uncontended spare cores, and only if a live profile of *your* workload (strace `io_uring_enter` counts + `iou-sqp` thread CPU, the M10 method) proves it out.

### Kernel cache TTLs (mount options / env; per-class)

Four kernel-cache TTL classes, each defaulting to the historical 1 s (the DAOS per-class split: directory dentries invalidate whole subtrees, so they get their own knob). Mount options are libfuse-style float seconds (`-o attr_timeout=2.5`) and win over the env knobs (milliseconds); both are per-mount. Longer TTLs widen the staleness window a single mount can observe of its own metadata — safe under the single-writer mount guard; revisit before any multi-writer future. On a **read-only mount** the four defaults are not 1 s: they derive from the writer's checkpoint cadence, because that is the interval a reader can prove freshness over ([Read-only coherent mounts](#read-only-coherent-mounts--o-ro--one-writer-plus-n-readers)). Explicit values still win verbatim there, which means setting one lengthens a reader's staleness window by exactly that much.

- `-o attr_timeout=<s>` / `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_ATTR_TTL_MS`: GETATTR/SETATTR reply TTL + the daemon attr-cache freshness window.
- `-o entry_timeout=<s>` / `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_ENTRY_TTL_MS`: dentry TTL for non-directory lookup/create results.
- `-o dir_entry_timeout=<s>` / `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_DIR_ENTRY_TTL_MS`: dentry TTL for directory results.
- `-o negative_timeout=<s>` / `SQUEEZEFS_FUSE_NEGATIVE_TTL_MS`: TTL for cacheable negative lookup replies (kernel-side negative dentries — repeated misses of the same name stop paying a round trip). `0` disables negative caching (misses reply bare ENOENT).

### External mount supervisor (`mount --daemon --supervise`)

With `--supervise` the `mount --daemon` parent stays alive as an external watchdog (JuiceFS-supervisor precedent): it probes `<mountpoint>/.stats` every 5 s (`SQUEEZEFS_SUPERVISE_INTERVAL_SECS`) and, after 30 s of sustained unresponsiveness (`SQUEEZEFS_SUPERVISE_UNRESPONSIVE_SECS`), logs loudly, dumps the daemon's `/proc` state (per-task wchan + kernel stacks when root), and — when kernel callers are blocked (`waiting > 0`) and the supervisor runs as root — writes `/sys/fs/fuse/connections/<id>/abort` to release them with `ECONNABORTED`. The abort kills the mount by design; the escalation message prints the daemon PID and the exact manual recovery commands (kill-by-PID → `squeezefs umount` → remount). This complements the in-daemon op watchdog, which can log a wedge but cannot clear one.

### Host auto-tuning (`squeezefs tune`)

Built-in host auto-tuning (`squeezefs tune`, requires root) optimizes virtual memory dirty page ratios (40/10), network socket buffer maxima (64 MiB), and live FUSE connection limits (`max_background`/`congestion_threshold` raise-only to the 256/192 measured class — never lowering a ring-capacity-negotiated mount, `read_ahead_kb` to 0). See [Kernel Tuning](../QUICKSTART.md#6-kernel-tuning-for-bare-metal-auto-tune).

```bash
squeezefs tune
```

### Other verbs

* **Unmount** — safely unmounts SqueezeFS by waiting for staging caches to flush before tearing down FUSE:
  ```bash
  squeezefs umount <mountpoint> [--force]
  ```
* **Instant metadata clone (CoW)** — OFFLINE and D0-guarded (like every offline mutating verb: refused while the set is mounted). The `sqmeta://` URI is **required** — both paths are resolved through the metadata set, and the single-writer guard is taken over it for the clone's duration. Metadata-only: the clone's map names the source's blocks and their refcounts rise; not one data byte is copied. A **staged** source is refused loudly (its acked payload lives in the mount's isolated staging, which the offline coordinator never opens) — clone those through a live mount (`cp --reflink=always`, which rides `copy_file_range`):
  ```bash
  squeezefs clone -g sqmeta://<meta_dev>[,<meta_dev>…] <src> <dest>
  ```
* **Storage pools & volumes (LVM)**:
  ```bash
  squeezefs storage pool create <name> <disks...>     # + add/remove/delete/list
  squeezefs storage volume create <pool> <name> --size <sz>   # + extend/delete/list
  ```

## Volume lifecycle & online maintenance

The 2026-07 volume-lifecycle program (`docs/design-volume-lifecycle.md`; closing record `.benchmarks/2026-07-22-volume-lifecycle-closing.md`) shipped dynamic volume membership, online fsck with repair, and a real defragmenter. Everything long-running executes as a **maintenance job**: durable (survives crashes and remounts; resumes by re-run or `job resume`), pausable, duty-cycle throttled (`--throttle 1–100`, retunable live), visible to `squeezefs job list` both on the live mount and offline from the volume records.

Every verb takes a **TARGET** that is either a live mountpoint (online — the operation runs on the mounted daemon through its admin lane) or a `sqmeta://` URI (offline — a short-lived coordinator takes the same exclusive writer guard as `format`; refused while anyone has the volume mounted).

### Data volumes

```bash
squeezefs volume list <target> [--json]            # durable ids, states, honest capacity math
squeezefs volume add-data <target> <device> [--no-rebalance]   # online add; auto-schedules a rebalance pass unless opted out
squeezefs volume remove-data <target> <volume-id> [--throttle N]   # preflight -> drain (CoW evacuation) -> retire
squeezefs volume undrain <target> <volume-id>      # cancel an in-flight drain; volume returns to active
```

- **Preflight is honest**: a remove that cannot fit the victim's census on the survivors is refused **with the numbers printed** (needed / available / in-flight transient / headroom). A running drain re-verifies at every checkpoint and self-pauses (`paused-capacity`) instead of running the survivors out of space.
- A **draining** volume is excluded from new placement but **serves reads to completion**. Shared clone blocks move exactly once. Retired volume ids are permanent — they never come back.
- Fill balance is also maintained continuously in the write path (balance-aware placement: emptier healthy volumes attract proportionally more new blocks; health always outranks balance). `defrag --rebalance` runs the bulk pass on demand.

### Metadata volumes

```bash
squeezefs volume add-meta <sqmeta-uri> <new-device> --take-slots <n|list>  # OFFLINE: unmount first
squeezefs volume remove-meta <sqmeta-uri> <victim-device>                  # OFFLINE: unmount first
squeezefs volume migrate-meta-slot <mountpoint> <slot> <volume-index>      # ONLINE background job
squeezefs volume repair-set <sqmeta-uri>                # reconcile membership stamps after a crashed change
squeezefs volume enable-multi-writer <sqmeta-uri>       # OFFLINE: stamp a pre-flip / --single-writer set multi-writer-capable
squeezefs volume enable-symmetric <sqmeta-uri> [--dry-run|--resume|--abort]
                                                        # OFFLINE: convert the set to the symmetric slot-tree forest (bit 17; DARK program)
```
