The drain is now OBSERVED: every read serve (the FUSE handler, the R-2 fast probe, the il sync probe, the direct-drive DMA to its CQE, the prefetch/read-lane fills, `copy_file_range`'s source read) counts itself in the purge generation it started under, and a candidate promotes once every serve stamped below its qualifying generation has completed — at the qualifying pass when nothing is in flight, or on the completion wake of the last such serve. Strictly safer than any timer (the count IS the serves) and it completes in the serve residence. `D_purge` survives only as the tripwire `free_grace_drain_overdue` (**must stay 0** — a pre-step serve outliving the fail-stop budget is a wedged I/O; the wait is never shortened). `0` = the `D_purge` timer verbatim, the ledger unarmed. Published `free_grace_drain_observed` (promotions decided by observation ≈ `free_grace_reader_acks`), `free_grace_drain_overdue`, `free_grace_drain_wakes`, `reader_serves_inflight`, `reader_serve_step_races` (the ordering recheck engaging — conservative), `reader_serve_slot_overruns` (**must stay 0** — a serve alive across 64 epoch steps). Under READ TOKENS (`SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1` on a `-o ro` mount) `0` REFUSES the mount loud at the arm — the reader's recall ack attests this term and no timer stands behind it (R-SYM-4); the lever governs the `=0` reader alone. |
| `SQUEEZEFS_FREE_GRACE_CHECKPOINT_COMPOSITE` | `on` | The writer→member checkpoint composite (`.benchmarks/2026-09-06-free-grace-checkpoint-composite.md`), both sides: a faster writer checkpoint alone buys nothing (the reader qualifies on a time bound, not on observing the checkpoint), so while the valve is ASKING — a prod in force — the authority's KV checkpoint ceiling becomes `max(P/2, 2 × measured cycle)` against the reader's routine poll `P` (a new root in every pass window), the membership grant CARRIES the ceiling in force (`free_grace_checkpoint_ceiling_ms`; a promise the writer honours for one routine ceiling past the grant), and the member's prod floor and elastic-pass floor follow it, so passes and beats run at `P/2`. In-process on the fleet-cadence shape: `bound_age` 7,724 → 6,750 ms at exactly 2× checkpoints/s and 2× renewals/s while asking — the cost the user accepted. The published `reader_staleness_bound_ms` is unchanged and the elastic cadence sits inside it (worst case `P/2 + P/2`). `0` = the shipped cadences verbatim. |

**Space pressure: ENOSPC, not corruption.** If the free list is entirely in
grace, allocation refuses `ENOSPC` — promptly, loudly, counted in
`free_grace_alloc_stalls`. Reallocating an offset a reader may still resolve
would serve another file's bytes, silently, on a passthrough volume; a
bounded availability loss is the lesser failure, and this wait always ends
by itself because allocation evaluates a **shorter** pressure deadline (one
cycle, floored) and fences past it. `df` counts held offsets as **used**,
which is honest: they are genuinely unavailable until acknowledged.

**The wait is BOUNDED in wall time and TERMINAL past its wall** (the
2026-09-05 s11 fleet wedge, `.benchmarks/2026-09-06-cowriter-enospc-wedge.md`):
a write-path allocation parks on grace-held space for at most twice the
routine fence bound (`free_grace_fence_bound_base_ms`), floored at one
second — a duration, never the reallocation label — and on a co-writer only
while the authority's last harvest reply reported a held ring
(`free_grace_bound_age_ms` nonzero). Past the wall the `ENOSPC` stands for
that write: the block stripe is released and the write replies. Before the
fix a co-writer's exhausted-lane write parked forever under its block
stripe (the field's 30-minute watchdog storm with `.stats` in D-state).

**Live signals** (`.stats`, writer side). `free_grace_mode` is `off` (no
plane — the shipped default), `idle` (armed, no members) or `armed`:

| Signal | Healthy reading |
|---|---|
| `free_grace_deferrals` / `free_grace_releases` / `free_grace_offsets` | the ledger: `deferrals = releases + offsets`. `offsets` should oscillate with churn and fall back toward 0, not climb monotonically |
| `free_grace_bytes` | device bytes held — the space the readers currently owe you back |
| `free_grace_forced_releases` | **the tripwire:** offsets released *without* an acknowledgement, i.e. past the bound or at the ring cap. 0 on a healthy fleet; nonzero means at least one reader was fenced, and only that reader's coherence was ever at stake |
| `free_grace_laggard_fences` | readers evicted for not acknowledging. Investigate alongside `membership_renewals` — a reader renewing but not acknowledging is a revalidation problem, not a network one |
| `free_grace_alloc_stalls` | allocations that refused ENOSPC with offsets held. Expected only on a genuinely full store; sustained growth means the readers are too slow for the write rate (raise capacity, or shorten the cycle with `SQUEEZEFS_MEMBERSHIP_LEASE_TTL_MS` / `SQUEEZEFS_META_REVALIDATE_MS`) |
| `write_enospc_refusals` | WRITE replies refused `ENOSPC` — the synchronous face of exhaustion (a promotion or sparse-stripe mint the write itself owns; custody the never-lossy ladder ACKed reports at `fsync`, not here). Growth is honest pressure. Its complement is the tripwire: writes that neither reply nor count while `alloc_lane_enospc_refusals` climbs (`fuse_op_watchdog_overdue` growing on `write`) is the pre-fix wedge shape |
| `free_grace_bound` | the label the writer may reallocate up to; `free_grace_fence_bound_ms` / `free_grace_pressure_bound_ms` / `free_grace_ring_cap` publish the derived numbers in force so this page cannot drift from them. **`free_grace_fence_bound_ms` is the deadline IN FORCE** — it moves as rung 2 tightens it — and `free_grace_fence_bound_base_ms` is the un-tightened derivation beside it |
| `free_grace_pressure_pct` | the graded pressure reading: `0` = the supply outlives the routine bound at the measured deferral rate (quiet), `100` = it is already gone. It is a RATE-derived forecast, not an occupancy: a nearly-empty ring under a violent storm reads high, which is the point |
| `free_grace_prods` (rung 1) | grants that carried a shortened renewal cadence to a member the writer was waiting on. `0` on a quiet writer; growth under a storm is the ladder working, and `free_grace_prod_renew_ms` is the cadence currently being handed out (`0` = none in force). **Growth with `free_grace_bound` flat is the stop-and-read signal**: the ask is being delivered and not answered, so expect the fence next — check that reader's `meta_kv_revalidate_epochs` and its `free_grace_reader_acks` |
| `free_grace_prod_decays` | expiry windows in which a live ask RELAXED one doubling step toward routine instead of lapsing (finding 18, `.benchmarks/2026-08-25-s11-freeloop-stall.md`: under a storm the runway reading sawtooths, and a pre-fix lapse handed the very next renewal beat the ROUTINE cadence — one routine grant is one routine-beat acknowledgement hole, and `free_grace_bound_age_ms` inherits the widest member's hole). Growth under a bursty storm is the bleed working; `0` with `free_grace_prods` churning just means the readings never lapsed. The ladder retires at routine, and a DRAINED ring retires at once with no decay step |
| `free_grace_prods_caught_up` | grants that carried the RELAXED ask — one doubling step of the cadence in force, capped below routine — to a member whose acknowledgement already covered every held label while the ask was live (finding 15 phase B1, `.benchmarks/2026-09-07-membership-renewal-isolation.md`; `SQUEEZEFS_FREE_GRACE_CAUGHT_UP_RELAX`). Disjoint from `free_grace_prods` (those are laggards). The shipped snap handed such a member ROUTINE, so at the instant the ring drained every member drew the 10 s beat — its only label source — and a refill two seconds later could be told to nobody for a beat (served `membership_renewals` 0/s for 7 s, `free_grace_member_ack_lag_ms.max` walking 1 s/s to 10–11.6 s). Growth around every drain-and-refill is the arm working; `0` with `free_grace_offsets` sawtoothing to 0 and back means the lever is off — expect the hole. **The member-side face**: `membership_renew_cadence_ms` on a co-writer reading the routine 10,000 while this authority's `free_grace_prod_renew_ms` reads 500 IS the hole in progress |
| `free_grace_bound_tightenings` (rung 2) | harvests that evaluated a deadline below the routine bound. Its ratio against `free_grace_forced_releases` is the whole point of the valve: tightenings are supposed to be many and forced releases none. Both `0` while `free_grace_offsets` climbs means the valve is disarmed (`SQUEEZEFS_FREE_GRACE_VALVE=0`) |
| `free_grace_demand_pct` | **the coupling face**, beside the scarcity face `free_grace_pressure_pct` (the sustain campaign, `docs/design-free-grace-sustain.md`): a sustained rewrite whose throughput is paced by the loop reads ≈ 100 HERE while scarcity reads 0 — the measured s11 failure shape. Read `free_grace_demand_waits` (the standing site-0 detector's count) beside it; `0` with the loop churning = `SQUEEZEFS_FREE_GRACE_DEMAND=0` |
| `free_grace_demand_prods` | rung a′'s share of `free_grace_prods` (⊆): members asked to renew at the FLOOR cadence because recycle coupling — not space scarcity — is live. The fence deadline never tightens off this arm: asked sooner, never fenced sooner |
| `free_grace_bound_refreshes` | bound recomputes run on the demand/prod harvest path (T7 collapsed to the floor beat). Law: ≤ elapsed ÷ the floor — a breach is a bug |
| `free_grace_bound_age_ms` / `free_grace_residence_ms` | the loop-latency instruments (owner-clock `now − BOUND` while holding; the per-release residence histogram). Post-campaign target on a coupled storm: bound age ≤ 12 s sustained |
| `free_grace_hold_phase_ns` | **the hold DECOMPOSED** (the hold-time campaign, `.benchmarks/2026-09-06-free-grace-hold-time.md`): per released offset, `defer_checkpointed` (the first KV checkpoint completed after the free — the instant a reader's poll could adopt a root carrying the dereference), `checkpointed_min_acked` (until the first bound publish that covered the label — every member acknowledged past it) and `min_acked_released` (the harvest); `total` is the residence and the three are exact-sum with it over the placed population (`free_grace_hold_unplaced` counts the rest). On the s11 shape the middle stage carried ≈ 7 s before the 2026-09-06 ladder re-derivation — the readers' qualify (`staleness + skew`) and drain (`staleness + D_purge`) windows plus their pass/beat quantization; since it, the qualify window is the writer's checkpoint ceiling + skew (≈ 1.1 s) and the drain is observed (milliseconds), so the stage reads ≈ 2.3 s in the fleet-cadence model — and the other two are sub-second; a large FIRST stage means the writer is not checkpointing (check `meta_kv_checkpoints`), a large LAST stage means nothing is allocating or freeing (the harvest runs per free and per allocation) |
| `free_grace_hold_ms` | the live hold — the EWMA of the residence at release — the number the capacity law multiplies your churn by (below) |
| `free_grace_member_ack_lag_ms` (`max` / `mean` / `min` / `members`) | owner-clock `now − acked` per live member (`now − joined` for one that has acknowledged nothing). The bound is the MIN over members, so `max` is the member holding the free list; `max − min` well above one beat names a laggard. With `SQUEEZEFS_STATS_KEY_CENSUS=1` the per-member census `free_grace_member_ack_lag_census` names it |
| `free_grace_checkpoint_marks` / `free_grace_checkpoint_cycle_ms` | the checkpoint marks the first stage is read against (≈ `meta_kv_checkpoints` on an armed writer) and the measured checkpoint cycle cost |
| `free_grace_checkpoint_ceiling_ms` / `free_grace_checkpoint_elastic_cycles` | the writer→member checkpoint composite (`SQUEEZEFS_FREE_GRACE_CHECKPOINT_COMPOSITE`): the checkpoint ceiling IN FORCE on the authority — the routine `max(flush tick, 1 s)` with no ask, half the reader's poll while the valve is asking (`free_grace_prod_renew_ms` nonzero) — and the cycles run under the elastic one. `meta_kv_checkpoints` and `membership_renewals` both run at ≈ 2× their routine rate while it is engaged: that is the composite's accepted cost, not a regression. Ceiling at the routine with `prod_renew_ms` nonzero = the lever is off. On a member's `.stats` the key is `free_grace_advertised_ceiling_ms`: the LANDING ceiling advertised with its label (the decision ceiling + the two checkpoint-tick terms — `checkpoint_landing_ceiling_for_elastic`; 1,100 ms routine on the shipped flush, 600 ms under a 500 ms elastic decision), the qualify term's and the pass floor's input; the member falls back to its own landing derivation when nothing was advertised. |
| `free_grace_bound_refreshes_on_ack` / `free_grace_bound_scan_ms` | lever (d)'s engagement (⊆ `free_grace_bound_refreshes`; growth under a storm is the arm working, 0 = `SQUEEZEFS_FREE_GRACE_REFRESH_ON_ACK=0`) and the measured cost of one O(members) minimum, the floor its rate limit rides |
| `free_grace_lane_push_releases` / `free_grace_lane_push_hints` | the lane-push lever's authority faces (finding 15 term 2, `.benchmarks/2026-09-06-free-grace-lane-visible.md`): binding acknowledgements whose arrival harvested every ring to its uncovered front (the release follows the ack, not the next free / allocation / harvest RPC — `min_acked_released` then reads lever (d)'s rate limit, ≤ `floor ÷ members`, instead of the wait for demand), and renewal grants that carried a nonzero lane-supply hint. Both 0 = `SQUEEZEFS_FREE_GRACE_LANE_PUSH=0`, or an authority with no co-writers. **A large `min_acked_released` with these growing is a bug; with these 0 it is the fleet's quiet phases** |
| `alloc_lane_visible_phase_ns` (`released_served` / `served_visible` / `total`) + `alloc_lane_visible_unplaced` + `alloc_lane_release_marks` | **the hop BEHIND the release** (finding 15 term 2): a released co-writer-lane block sits on the AUTHORITY's free list, reachable only by that co-writer's next harvest RPC. `released_served` (authority clock — the stage the authority's `.stats` carries) is that wait; the co-writer's `.stats` carries all three, `served_visible` its own round trip and `total` exact-sum. The authority's `alloc_lane_release_marks` is the blocks currently waiting (and `alloc_lane_supply_blocks` its whole held-for-peers supply); `alloc_lane_visible_unplaced` counts served blocks that reached the list other than through a grace release. **`released_served` in seconds means the co-writers are not asking**: a co-writer holding supply above its watermark never polls, so its released blocks accumulate here until the pushed refill (or its next ENOSPC) — with the lever on, expect ≤ one renewal cadence |
| `free_grace_lane_push_wakes` / `alloc_lane_pushed_harvests` / `free_grace_lane_supply_hint` (co-writer side) | the lever's co-writer faces: hints that woke a refill, the pushed harvests they produced (⊆ `alloc_lane_harvests`), and the last hint learned (blocks of this lane on the authority's lists, SUMMED over its volumes — 0 = nothing owed there right now). The pushed decision is per VOLUME (`.benchmarks/2026-09-07-cowriter-lane-aware-placement.md`): a volume owed blocks asks, a DRY volume asks on any hint even when owed nothing (the summed hint cannot name the volume; one RPC either refills it or proves the supply is its sibling's), a stocked volume owed nothing never asks. Wakes with no pushed harvests = every volume is stocked and owed nothing |
| `backend_placement_lane_failovers` / `backend_placement_lane_exhausted_picks` (co-writer side, the `placement` object) | lane-aware placement on a laned co-writer (`SQUEEZEFS_COWRITER_LANE_PLACEMENT`, `.benchmarks/2026-09-07-cowriter-lane-aware-placement.md`): placed allocations that landed on a volume OTHER than the §5.9 pick because the pick's lane refused `StorageFull` and a sibling's answered (each is one wasted harvest RPC on the dry volume — nonzero under churn is the failover working, a large rate beside a flat `alloc_lane_reachable_blocks` means every volume is dry and the harvest is the only refill), and picks that landed on a lane-exhausted volume while a sibling had lane supply — **must stay ≈ 0**: the pick reads the same O(1) counters, so growth is predicate rot. Both 0 on every single-writer / authority mount and under the lever off |
| `free_grace_lane_push_wakes` / `alloc_lane_pushed_harvests` / `free_grace_lane_supply_hint` (co-writer side) | the lever's co-writer faces: hints that woke a refill, the pushed harvests they produced (⊆ `alloc_lane_harvests`), and the last hint learned (blocks of this lane on the authority's free lists, summed over the mount's data volumes — 0 = nothing of the lane is there right now). Since the refill-hint gate (2026-09-07) a nonzero hint IS the pushed refill's condition, so wakes ≈ pushed harvests on a laned co-writer; wakes with no pushed harvests = `SQUEEZEFS_ALLOC_LANE_REFILL_HINT=0` with `alloc_lane_owed_blocks` 0 (the retired gate) |
| `alloc_lane_hint_refills` / `alloc_lane_owed_blocks` (co-writer side) | **the refill gate's ledger** (finding 15, `.benchmarks/2026-09-07-lane-refill-hint-gate.md`): proactive harvests — ahead + pushed — that fired with the owed word at 0, i.e. the ones the retired owed-only gate would have declined (supply the authority's publish RECOMPUTE put on its list; ⊆ `alloc_lane_ahead_harvests + alloc_lane_pushed_harvests`, 0 under `REFILL_HINT=0`); and the owed word itself — the EXPLICIT-ship arm's face (shipped `Freed` verdicts − harvest adoptions, clamped), a strict subset of the advertised supply and no longer a gate. **The shape to look for**: `alloc_lane_harvests` climbing with `alloc_lane_enospc_refusals` while `alloc_lane_ahead_harvests + alloc_lane_pushed_harvests` sit flat and `free_grace_lane_supply_hint` reads hundreds is the pre-fix starvation — the refill running only from inside ENOSPC parks |
| `alloc_lane_harvests` / `alloc_lane_harvest_coalesced` / `alloc_lane_harvest_declined_stale` (co-writer side) | **the single-flight harvest's ledger** (finding 15 phase B1, `.benchmarks/2026-09-07-lane-harvest-single-flight.md`, `SQUEEZEFS_ALLOC_LANE_HARVEST_SINGLE_FLIGHT`): the harvest RPCs actually sent, the callers that found one in flight on their allocator and awaited ITS outcome instead of sending their own, and the callers that took a fresh EMPTY reply's `0` without a wire trip because nothing the authority advertises had moved (no grant, no wake, no owed `Freed`). `harvests + coalesced + declined_stale` = every would-be call. Under a park on a starved lane expect `coalesced` ≈ the park population per RPC and `declined_stale` ≈ one per slice per parked allocation, with `harvests` ≤ one per renewal grant per volume; `harvests` climbing at the slice rate (≈ 20/s per parked allocation) with both others 0 is the lever off. **The authority's face**: its `membership_renewals`/s holding steady under a co-writer ENOSPC storm is the point — the B1 capture read 22/s → 1–12/s with the ring's bound ageing to 11 s while every member's own ack was fresh |
| `alloc_lane_volume_hint_skips` (co-writer side) | **the per-volume hint's engagement** (`SQUEEZEFS_ALLOC_LANE_VOLUME_HINT`, finding 15's fpp residue): pushed refill decisions a volume declined because the grant's vector advertised 0 of this lane on it while the mount sum — the sibling's share — would have fired the shipped decision. Each is one empty harvest RPC the vector saved; on the s11 fpp phases the sum-driven pushed tick sent ≈ 20–30 % of a co-writer's harvests to the volume the authority held nothing for. 0 with the lever off, and on every mount that is not a laned co-writer. The decline's witness is per volume too: `alloc_lane_harvest_declined_stale` now grows while the authority advertises 0 for THAT volume, whatever the sibling does |
| `free_grace_ack_renewals` (reader-side) | lever (b)'s engagement: promotions that carried themselves home at once (≈ `free_grace_reader_acks` under a storm; 0 = `SQUEEZEFS_FREE_GRACE_ACK_RENEWAL=0`) |
| `free_grace_qualify_lag_ms` (reader-side) | the acknowledgement's qualify window in force — the writer's checkpoint landing ceiling + skew (ladder re-derivation item 1; 1,122 ms on the shipped clocks), or the retired `staleness + skew` (2,022 ms) under `SQUEEZEFS_FREE_GRACE_QUALIFY_CEILING=0`. A candidate qualifies on the first purging pass that began this long after its label was learned |
| `free_grace_drain_lag_ms` / `reader_layout_step_gen` / `reader_layout_step_misses` (reader/co-writer-side) | the drain window's TIMER in force — what the clock still waits out: 0 with items 2 and 3 both on, `D_purge` under `SQUEEZEFS_FREE_GRACE_DRAIN_OBSERVED=0`, `staleness + D_purge` with both levers off — the reader's layout-cache purge generation (one bump per epoch step per volume — 0 for the life of a plain writer) and the pre-step layout entries the step gate refused (each one re-resolve a TTL would have deferred; growth ≈ the hot ino population × the step rate under a storming writer, structurally 0 on a writer) |
| `free_grace_drain_observed` / `free_grace_drain_overdue` / `free_grace_drain_wakes` / `reader_serves_inflight` / `reader_serve_step_races` / `reader_serve_slot_overruns` (reader/co-writer-side) | the OBSERVED drain (ladder re-derivation item 3): promotions decided by the pre-step in-flight count reaching zero (≈ `free_grace_reader_acks` on an armed reader; 0 = `SQUEEZEFS_FREE_GRACE_DRAIN_OBSERVED=0`), candidates whose drain outlived the `D_purge` fail-stop budget (**must stay 0** — a read serve that long is a wedged I/O, and the acknowledgement waits on it regardless; pairs with `invariant_tripwires`), completions of the last pre-step serve that woke the revalidation task early, the read serves counted right now, stamps whose ordering re-check saw an epoch step land mid-stamp (counted one generation early — conservative, never a fault), and slots re-stamped while still occupied — a serve alive across 64 epoch steps (**must stay 0**; the slot is counted pre-step for every candidate until it drains) |
| `free_grace_pass_prods` / `free_grace_pass_interval_ms` (reader-side) | L2b's elastic revalidation passes: a prodded member also tightens its pass cadence toward the 1 s checkpoint ceiling (more qualify/promote passes — the qualification windows and the PUBLISHED staleness bound never move). Structurally 0 where the routine interval already sits at the floor; `SQUEEZEFS_FREE_GRACE_PASS_ELASTIC=0` restores the routine cadence verbatim |
| `free_grace_ack_pipeline_depth` / `free_grace_acked_lag_ms` (reader-side) | the pipelined acknowledgement ladder (L1): candidates in flight (≤ the derived cap; ≤ 1 under `SQUEEZEFS_FREE_GRACE_ACK_PIPELINE=0`) and the reader's own promote lag |
| `free_grace_reader_acks` (reader side — a reader's `.stats` reads `free_grace_mode: "reader"`, beside `free_grace_{learned,acked,reader_pending}_label`) | acknowledgements this reader has emitted. **Flat while the writer churns is the failure to look for** — it means this reader is holding the writer's free list. `learned` moving with `acked` flat says the revalidation pass is not advancing (check `meta_kv_revalidate_epochs`); a standing `reader_pending_label` says an acknowledgement is waiting out its drain window, which is normal |

**Recall-driven under READ TOKENS (design-symmetric-metadata §5.7.3 — PR 5, dark until the PR-14 flip).** On a writer whose symmetric plane is armed (`SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1` on a bit-17 set) the epoch acknowledgement is NO LONGER the qualification: a layout publish that frees a block RECALLS the file's read tokens before it commits, a reader acks only after its in-flight DMA on the ino drained (`ro_coherence::ServeStamp`) and its block-key census was purged (the R-6 purge), and the terminal free then publishes to the free list DIRECTLY — `free_grace_recall_gated_frees` counts it, and the free's hold IS the recall round trip (`dlm_token_recall_rtt_ns.total`: 2.3 ms for one writer × 64 in-process readers on the dev box — scoping, against the 2,724 ms epoch-fan-in composite of `.benchmarks/2026-09-06-free-grace-hold-time.md`). The ring above survives as the **TIMEOUT path**: while a LIVE member's recall is unacked past the bound (`dlm_token_recall_timeouts_live` — must-stay-0), frees ride the ring for `T_owner` exactly as above (`free_grace_recall_timeout_deferrals`, and `free_grace_deferrals` with them — the closure `deferrals ≡ releases + offsets` holds on both paths), released by the ring's own law: the acknowledgement, or the fence that evicts the non-acker at `T_owner`. The epoch fan-in (`membership_min_acked_free_epoch`, the shard fan-in) stays the unarmed posture's mechanism and the ring's release law under tokens; its retirement as the qualification is this arm's, its deletion the PR-14 flip's.

## Observability

A mounted filesystem exposes live daemon metrics as JSON on the virtual **`.stats`** inode at the mount root (`cat <mountpoint>/.stats`) — the preferred live regression signal (layout mix, cache/tier counters, `meta_kv_*`, `writer_guard_*`, transport geometry, patch/fold ledgers, memory-budget level).

**`.stats` / `.config` access (VAL-7a).** Both virtual inodes are **mode `0400` owned by the mount uid** (with `-o default_permissions` always set, the kernel enforces that): their payload is a map of the daemon's private state — every backing-device path, every staging directory, the read-cache census and per-inode write custody — so on an `--allow-other` mount they must not be readable by co-tenants. Read them as the mount owner or as root.

**Lock-manager fields (`dlm_*`).** `dlm_mode` is the lock authority's mode: **`solo`** means this daemon is the lock master for every metadata slot, which is the only mode that ships — so `dlm_rpcs` (lock operations needing a remote slot owner) is **0 by construction and must stay 0**. `dlm_rpcs` keeps that meaning exactly: it counts **lock** round trips, never metadata ones — the metadata face is `meta_ship.dlm_rpcs_meta` (see [Metadata function shipping](#metadata-function-shipping-dlm-s8)). Nonzero on a single-node mount is a bug, never load: the lock refused rather than granting custody its owner never issued, and the daemon logged one loud line per event naming the object and its home slot. `dlm_term` is the durable writer era every fencing token this mount mints carries (the process-wide maximum; the per-volume face is `writer_guard_term`) — it must be strictly greater than any predecessor's on the same volume set, and `0` means the volumes predate incompat bit 7. Cross-**mount** write exclusion is not this subsystem's job in `solo` mode — it is the single-writer mount guard's (see [Single-writer mount guard](#single-writer-mount-guard-guarantee-classes)). Since **DLM S9** `dlm_rpcs` counts a *travelling* acquire wherever a custody client is armed, and the remote-custody ledger lives in the `dlm_custody` object beside it (`mode`, `dlm_custody_held`, the grant/renew/revoke counters, `dlm_custody_generation`) — all `0`/`off` on every mount that ships; the field guide is [Multi-writer data plane](#multi-writer-data-plane-dlm-stage-s9).

**Allocation-partition fields (`alloc_lane_*`, DLM S9).** `alloc_lane_writers` is the number of lanes a data volume's block space is partitioned into — **`0` means unpartitioned**, which is every mount that ships, and then every other field in the family is `0` by construction (a single writer installs no partition at all). On a partitioned mount: `alloc_lane_id` is this mount's own lane, `alloc_lanes_owned` is what it may mint in (its own plus every lane adopted after its holder was proven dead — `alloc_lane_adoptions` counts those acts), `alloc_lane_reservations` is the durable watermark commits (one per grain of **fresh** blocks per lane; reuse pays none, so raises ÷ fresh blocks is the live amortization factor and growth proportional to allocations means the grain collapsed), `alloc_lane_shipped_reservations` is the CO-WRITER engagement gauge (a co-writer holds no metadata authority, so every one of its raises travels to the authority and is committed there — 0 with a nonzero `alloc_lane_id` means the mount is committing its own frontier, which only an authority may do), `alloc_lane_raise_refusals` **must stay 0** (a raise refused for naming a lane the authority did not assign, a width that is not the era's, or a lease that is not custody — and any refusal means an offset was NOT handed out, so the write stalled loudly instead of using an uncovered one), `alloc_lane_stranded_bytes` is the **published capacity bound** — bytes belonging to lanes this mount cannot reach, including the unassigned lanes the power-of-two rounding leaves — and `alloc_lane_enospc_refusals` **must stay 0**: it counts allocations refused because this lane was exhausted while the set still had free space. The planning rules are in [Multi-writer capacity planning](#multi-writer-capacity-planning--the-data-plane-allocation-partition).

**Membership fields (`membership_*`, DLM S6).** `membership_mode` is `off` (no plane armed — the default), `owner` (this mount is the lease authority) or `member`. On an owner, `membership_members` / `membership_readers` / `membership_writers` are the live census, `membership_lease_ttl_ms` and `membership_self_deadline_ms` are the two clocks as armed, `membership_grace_remaining_ms` is a failover window in progress, and `membership_min_acked_free_epoch` is the freed-offset epoch every live member has acknowledged passing. The counters: `membership_renewals` is the heartbeat itself — it is the counter that used to be one journal transaction per client per 10 s, so it grows while `meta_kv_journal_entries` does not, which is the whole point; `membership_registration_commits` is bounded by mounts and membership changes, so growth proportional to renewals is a regression, not load; `membership_self_fences` **should stay 0** — nonzero means members are fencing their own objects because renewals are not completing (availability lost, divergence prevented); `membership_renew_refusals`, `membership_evictions`, `membership_grace_refusals`, `membership_grace_reclaims` and `membership_census_serves` are the refusal, revoke, failover and read-side ledgers; `membership_reclaim_refusals` counts a member's re-asserted reclaims the venue REFUSED before `T_self` (unreachable, or an owner that would not admit it) — one per attempt, so it is also the retry cadence's face: a member whose owner died re-asserts against the successor the home volume's rendezvous names, at the cadence law over the window left (`min(renew_interval, remaining-to-T_self / 3)`), never a spin (symmetric PR 12b found the un-parked arm re-asserting at 1 ms against a dead manager for its whole `T_self`). On a member, `membership_renew_sched_lag_ms` is a **max-gauge**: the worst observed intended-wake → actual-run scheduling lag of the renewal tick — growth toward `T_self` is lease-venue starvation surfacing in stats *before* it becomes a self-fence (the renewal cadences run on the dedicated `sqz-lease` thread precisely so meta-plane congestion cannot cause it; finding 2, 2026-08-20). **Where a renewal's time went** (finding 15 phase B1, 2026-09-07, `.benchmarks/2026-09-07-membership-renewal-isolation.md`) is decided by three always-on faces, never argued: on a member, `membership_renew_phase_ns` decomposes every renewal into `carry_wait` (the decision — a beat's due instant or the promotion that asked for carriage — to the frame's send: lane scheduling plus the blocking venue's wait), `rtt` (send → reply) and `total` (decision → grant adopted; ≥ the other two), and `membership_renew_cadence_ms` is the BEAT in force; on the authority, `membership_renew_serve_ns` is frame-in → reply-built inside the membership verb (one `scc` probe plus atomics — tens of µs whatever storm the publish plane is under). Read them together: all three member spans small and the serve small while the member's cadence reads the routine 10,000 and the authority's `free_grace_prod_renew_ms` reads 500 is not a stall anywhere — it is the drained-instant beat hole `SQUEEZEFS_FREE_GRACE_CAUGHT_UP_RELAX` closes. `carry_wait` in the hundreds of ms with `rtt` small is the venue: the round trip queued behind bulk blocking work, which `SQUEEZEFS_MEMBERSHIP_RENEW_LANE=0` reproduces (`membership_renew_lane_calls` is the dedicated `sqz-lease-io` thread's engagement — ≡ renewals on the default).

The **key census** fields (`read_lru_keys`, `write_lru_keys`, `nvme_staged_write_file_ids`, `nvme_read_cache_block_keys`, `active_writes`) are **opt-in**: set `SQUEEZEFS_STATS_KEY_CENSUS=1` on the daemon to populate them (read live, no remount needed — the flag also reports itself as `stats_key_census`). The census-free count gauges always export and are what tooling should key on: `read_lru_key_count`, `write_lru_key_count`, `nvme_staged_write_file_count`, `nvme_read_cache_block_count`, `active_write_block_count` (`squeezefs umount`'s unflushed-staged-write check reads the last two).

* **Show filesystem status:**
  ```bash
  squeezefs status [sqmeta://<meta_dev> | <mountpoint>]   # config + volume summary (JSON)
  ```
  The report's `"Clients"` array carries the volume's real mount registrations (same records and classification as `squeezefs clients` below). When the backing device is fabric-attached, the report also carries a per-volume `"Fabric"` section (see [Fabric observability](#fabric-observability)).

* **List client mount registrations:**
  Serves the `client:{id}` heartbeat records and the single-writer `writer_claim` recorded on the volume set's root inos — the same records the format preflight and the mount guard consume, under the same staleness law. Read-only probe: works beside a live mount and never perturbs it. States: `live` (fresh heartbeat), `stale` (heartbeat older than the 45 s TTL — crashed or partitioned holder), `dead` (writer claim whose same-host pid is provably gone — reclaimable immediately, no TTL wait).

  When a mount serves the [membership plane](#membership-plane--lease-based-liveness-dlm-s6), the report additionally carries its **live members** — kinds `member-writer` and `member-reader` — read from the owner's RAM census (one attribute read plus a paged RPC, independent of member count) rather than from per-client records. Read-only **coherent readers appear only this way**: a reader writes nothing, anywhere, so it has no record to list. Their `live`/`stale` classification is the same 45 s law as the records', measured against the lease instead of a heartbeat timestamp.
  ```bash
  squeezefs clients sqmeta://<meta_dev> [--json]
  ```
  ```text
  KIND    ID                                     PID      STATE  AGE   VOLUME
  client  0d3179c8-6a02-4f45-9c11-0c8ad6a0a1b2   731022   live   4s    /dev/xai-meta/mds01
  writer  9c41c2e6-6a4e-4bfb-b41c-2fb1b1f2b7aa   731022   live   4s    /dev/xai-meta/mds01
  2 registration(s): 2 live, 0 stale, 0 dead (reclaimable).
  ```

* **List the appender pages of a symmetric-forest volume (offline/URI query):**
  One row per appender directory entry — id, state (`free` / `live` / `recovering` / `recovered`), term, the KD-MW-2 identity (`node_token`, `mount_slot`; `--json` adds the writer id), ring segments and bytes, leased routing slots — read off a read-only probe (the mount's own bootstrap; nothing is written, safe beside a live mount). Refuses a volume without incompat bit 17: until `squeezefs volume enable-symmetric` / `format --symmetric` land (design-symmetric-metadata PR 11) only the test seam stamps the bit, so on every field volume today this verb refuses by design. See [the appender region](#symmetric-metadata-program--the-appender-region-pr-2-dark).
  ```bash
  squeezefs appenders sqmeta://<meta_dev> [--json]
  ```
  ```text
  id   state       term  node_token           mount_slot segments  ring_bytes   leased_slots
  0    live        3     0x9f2c1d8e4b7a4c3f   2864434397 1         1032192      0
  1    unallocated
  ```

* **Show space/inode usage (offline/URI query):**
  Answers from the same authoritative sources as the mounted daemon's statfs — formatted capacity/quotas from the format config, allocator-tracked striped-block usage (rebuilt by the same live-inode-tree walk a mount runs), and the v3 monotonic inode watermark — via read-only probes: **no live mount required**, and beside one it reports the durable point-in-time state. Aggregate plus per-volume rows (data volumes: size/allocated; meta volumes: KV heap size/free, next-ino).
  ```bash
  squeezefs df -g sqmeta://<meta_dev> [--json]
  ```
  ```text
  SqueezeFS 'squeezefs' — offline query over 1 meta / 1 data volume(s), durable state
  Data:   capacity 8.00 GiB   used 64.00 MiB (0.8%)   free 7.94 GiB
  Inodes: quota 1000000   used 2   free 999998
  ```
  A **mounted** filesystem also answers plain `df -h <mountpoint>` from the OS (see [`df` / statfs semantics](#df--statfs-semantics)).

### The `.stats` families — complete reference

Every key the daemon exports, grouped by family (reconciled against a live `.stats` dump of `2fd8c37c` — the 1.2.4 tree — on 2026-09-12: 1,406 exported names, every one named below; keys that appear only under an armed plane, an engaged lane or `SQUEEZEFS_OP_PROFILE=1` say so in their row). Shapes: a bare name is a monotone **counter** unless marked *gauge* (a live level) or *word* (a string/enum); `*_bytes` are bytes; families written `prefix_{a,b,c}` expand to one key per brace member. **Tripwires** are keys that must stay 0 on a healthy mount — nonzero is the stop-and-read signal, never load. **Engagement gauges** are the keys a benchmark row must account for before its number is valid (a row whose engagement delta does not match its op count measured something else). Percentages, ratios and "≈" laws are the reading, not a promise.

**Latency histograms — the one shape.** Every `*_phase_ns` / `*_gap_ns` / `*_timeline_ns` family (and `lease_lock_wait`, `block_lock_wait`, `write_lock_wait*`, `write_lock_hold_*`, `dlm_acquire_time`, `ipc_ingress_ns`, `ipc_session_admission_ns`, `membership_renew_serve_ns`) is an object per phase of the form `{ "buckets": { "<=1us": n, "<=2us": n, …, "<=16s": n, ">16s": n }, "count": N, "sum_ns": S, "mean_ns": S/N }` — 26 log-spaced buckets for the distribution plus **exact** count and sum, so `sum_ns ÷ count` is the true mean (not a bucket-midpoint estimate) and a phase table's members can be checked for containment to the nanosecond (`total ≡ Σ phases` wherever the family says *exact-sum*). A `*_phase_ns` family is an object whose members are the phases, each of that shape (`fsync_phase_ns.total`, `read_serve_phase_ns.block_fetch`, …); `meta_op_phase_ns` nests one level deeper — per op (`create`, `getattr`, `lookup`, `mkdir`, `readdir`, `readdirplus`, `rename`, `setattr`, `unlink`) the three phases `entry_to_backend`, `backend`, `backend_to_reply` and `total`. Root-daemon and transport-side histograms share one bucketing core, so the two tables compose end to end (`fio clat − read_transport_phase_ns.total` is the kernel-side residue by subtraction). Histograms are always-on unless the family says it rides `SQUEEZEFS_OP_PROFILE`. **Count distributions are a different, smaller shape**: `fold_fill`, `meta_reclaim_batch_size`, `meta_reclaim_gather_fill`, `writeback_queue_depth` are bare bucket maps `{ "0": n, "1": n, "2": n, "<=4": n, …, "<=4096": n, ">4096": n }`, and `meta_commit_group_size` / `transport_commit_batch` are `{ "1": n, …, "8": n, "<=16": n, "<=32": n, … }` (with `meta_commit_group_size_median_lb`, the median's bucket lower bound, beside the first). `striped_block_concurrency` is not a histogram at all: it is the derived striped-I/O concurrency cap in force (`clamp(cores × 2, 4, 64)`), a gauge.

#### Identity, posture, build

| Keys | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `build_commit`, `build_tag`, `build_profile` (words) | The fleet mixed-version detector: full git commit (`-dirty` when built from a modified tree), the exact release tag (empty when untagged), the cargo profile (`release` / `dist`). See [Versioning & releases](#versioning--releases) |
| `mount_posture` (word) | `writer` / `reader` / `co-writer` — which of the three mount shapes this daemon is |
| `read_only_mount`, `reader_revalidate_interval_ms`, `reader_staleness_bound_ms`, `reader_staleness_bound_owners` | The read-only mount's posture and its two DERIVED numbers (interval + the writer's ≤ 1 s checkpoint ceiling — 0 on write mounts); `_owners` is how many distinct OWNERS this mount's projection revalidates against (0 on a write mount, 1 on a reader/co-writer, K−1 on a partial authority) |
| `direct_device_true`, `write_shared_enabled`, `write_guard_narrow_enabled`, `read_mostly_cache`, `read_lane_armed`, `zcrx_lane_armed`, `stats_key_census` | Posture words/flags echoing the mount's levers: strict device-true O_DIRECT, shared-mode write admission, the W-2 narrow write-stream guard (`SQUEEZEFS_WRITE_GUARD_NARROW`), read-mostly cache backing, the read-lane hold armed, the zcrx lane armed, the key census armed |
| `inline_max_bytes` (gauge, top level) | The inline-layout ceiling in force — one page (4096) by default, `SQUEEZEFS_INLINE_MAX_BYTES` up to the volume's format bound; a file up to it keeps its payload in its layout record, above it the file is staged or striped |
| `small_file_packing` (word, top level — `true`/`false`), `pack_max_slot_bytes` (gauge, top level), `pack_open_blocks_census` (top level, census — opt-in) | The small-file packing posture (`SQUEEZEFS_SMALL_FILE_PACKING`) and its one law with two faces (`SQUEEZEFS_PACK_MAX_SLOT_BYTES`, derived `block_size/2`: a slot above it takes its own block, a pack block at or below it in live bytes is a compaction victim); the census names the open pack blocks and rides `SQUEEZEFS_STATS_KEY_CENSUS` like every other key census (an empty array otherwise). The counters are under [Small-file packing](#small-file-packing-pack_-packed_-layout_promoted_) |
| `meta_format_version` (word, per volume), `meta_volume_atomicity` (`cow-checksummed`), `meta_volume_atomicity_physical` (`atomic4k` / `likely` / `unknown` / `file-backed`), `data_volume_write_cache` (`<dev>=<class>` per data device) | The durability faces: the metadata format (always `3`), the contract class vs the informational hardware probe, and the DATA plane's write-cache class (`write-back` = acknowledged writes stay volatile until a barrier) |
| `meta_routing_width`, `meta_slot_mint_spread`, `meta_slot_stamp_{runs,cursors}_max` | Dynamic metadata routing: the derived width (65536), the mint spread (64 slots per volume), the stamp encoding's high-water marks |
| `volume_states` (top-level array — one object per data volume: `id`, `backing_dev`, `state`, `healthy`, `capacity_bytes`, `used_bytes`, `free_bytes`), `volumes_peer_owned`, `placement` (top-level object: `backend_fill_spread`, `backend_placement_lane_{failovers,exhausted_picks}` — the laned co-writer's lane-aware placement faces, 0 elsewhere — and `backends[]`, one object per data volume with `id`, `eligible`, `backend_fill_ratio`, `backend_placement_weight`, `backend_placement_picks`), `placement_table_refreshes` | Per-volume lifecycle state, volumes a peer owns (multi-owner sets), the balance-aware write-placement table and its refresh/pick ledger ([Balance-aware placement](#data-volumes)) |
| `client_slot` | This mount's slot in the node's client identity (`node_{…}.m{slot}`) |
| `cache_capacities` (top-level object: `{read_lru,write_lru,nvme_read_cache,nvme_staging}_{current,max}_bytes`), `internal_caches` (top-level object: `metadata_cache_size`) | The tier occupancy/capacity objects (the same numbers the read-path and staging rows below name) |
| `metrics` | The container object most counters below live in (`.stats` has a small top level: identity, caches, the key-census counts and arrays, `volume_states`, `placement`, and the `metrics` object). Per-volume metadata keys inside `metrics` are ARRAYS in volume order (`meta_format_version`, `meta_volume_atomicity[_physical]`, `writer_guard_{mode,fenced,pr_reacquires,term}`, `meta_kv_heap_*`, `meta_kv_merge_*`, `meta_kv_journal_*_per_volume`, `meta_kv_pending_free`, `meta_kv_free_extents`, `meta_kv_replay_*`, `meta_kv_times_echo_pending`, `meta_kv_journal_full_stalls`, `meta_kv_enospc_refusals`); per-DEVICE strings are `<dev>=<value>` arrays (`data_volume_write_cache`, `data_write_lane_submits`) |

#### Tripwires (must stay 0) — the daemon-wide set

`invariant_tripwires` (a runtime concurrency-outcome violation reported instead of a panic), `detached_task_panics` (a detached data-path task unwound — its work is lost), `job_worker_panics`, `lane_exec_task_panics` (a handler completed by panic), `lane_exec_tick_rescues` (a lane notify that never delivered), `lock_ticked_reregisters` (a lost lock wake absorbed by the 2 s tick), `open_count_stranded` (a final forget with open handles counted — a lost RELEASE), `dir_nlink_underflows` (a suppressed parent-nlink decrement), `data_dma_fence_refusals` / `data_dma_epoch_refusals` (DMA refused because this mount is fenced / custody moved), `write_pipeline_fence_drops`, `rewrite_shadow_fence_drops`, `overlay_fence_drops`, `block_free_reclaim_fence_halts` (the write-side, rewrite-side, overlay-side and reclaim-side faces of a fenced writer), `block_double_frees` (an offset released twice), `block_untracked_free_refusals` / `block_live_free_refusals` (a free refused because the offset is untracked / still justified by the durable ledger — leak-safe containment of a double-free lineage), `block_release_skipped_no_record` (a reclaimed corpse's block release skipped BEFORE the free funnel because this ino held no durable record for it — a prior era released the reference but never destroyed the record; the block's RAM refcount belongs to the offset's live owner and is untouched; growth names corpses the same sweep then destroys, never data), `block_key_incarnation_{refusals,exhausted,unknown}` (a block named by a dead incarnation), `block_claim_anomalies` (a block claimed from the free list while this process's refcount map still tracked it — on an authority the double-owner mint seen from the claimer's side, on a co-writer a displaced block whose local hygiene never ran), `packed_mapping_refusals` (a size-carrying `bk:off:len` mapping refused at the one decode choke point — a window past the block, a grain-misaligned offset, an undecodable decoration; each is `EIO`, never another tenant's bytes; fsck C12 names it), `fsck_tenant_overlap_findings` (fsck C12 — two live tenant windows of one block intersecting at different offsets), `served_publish_free_block_refusals` (the authority's served-publish screen refused a publish adopting a block it holds released), `pack_release_untracked_noops` (on a single-writer mount — a pack-reference release on an entry already retired; legitimate only on a co-writer whose tenants were all deleted between the group landing and the seal), `reclaim_destroy_refused_release_failed` (a reclaim destroy withheld because the entry carrying the inode's reference releases did not commit — growth is a failing metadata volume, never a leak: the record survives and the next sweep retries the pair), `free_grace_drain_overdue` / `reader_serve_slot_overruns` (a pre-step read serve outliving the fail-stop budget / alive across 64 epoch steps — on an armed reader), `transport_cq_overflows` (dropped ring completions; `transport_cq_nodrop` is the capability probe), `transport_requests_abandoned`, `read_dest_overruns`, `ipc_direct_reap_stalls`, `ipc_descriptor_rejects`, `ipc_sessions_poisoned`, `fuse3_zc_bridge_cancels` / `fuse3_zc_bridge_lost` / `fuse3_zc_bridge_orphans` (a zc bridge op hit its deadline / a ring completion was provably lost / a pend lost its deadline coverage), `fuse3_zc_release_failures` / `fuse3_zc_retain_refused`, `fuse3_zc_slot_payload_skips`, `zcrx_lane_poisoned`, `meta_kv_block_refs_drift` (fsck C8), `fsck_findings` (on a healthy volume), `fsck_map_orphan_records`, `staging_foreign_scope_{records,refusals}` (another node's staging root), `alloc_lane_{raise_refusals,enospc_refusals}`, `dlm_token_cache_misses`, `dlm_custody_grace_conflicts`, `dlm_grace_conflicts`, `membership_self_fences`, `free_grace_{forced_releases,laggard_fences}`, `meta_ship_publish.{refusals,owner_panics}`, `writer_guard_fenced` (this mount was fenced — read beside every fence-drop family above). `fuse_op_watchdog_overdue` and `transport_lease_overlong` are **loud-never-fatal**: growth is a long-running op or a write handler past the 1 s lease watchdog, attributed in the log.

#### Metadata store (`meta_kv_*` and the conveyor)

| Keys | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `meta_kv_node_cache_{hits,misses,evictions}`, `meta_kv_node_appends`, `meta_kv_node_{append,rewrite}_bytes`, `meta_kv_node_{compactions,splits}`, `meta_kv_node_freeze_shadow_dropped`, `meta_kv_node_dropped_tail_bsets` | The CoW node layer: cache behaviour, appends and rewrites, structural modifications, overlay records shadowed at freeze, torn tails dropped at load |
| `meta_kv_journal_{bytes,entries,full_stalls}`, `meta_kv_journal_{entries,bytes}_per_volume` (arrays) | Journal traffic (one checksummed entry per transaction) and the per-volume balance instrument — a healthy multi-volume set moves every volume under load; `full_stalls` = admission parked on a full ring |
| `meta_kv_checkpoints`, `meta_kv_commit_smo_retries`, `meta_kv_dentry_collision_overflows`, `meta_kv_delta_orphans`, `meta_kv_replay_{entries,dropped_torn,ms}` | Checkpoint cadence, lock-then-revalidate retries against structural modifications, dentry hash overflows, orphaned delta records, and the mount-time replay (torn entries detected-and-dropped, never applied) |
| `meta_kv_block_refs_{staged,released,recovered,drift,unresolved}` | The durable block-reference ledger (one record per reference); `drift` is fsck C8's live must-stay-0 |
| `meta_kv_free_extents`, `meta_kv_pending_free`, `meta_kv_pending_free_{parked,released,overflow}`, `meta_kv_replay_root_frees_dropped` | The A/B extent allocator: free extents, frees parked on durable-tail coverage and released by it (`parked − released` tracks the gauge), at-cap overflow (≈ 0 steady state), and replayed in-window frees DROPPED at mount because their extent is a LIVE tree root — the root-swap carve-out's retirement (design-smo-replay-currency §2 C′: a root swap journals no pointer record, so a kill before the record naming its successor leaves the mount replaying through the predecessor, live again; parking its free released the live root at the first post-mount checkpoint — the shipped defect symmetric PR 3 found and fixed). One per unpublished root swap in the recovered window, flat rings and appender rings alike; **0 on every clean remount** |
| `meta_kv_heap_full` (0/1 per volume), `meta_kv_enospc_refusals`, `meta_kv_heap_full_cycles`, `meta_kv_heap_promised` (per volume) | **A full metadata volume** (design-cow-kv-metadata §4.7, amended 2026-09-11). `heap_full` = 1 says growth that needs a NEW leaf answers `ENOSPC` — the volume is **not** failed: reads, deletes, overwrites and every commit whose records fit a leaf in place keep landing, and it clears when budget returns (one WARN / one INFO per transition, never per refusal). `enospc_refusals` counts the user commits the heap admission refused (each one `ENOSPC` to the application). `heap_full_cycles` counts checkpoint cycles whose flush pass deferred a node because the allocator answered `NoSpace` — the **space standstill** class, which the wedged-tail audit no longer mistakes for a wedge: ≈ 0 even on a full volume (the admission promises every SMO's extents), growth = a foreign claimant or an under-projection, read beside `meta_kv_heap_promised` (extents promised to pending SMOs; 0 at quiesce). The FAILED terminal ("pending-free retirements wedged … volume marked FAILED") is reserved for the wedge class — retirements parked, nothing released, the tail not advancing, and nothing deferred for space. What a full volume cannot do: return extents through deletes — the v1 tree merges underfull leaves (design-cow-kv-metadata §4.6a): `rm` frees record space inside leaves, the heap-full recovery sweep merges adjacent underfull siblings level by level (net one extent returned per merge, 1–2 barriered cycles later), and creates resume into the returned extents — `meta_kv_node_merges` / `meta_kv_merge_sweeps` are the recovery's engagement; if the volume stays full with nothing mergeable, the data is simply there: grow the metadata volume set (`volume add-meta`) for more |
| `meta_kv_node_merges`, `meta_kv_interior_merges`, `meta_kv_root_collapses`, `meta_kv_merge_candidates` (per volume, live), `meta_kv_merge_sweeps`, `meta_kv_merge_laps`, `meta_kv_merge_sweep_ns` / `meta_kv_merge_sweep_projections` | **Leaf merge — the tree shrinks** (design-cow-kv-metadata §4.6a, 2026-09-11). `node_merges` counts underfull-sibling merges (two adjacent leaves → one successor, one extent returned through pending-free 1–2 barriered cycles later); `interior_merges` the same one level up (how underfull leaves under DIFFERENT parents become siblings — §4.6a (d′): a tree converges to its fixed point in ≤ 3·height + 1 passes); `root_collapses` the height decreasing. `merge_candidates` is the EXACT count of underfull leaves the last complete lap projected (≡ `frag_d4_mergeable_leaves` on a quiescent volume — one walk, one law); `merge_sweeps` counts heap-full recovery sweeps, `merge_laps` completed laps of the bounded, cursor-resumed projection walk (a lap that ran out of its budget — one checkpoint tick period, derived — resumes next cycle from its cursor; `merge_sweep_ns` ÷ `merge_sweep_projections` is the measured per-leaf cost, ≈ 2 µs on 4 KiB-value leaves and ≈ 50 µs on dense inode/dentry leaves). A heap-full volume whose `node_merges` stays flat while `merge_candidates` > 0 is the stop-and-read signal (a refused claim at the compaction floor — read `meta_kv_heap_promised`). `defrag --meta` runs the same walk under the job throttle (`defrag_meta_merges`). |
| `meta_kv_forest_slot_trees_minted`, `meta_kv_forest_root_publishes`, `meta_kv_forest_key_violations`, `meta_kv_forest_reader_window_skips`, `meta_kv_forest_reader_unpublished_children` | **The slot-tree forest** (design-symmetric-metadata §5.2, incompat bit 17 — PR 1, dark until the PR-14 flip; a volume without the bit moves none of these). `slot_trees_minted` = guest slot trees minted on a slot's first record (one extent each — the lazy-mint engagement gauge); `root_publishes` = `slot_state` records the checkpoint published into tree 0 (one per guest slot whose root moved since its last publication; ≤ checkpoints × slot trees); `key_violations` **must stay 0** — a record that reached a forest volume's commit, replay or READ path under a key the §5.2.1 codec refuses (a kind byte that is another tree's id, a wrong length, a by-block prefix other than refs): the partition-violation class of "a kind byte is never another tree's id" — the kind-routed RECORD walks SKIP such a record (so a census is never truncated by one) while the by-block refs scan REFUSES it (a population is never answered short), and fsck's forest C1 walk reads the slot trees raw to report it — repaired in place only when its kind byte is one this binary knows in a shape it never takes, `report-only` for a kind this binary does not know (a later binary's record is never deleted); `reader_window_skips` = window records a NON-WRITER open (a `-o ro` reader, a co-writer, a probe) skipped at replay because their slot tree had no published root yet — served at the poll after the writer publishes (S5 bounded staleness); `reader_unpublished_children` = directory entries such a mount withheld from a `readdir` page because the CHILD's slot tree is not yet published (what keeps `readdir` and `lookup` agreeing on the partial view); both 0 for the life of every write mount by construction. |
| `appender_id`, `appenders_live`, `appenders_capacity`, `appender_joins`, `appender_leaves`, `appender_self_recoveries`, `appender_ring_bytes`, `appender_ring_segments`, `appender_ring_grows`, `appender_flush_ceiling_overruns`, `appender_flush_ceiling_ms`, `appender_pressure_cycles`, `appender_live_pages_at_mount`, `appender_native_slot`, `meta_kv_replay_key_violations`, `meta_kv_replay_lease_violations`, `meta_kv_replay_extent_violations`, `meta_kv_bitmap_generation_raises` | **The appender region** (design-symmetric-metadata §5.3 / §11, incompat bit 17 — PR 2, dark; per volume and every one 0 on a bit-17-absent mount, where no region exists — except `meta_kv_bitmap_generation_raises`, which is process-wide and 0 on a HEALTHY mount of either layout). `appender_id` = this mount's appender (0 = the manager's region, KD-SYM-3); `appenders_live` = regions this mount holds with their pages `Live` (0 on a probe / `-o ro` reader, which never joins); `appender_live_pages_at_mount` = `Live` pages the directory held at mount, foreign ones included (the probe's face of a page a writer would refuse to join over); `appender_native_slot` = the volume's native routing slot (page entries name routing slots, converted against it); `appenders_capacity` = **derived** `heap/16 ÷ ring` — the ONE hard resource a join refuses on (§5.11); `appender_joins` / `appender_leaves` / `appender_self_recoveries` = regions joined at open, released at unmount, and own `Live` residue recovered at open (a predecessor that died un-recovered), with the closure law `joins − leaves − recoveries ≡ live` (recoveries performed for OTHER nodes are PR 10's, 0 here); `appender_ring_bytes` / `appender_ring_segments` = the pages' segment tables summed (appender 0's is the fixed extent past its four page slots); `appender_ring_grows` = growth events — a declared region's ring that stalled on admission and was found drained at a checkpoint gained one segment (≤ 8); `appender_flush_ceiling_overruns` **must stay 0** (KD-SYM-10: a region whose OLDEST dirty slot-tree leaf — stamped at its clean → dirty transition — was older than `appender_flush_ceiling_ms` at a flush pass's covering barrier; a healthy mount under a steady stream at the default cadence never moves it — a parked DEVICE is the legitimate way, and the contract suite does); `appender_flush_ceiling_ms` = the bound in force — the checkpoint LANDING ceiling of the cadence (`CHECKPOINT_MAX_AGE_MS` + two tick periods: 1,100 ms at the shipped 50 ms flush; on a parked cadence the parked tick's, so parking the cadence does not move the gauge either — KD-SYM-10's "within `CHECKPOINT_MAX_AGE_MS`" names the cadence TRIGGER the tick fires AT, and a leaf is durable one landing later); `meta_kv_bitmap_generation_raises` = DUR-4 law 2's raise (a bitmap page image whose generation tied or trailed the newest on-disk copy, written above it with a loud WARN) — the failed-cycle-retry shape, **0 on a healthy mount** including across clean unmount/remount cycles (the mount resumes its checkpoint seq above the bitmap's newest generation, not the ledger's alone); `appender_pressure_cycles` = checkpoint cycles a DECLARED region's ring pressure made due (§4.6 pt 2 per region: its un-reclaimed distance past half its admissible window — a committer parked at a full region ring is drained by the next cadence tick, never by the ceiling; 0 on an unpartitioned mount by construction); `meta_kv_replay_{key,lease,extent}_violations` **must stay 0** — the three per-ring partition classes of §5.3.4 (one key in two rings' windows; a record for a slot the ring's appender does not lease, or the manager's structure in a content ring; an allocator delta in a ring whose appender holds no grant): a mount that counted one REFUSED, so a nonzero value beside a served volume is unreachable by construction. |

`meta_pr_wero` = 1 when the hold is rtype 3 (registrants join under it — `SQUEEZEFS_META_PR_WERO`), 0 for the shipped rtype 1; `manager_failover_bound_ms` = **derived** `CLIENT_STALE_TTL_SECS` + the ladder's measured wall at this open + the ring replay's measured wall — the manager-death row of §1.6, published so the docs cannot drift, and ALSO `manager_dependency_stall_bound_ms`: the grant is sized so foreign-homed appenders keep compacting through that window; `manager_dependency_stalls` **must stay 0 at the sized grant** — an appender that consumed its whole grant while the manager could not refill DEFERRED its compactions (records stay in its ring, never lost; a user commit that needs the extent refuses EAGAIN-class), so growth names the grant knob `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_GRANT_EXTENTS` or an unreachable manager; `extent_grants` / `extent_grant_extents` / `extent_returns` = grant verbs, extents granted, return batches (one control entry each, ring 0), with the closure law `extent_grant_extents ≡ extent_grant_claimed + extent_grant_returned + extent_grant_unclaimed` published as its terms (claimed = images live or parked on the appender's tail; `manager_service_ns` = exact-sum `admit / execute / reply / total` per verb; `manager_verb_replays` = verbs answered `already` off DURABLE state (a `JoinAppender` re-sent to this manager or a successor, a return of extents the record no longer grants — the §5.3.5 idempotency frame engaging, expected under retries); `manager_verb_refusals` **must stay 0** (the durable witness contradicted the caller: appender 0 asked for a grant, a join past `appenders_capacity`, a `ReturnExtents` naming an extent that holds a LIVE image); `manager_verb_rejected` = the OTHER class — frames whose wire integers named what the durable state cannot (a return run outside the volume's extents, an overflowing `start + len`), REJECTED at the service edge before anything proportional to them is allocated ("bounded codec = bounded execution": every wire-carried count is validated against durable state first — an explicit `ExtentGrant { want }` is CLAMPED to the derivation's cap, never trusted; and the slots under that quarantine now — durable in tree 0 across a restart; 0 on every mount that never recovered an early record), `merge_sweep_foreign_skips`, `meta_kv_leaf_lease_refusals` | **Slot leases** (design-symmetric-metadata §5.1 / §11, incompat bit 17 + `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1` — PR 4, dark; per volume, and every one 0 — `dlm_mode` `solo` — on an unarmed or bit-17-absent mount, pinned). `symmetric_meta` = 1 when the plane is armed; `slot_leases_held` = the slots this mount leases (native + the rotor + every slot acquired since); `slot_rotor` = `M` in force (`clamp(W / (2 × writers_known), 1, 64)`, 64 solo; `SQUEEZEFS_SYM_MINT_SLOTS`); `slot_acquires` / `slot_grants` = `AcquireSlot(s)` verbs and fresh grants (a grant moves the slot's `g` once; a replay answers `already` and moves nothing); `slot_offers` = offers made by this holder, split `_idle` (an idle tree — `ops_h` = 0 — offered to the requester that shipped ≥ `N_floor` ops into it) / `_dominated` (a live tree offered to the ONE requester whose ships ≥ 2 × the holder's ops over the common `T_idle` window) / `_expired` (lapsed after one renewal beat unaccepted) — closure `offers ≡ handovers + expired + standing`; `slot_handovers` = accepted offers completed flush-then-transfer, `slot_handover_phase_ns` = exact-sum `flush / page / tree0 / grant / total` (`flush` = the door drain plus the cycles until the departing region's tail passes the slot's record frontier and the root is published — the window is clear of the slot before any other home says it moved); `slot_ships` = served ships counted for dominance (**the crowd is counted here and moves nothing** — twelve thousand single-shot creators read as 12,000 ships and 0 offers); `slot_lru_releases` = idle non-rotor slots released past the page budget (108), `slot_forced_shrinks` = idle rotor slots released when the census grew and `M` fell, `slot_roots_shipped` / `slot_roots_published_for_lessees` = the page-budget overflow law's wire form (PR 12b round 4: roots a joined appender's checkpoint shipped as `PublishRoots` because its page could not hold them / roots this manager wrote into tree 0 for its wire lessees — 0 on every region inside its budget), `slot_region_releases` = declared regions gone `Free` because their last slot went; `slot_lease_conflicts` **must stay 0** (two `Live` pages attesting one slot at tree 0's `g`, or a `Live` entry contradicting tree 0's lessee at or above its `g` — C14's live face, the mount REFUSES), `slot_lease_stale_entries` = `Live` attestations BELOW tree 0's `g` dropped at the arm as residue of a leave or handover the page never recorded; `affinity_a_max_bytes` = the ceiling in force (`max(used_leaf_bytes / 64, node_size)`, or the static knob); `affinity_mints` / `rotor_mints` partition every mint (`≡ mints`), `affinity_ceiling_spills` = children spilled from a parent's over-cap tree to the rotor slot with the most headroom, `affinity_ceiling_overflows` = the extra rotor slots granted when EVERY rotor tree was strictly over the cap (≤ `M` more; `block_ref_probes` = on-demand refcount probes served — one slot tree's `06 ‖ vol ‖ blk` range (`KvMetaBackend::block_ref_probe`; the W1 sole-owner patch's durable clause on an armed set, so it grows with the eligible in-place overwrites there; `shared_release_failures` (**must stay 0**) = a SHARED block's release the index home could not decide (its journal failing) — the reference and the mark KEPT, the block allocated, the failure named and never discarded; `pack_open_scopes` = the open pack blocks' scope population (≤ the leased slots being written on an armed set — up to 64 rotor + affinity packs per meta volume on a solo mount; ≤ 1 per data volume unarmed — PK2's scope); `fsck_shared_index_drift` = confirmed C16 findings, **report-only** and 0 on every healthy set (a SHARED flag without an index entry or an entry without a flag — the clone protocol's crash windows, healed by the block's next release). |

without an armed plane the 45 s grace window is the belt); `dir_rename_lock_acquires` / `dir_rename_lock_wait_ns` = set-wide directory-rename lease takes and the wait for one (a file rename never takes it; every one 0 on an unarmed or bit-17-absent mount, pinned). `dir_striped_dirs` = striped directories this mount FLIPPED, net of the `rmdir`s it completed (a directory another mount flipped is not in its count); `dir_stripe_flips` = flips completed here (automatic, explicit or at `mkdir`); `dir_stripe_supply_rpcs` = stripe inos MINTED as supplies — asked of a creator over the wire or minted by the holder for the remainder (the served side of a wire ask counts here too), `K` per flip; `dir_stripe_ships` = verbs shipped to OTHER stripe holders: a create into a foreign stripe (one shipped insert — closure `dir_stripe_ships + own-stripe creates ≡ creates into striped directories`) plus the `rmdir` protocol's `IsEmpty` / `DestroyStripe`; `dir_stripe_readdir_merges` = K-way `readdir` pages served; `dir_stripe_migrated_names` = names re-homed from a directory's own tree into their stripes after a flip; `dir_stripe_time_batches` = fold persists onto a striped directory's own record (a stripe's newer time written back once per CHECKPOINT CADENCE per directory — the design's per-stripe Δtime batch, as built; never at the `stat` rate); `dir_stripe_dying_refusals` = inserts refused `ENOENT` because their stripe was marked dying (or destroyed) by an `rmdir` — the insert that parked behind the rmdir's guards (R26's closer — legal, never a tripwire); `dir_stripe_rmdirs` = striped directories removed; `dir_stripe_rehomed_on_touch` = names a mutation found in the directory's OWN tree and re-homed into their stripe on the way (a mid-migration name, or a stale-route insert's — the self-healing face of the permanent secondary home); `fsck_stripe_findings` = confirmed C17 findings (**must stay 0**; report-only — see [directory striping](#symmetric-metadata-program--directory-striping-pr-7b-dark)). |
| `dlm_mode` (word), `dlm_rpcs`, `dlm_term`, `dlm_acquire_time` (histogram), `active_leases_count` (gauge), `lease_acquire_{ok,fail}`, `lease_lock_wait` (histogram), `lease_retry_{waits,exhaustions}` | The lock authority (`solo` on every shipped mount; `dlm_rpcs` must stay 0 there), the durable writer era, per-inode lease acquisition and the retry ladder (waits that later won vs the ladder giving up) |

| `dlm_token_grants_served`, `dlm_token_recalls`, `dlm_token_recall_acks`, `dlm_token_recall_expired_with_lease`, `dlm_token_recall_timeouts_live`, `dlm_token_releases`, `dlm_token_recall_batches`, `dlm_token_grant_parks`, `dlm_token_regrant_under_recall_waits` (PR 12b round 4 — grants that found the client's registration under a PENDING recall and waited it out before registering afresh: served on the doomed registration, the token stood at the reader untracked here and was never recalled again; a pass waits for the recalls it ISSUED — `holders_under_recall` — never for a first-touch grant parked behind it), `dlm_token_outstanding`, `dlm_token_recall_fanout_{p50,p99}`, `dlm_token_recall_fanout` (count distribution), `dlm_token_recall_rtt_ns` (exact-sum `send / drain / ack / total`) — the HOLDER side; `dlm_token_grants`, `dlm_token_cached` (gauge), `dlm_token_hits`, `dlm_token_recalls_received`, `dlm_token_recalls_acked`, `dlm_token_reader_releases`, `dlm_token_serve_refusals`, `dlm_token_channel_rounds`, `dlm_token_fetch_retries`, `dlm_token_channel_fresh` (0/1), `dlm_token_channel_alive` (0/1), `dlm_token_grant_rtt_ns`, `dlm_token_cached_bytes` (gauge), `dlm_token_sheds`, `dlm_token_oversize_refusals`, `dlm_token_recall_purged_keys`, `dlm_token_recall_scoped_purges`, `dlm_token_recall_census_purges`, `dlm_token_grant_sessions`, `dlm_token_grant_sessions_dialed`, `dlm_token_reader_holder_planes` (per volume — symmetric PR 12: the per-LISTENER planes the reader dialed for slot holders other than the manager's; 0 solo), `dlm_token_reader_unbound_holders` (PR 12: diverts REFUSED because the object's slot holder has published no endpoint — never the projection; every refusal counts), `dlm_token_reader_holder_resolves` (PR 12: durable holder resolves ATTEMPTED — one appender-directory read + one claim-set `getxattr` each; bounded to one per holder per epoch step by the reader's negative cache, so refusals may outrun resolves by design) — the READER side; `dlm_token_lease_swept_grants`, `dlm_token_not_holder_redirects`, `slot_resolve_dead_redirects` (PR 12b round 3: redirects WITHHELD because the object's lessee is a member the S6 owner lists DEAD — the holder answers `HolderDead`, the reader refuses EAGAIN with no dial, and reads the recovered tree here once the ledger poll lands; a joiner whose per-holder read failed at the holder's address asks the manager's word ONCE, never a second dial), `dlm_token_park_expired_refusals`, `dlm_token_nonmember_refusals` (verbs from a client the installed membership owner does not list — refused BEFORE any grant or registration; **must stay 0** on a fleet whose readers arm through the lease requirement), `dlm_token_custody_rejected` (symmetric PR 9: `CustodyGrant` frames REJECTED at the service edge — a wire word the forest / the durable record does not name; the buggy/hostile-peer class, **must stay 0**) — the holder's lease arm, its slot screen and PR 8's park; `foreign_frames_screened`, `appender_fence_breach`, `foreign_frame_overwrite_detected` — the bset frame screen | **The Token family** (design-symmetric-metadata §5.7 / §11, incompat bit 17 + `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=1` — PR 5, dark; per volume, every one 0 / `null` on an unarmed or bit-17-absent mount, pinned). A foreign object is read under a READ TOKEN from its slot holder, whose grant CARRIES the records. Holder: `grants_served` = grants answered (a re-grant to a holder answers `already` and is counted once more); `recalls` = tokens recalled by the conveyor pass BEFORE the conflicting commit landed — closure **`recalls ≡ recall_acks + recall_expired_with_lease`** (a reader acked, or its membership lease expired and the token died with it); `recall_timeouts_live` **must stay 0** — a LIVE member that did not ack inside its lease TTL (a stuck reader; the commit proceeds, the free-grace ring opens as the timeout path); `releases` = voluntary releases (a reader's eviction or clean leave); `recall_batches` = passes that recalled anything (one batch = the UNION of the batch's objects, recalled once); `grant_parks` = grants parked while the object's recalled commit was in flight (a grant never hands out pre-commit records); `outstanding` = `(object, holder)` pairs held right now; `recall_fanout` = holders per recalled object (`p99` = the broadcast shape's price — 1 writer × R readers reads R); `recall_rtt_ns` = per batch, exact-sum: `send` (the pass's call → the last frame handed to a reader's standing poll), `drain` (→ the last ack's arrival: the readers' in-flight serve drain + R-6 purge + the ack's travel), `ack` (→ the pass observing it), `total`. Reader: `grants` = foreign first touches (0 RPCs after until recalled — `hits`), `cached` = tokens held, `recalls_received` / `recalls_acked` (an ack travels only after the reader's in-flight serves on the object drained and its block-key census was purged), `reader_releases` = tokens released at eviction or the clean leave, `serve_refusals` = serves refused because the reader's lease is past `T_self` or its recall channel is not fresh (a reader that cannot be recalled serves NOTHING from cache), `channel_rounds` = standing-poll rounds, `fetch_retries` = fetches retried because a recall landed mid-fetch, `channel_fresh` = the recall channel completed a round inside its window, `channel_alive` = its task is running (0 after the clean leave — or a reader whose channel task died: it serves nothing and its grants are the holder's lease arm's), `grant_rtt_ns` = one fetch to completion; `cached_bytes` = the ENCODED records the cache holds (attrs, carried xattrs, the dentry set — the R5 component `dlm_token_records_bytes`, budget 1/256 of the R5 budget floored at one control frame; the cache evicts by BYTES, second-chance, each eviction a drained voluntary release), `sheds` = entries the R5 authority DROPPED under pressure (not released — the holder's next recall finds nothing cached), `oversize_refusals` = grants refused because ONE entry would exceed the whole budget (a directory too large for this reader's budget — R5 sizing is the remedy; the streaming form is owed); the recall purge is SCOPED (review round 1, Issue 10): `recall_purged_keys` = block keys purged by the recalled objects' OWN layouts (the stored map values and their free-able bases), `recall_scoped_purges` = objects purged that way, `recall_census_purges` = sink calls that fell back to the WHOLE block-key census — an object the reader held no entry for (shed, or never granted) or an off-record map; ≈ 0 on a healthy reader, growth = the warm read tier being thrown away per recall; `grant_sessions` = the grant session POOL's depth (review round 1, Issue 16 — the D-1b session-depth derivation, one per 8 cores in `[2, 8]`: a grant parked at the holder holds ONE session and every other grant of the volume rides the rest), `grant_sessions_dialed` = pool sessions dialed so far (lazily; ≤ depth). A grant's records are PAGED under one byte budget (half the control frame): the xattrs by name first, the dentries by cookie after, a continuation page re-carrying nothing — so an xattr-heavy directory is served, never a fail-closed encode refusal. Holder's lease arm: `lease_swept_grants` = grants of a member whose membership lease the S6 owner saw EXPIRE — swept at the recall that found it, and at the member's DEPARTURE from the census (the owner's cadence eviction or its clean leave reach every armed holder through the membership departure sink, so a pass parked on a dead reader's recall completes AT the eviction, never at the recall deadline; a token client the owner no longer lists reads `Expired`) — the `recall_expired_with_lease` count's per-grant face, the dead member's OTHER grants gone with it; `not_holder_redirects` = grants answered `NotHolder { holder }` because the object's slot is leased to an appender that is not one of this mount's regions (one lease-table read BEFORE anything is registered or read — the reader fails closed naming the holder; PR 12's per-slot binding re-resolves on it) — never to a lessee the S6 owner lists DEAD: that answer is `HolderDead` (`slot_resolve_dead_redirects`), the retryable class until the recovery makes the slot this manager's (round 3, F2 — the storm leg's reader parked 24 minutes behind a redirect to an address nobody answered at); `park_expired_refusals` = token verbs refused because this holder's appender PARK EXPIRED (PR 8's `park_gate::admits_token_service` — a parked lessee is still the lock master and keeps granting and recalling; an expired park poisoned custody and its slots are the successor's, so every verb refuses and a reader's channel never completes a round against it). The frame screen (§5.8.2, both loaders): `foreign_frames_screened` = frames a load stopped at under the four-rule screen (a generation above the slot's `g_current` off a PR fence, at or past the recorded tail with an older `g`, non-monotone `g` in one log, and — rule 4, review round 1 Issue 18 — the CURRENT generation from an appender that is not the slot's LESSEE: a leased generation has one lessee, so such a frame is a manager bug or a forgery — inert while the slot is UNLEASED, where a release kept `g` and the former lessee's frames stand beside the maintaining manager's; SUSPENDED on a slot whose lessee's page is `Recovering` — from that write to the recovery's tree-0 step, when the next grant moves `g` — so the recoverer's `(0, g)` frames on it load: the suspension admits only a frame at `g` from an appender that is neither the dead lessee nor `0` on a slot it never leased, a corrupted or hostile appender; rules 1–3 stay armed and on PR the recovery's preempt fences the dead lessee's key regardless — PR 10, Issue 33); `appender_fence_breach` **must stay 0 on a PR substrate** (rules 1 and 4 under a device fence: a fenced appender's frame reached the device — the fence did not hold); `foreign_frame_overwrite_detected` = a screened frame with the CURRENT generation's frame behind it — a zombie overwrote an acked frame (§5.8.2's residual class (ii) on a non-PR substrate), refused LOUD rather than served with a hole. |
| `meta_kv_revalidate_ledger_read_bytes`, `meta_kv_revalidate_ledger_full_reads` | The coherent reader's control-plane poll economy (PR 5's predicted-slot-first ledger read): the poll reads the 4 KiB slot the writer's NEXT record must land in (`(adopted seq + 1) % 32`) before anything else — `bytes ÷ meta_kv_revalidate_polls` is the poll's cost (4 KiB idle; `k + 1` slots after `k` writer checkpoints, against the 128 KiB whole-ledger read every S5 poll used to pay); `full_reads` = polls that fell back to the whole-ledger read — a TORN predicted slot (the writer mid-write, or damage) or a bit-8 partitioned volume; steady growth on a solo volume is the torn-slot class. 0 on every write mount. |

#### Writer guard, data plane, multi-writer, membership, grace
