|---|---|
| `writer_guard_{mode,fenced,pr_reacquires,term}` | The single-writer mount guard: guarantee class in force (`flock+pr`, `flock+claim`, `co-writer`, `peer-owned`, …), fenced (tripwire), reservation re-acquires (expected across a kernel-nvmet target power cycle — nvmet has no PTPL; growth on a steadily-serving target is the signal), the durable writer term |
| `pr_registrant_shared`, `data_plane_fence_mode`, `data_plane_wero_reacquires`, `dlm_quarantined_offsets` (gauge), `dlm_quarantine_releases` | Data-plane custody: a shared PR host identity (co-located mounts), whether a WERO hold is device-enforced on the data namespaces, its re-acquires (growth = the target dropped reservations), the dead-epoch do-not-reallocate quarantine and its drain-proof releases |
| `dlm_custody` (object: `mode`, `dlm_custody_held`, `dlm_custody_{grants,renewals,releases,releases_deferred,conflicts,reclaims}`, `dlm_revokes_{issued,expired}`, `dlm_custody_unknown_leases`, `dlm_custody_self_fences`, `dlm_custody_renew_retries` / `dlm_custody_holder_moves` (1.3.0: failed renewals retried at the cadence law over the window left to `T_self` — never the retired 25 ms sleep; leases fenced by the owner's word because the symmetric slot holder re-resolved to another address — 0 unarmed), `dlm_custody_{quarantined_offsets,drain_proofs}`, `dlm_custody_grace_conflicts`, `dlm_rpcs_custody`, `dlm_custody_range_{acquires,extensions}` (the client's wire-face range ledger), `dlm_custody_notice_poll{s,_notices,_failures}` (the standing notice poll: rounds = channel liveness, notices = the quiet-incumbent engagement, failures = rounds that died — session churn), `dlm_custody_via_slot_holder` / `dlm_custody_token_carried` / `dlm_custody_token_carried_pages` / `dlm_custody_token_carry_failures` / `dlm_custody_holder_fences` / `dlm_custody_recalls_absorbed` / `dlm_custody_recalled` / `dlm_custody_stale_holder_grants` (symmetric PR 9: grants served by a file's slot HOLDER rather than the set authority; those whose reply carried the file's token; xattr pages a carried token fetched past its first; carried installs that failed after custody was granted (the lease kept); slot-holder clients that fenced their OWN custody at `T_self` (must-stay-0); handover recall notices absorbed; grants this process's authorities recalled for handovers; grants answered by an appender that no longer held the slot — a request resolved before a move and served one RTT after — released at once and re-acquired where tree 0 points (≈ 0: at most one per handover racing an in-flight acquire) — 0 unarmed and for every own file), `dlm_custody_grant_census` (opt-in)), `dlm_custody_generation`, `dlm_custody_epoch_advances`, `dlm_custody_phase_ns` (`rtt`, `arbitrate`, `adopt`, `renew`) | Remote write custody on an armed multi-writer plane — all `0`/`off` on a single-writer mount by construction ([Multi-writer data plane](#multi-writer-data-plane-dlm-stage-s9)); | `free_grace_mode` (word — `off` alone on an unarmed mount; the rest of the family appears only with the plane armed): `free_grace_{deferrals,releases,offsets,bytes}`, `free_grace_bound`, `free_grace_{fence_bound_ms,fence_bound_base_ms,pressure_bound_ms,ring_cap}`, `free_grace_members`, `free_grace_{forced_releases,laggard_fences,alloc_stalls,pressure_parks}`, `free_grace_pressure_pct`, `free_grace_{prods,prod_renew_ms,prod_decays,prods_caught_up,bound_tightenings}`, `free_grace_demand_{pct,prods,waits}`, `free_grace_bound_refreshes`, `free_grace_bound_age_ms`, `free_grace_residence_ms`, `free_grace_hold_phase_ns` (`defer_checkpointed`, `checkpointed_min_acked`, `min_acked_released`, `total`), `free_grace_hold_{unplaced,ms}`, `free_grace_checkpoint_{marks,cycle_ms,ceiling_ms,elastic_cycles}`, `free_grace_member_ack_lag_ms` (+ the gated `_census`), `free_grace_bound_refreshes_on_ack`, `free_grace_bound_scan_ms`, `free_grace_lane_push_{releases,hints}`; **reader / co-writer side** (`free_grace_mode` = `reader`): `free_grace_reader_acks`, `free_grace_{learned,acked,reader_pending}_label`, `free_grace_ack_renewals`, `free_grace_pass_{prods,interval_ms}`, `free_grace_ack_pipeline_depth`, `free_grace_acked_lag_ms`, `free_grace_qualify_lag_ms`, `free_grace_advertised_ceiling_ms`, `free_grace_drain_{lag_ms,observed,overdue,wakes}`, `reader_layout_step_{gen,misses}`, `reader_serves_inflight`, `reader_serve_{step_races,slot_overruns}`, `free_grace_lane_push_wakes` / `free_grace_lane_supply_hint` | The freed-offset grace period and its pressure valve ([Freed-offset grace period](#freed-offset-grace-period-spec-68-item-3) — every key's healthy reading is in that section's table); closure law `deferrals ≡ releases + offsets` |

| `free_grace_recall_gated_frees`, `free_grace_recall_timeout_deferrals`, `free_grace_s5_reader_deferrals`, `free_grace_s5_class_scans` | **Recall-driven free-grace** (design-symmetric-metadata §5.7.3 — PR 5, dark; 0 on every mount without an armed token holder). Under GPFS-strict tokens the freeing publish recalled every reader's token before it committed and a reader acks only after its in-flight DMA on the ino drained and its block-key census was purged — so the recall IS the qualification: `recall_gated_frees` = terminal frees that published to the free list DIRECTLY (no ring, no deferral; `free_grace_hold_ms` for them is the recall round trip, `dlm_token_recall_rtt_ns.total`); `recall_timeout_deferrals` = frees that rode the grace RING because a LIVE member's recall was unacked past the bound (its own key — PR 8's `free_grace_timeout_deferrals` is the RING's forced/tightened releases, Σ per data volume) (`dlm_token_recall_timeouts_live`'s window, open for `T_owner` — the ring is the timeout path, released by its own law: the acknowledgement, the fence at `T_owner`); every ring deferral still counts `free_grace_deferrals`, so the closure `deferrals ≡ releases + offsets` holds on both paths. The gate is per READER CLASS (review round 1, Issue 3): a free waits on token acks for the token readers AND on the ring's epoch qualification for any `SQUEEZEFS_SYMMETRIC_META=0` S5 member-reader still enrolled (the membership census's live readers minus the token clients the holder has served) — `s5_reader_deferrals` counts the frees that rode the ring for such a reader; the ring bypass applies only when NO S5 reader is enrolled, so a mixed set keeps the S5 reader's protection verbatim; the verdict is CACHED against the membership census generation and the token-client registry's generation — `s5_class_scans` counts the O(members) recomputes (≈ the census changes, never the frees; a per-free growth is the cache not keying). |

#### Layouts, publish, block-map tree

| Keys | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `layout_{inline,staged,striped}_writes` | Writes by layout class (inline up to `inline_max_bytes` / locally staged / striped blocks) |
| `layout_promoted_{inline,block,packed}` | **Staged-file PROMOTION outcomes**, every promoter (staging pressure, the dismount pass, the fsync lever): `DataRouter::promote_staged_file` dispatches on size — a file that fits the inline ceiling promotes INTO INLINE (its payload becomes the layout record, no block), a larger one into a slot of the shared open pack block (`packed`, the default since PK7) or into one whole block (`block` — the `SQUEEZEFS_SMALL_FILE_PACKING=0` arm, and any slot above `pack_max_slot_bytes`). `packed + block + inline ≡ promotions` on every row; `packed` is the packing engagement instrument |
| `dismount_promoted_{files,inline_files,bytes}`, `dismount_promote_failures` | **The clean-unmount promotion pass** (`.benchmarks/2026-09-09-dismount-staged-residue.md` §7): staged-layout files the dismount teardown promoted to the shared backend (`inline_files` ⊆ `files` — the ones the size dispatch sent inline) and the ones whose promotion FAILED (they stay resident, recovered by the next mount at this mount point; pairs with the ERROR line naming the first causes). Engagement: a clean unmount of a mount holding staged-layout files must move `files` by that population |
| `fsync_promoted_{files,inline_files,bytes}`, `fsync_promote_failures`, `fsync_promote_noops` | **The fsync-time promotion lever** (`SQUEEZEFS_FSYNC_PROMOTE_STAGED`, default off — all 0 with the lever off): staged-layout files an fsync promoted, promotions that FAILED (the fsync still succeeded on the local durability; the entry stays resident, WARN-logged), and NO-OPS — an fsync that found the entry already promoted by a racing promoter (exactly-once by the generation-gated commit). The leg's time is `fsync_phase_ns.staged_promote` |
| `layout_publish_{batches,batched_blocks}` | Per-inode publish coalescing: blocks ÷ batches is the live coalesce factor (≈ 1 on a streaming shape = the conveyor regressed to per-block publishes) |
| `layout_{delta,full}_commits`, `layout_delta_{bytes,folds}`, `publish_full_save_{indirect,chain_cap,other}` | Layout delta records vs full re-base saves, and why a full save happened |
| `publish_phase_ns` (exact-sum: `queue_wait`, `lock_wait`, `base_fetch`, `apply`, `save_encode`, `blob_write`, `meta_commit`, `commit_{guard,inode_read,slot_probe,tx_wait}`, `total`) | The publish path's residence decomposition |
| `publish_base_{dirty_serves,ram_serves,fetches}` | Where the read-modify-write base came from (closes exactly against the pass count; steady rewrite runs `ram_serves ≈ passes`) |
| `publish_commit_group{s,_saves}` | Delta-class layout saves aggregated into one multi-inode transaction per conveyor window (saves ÷ groups = the live aggregation factor) |
| `publish_indirect_blob_bytes`, `layout_indirect_map_read{s,_bytes}`, `publish_blob_{composes,orphan_reclaims,orphan_reclaim_dedups,foreign_free_skips}`, `publish_compose_spills`, `publish_stale_binding_drops` | The legacy indirect-blob map path (trends to 0 on tree-mapped volumes), its compose/orphan hygiene (`orphan_reclaim_dedups` = own-mint blob reclaims DECLINED because the lineage was already closed — a stale clone of a reclaimed entry re-inserted; before it each one shipped a free the authority refused), and a served publish dropping entries stamped with a dead block incarnation (the shield working under stale-cache churn) |
| `publish_map_record_bytes`, `map_migrate_{inos,records,resumed}`, `map_sweep_{jobs,chunks,records,resumed}`, `kvmap_write_map_bytes` (gauge), `kvmap_write_map_budget_bytes`, `kvmap_partial_inos` (gauge), `kvmap_{overlay,window}_saves`, `block_map_window_{bytes,hits,fills,evictions}` | The block-map tree ([Large files](#large-files--the-block-map-tree)) |
| `unpublished_offsets_recovered` | Pre-allocated-but-unpublished destinations recovered at mount |

#### Small-file packing (`pack_*`, `packed_*`, `layout_promoted_*`)

Design `docs/design-small-file-packing.md`; acceptance `.benchmarks/2026-09-10-packing-rows-squeeze-test.md`. The posture words are `small_file_packing` / `pack_max_slot_bytes` at the top level (above). Every counter here is 0 on a mount that never promoted a staged-layout file.

| Keys | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `layout_promoted_packed` (above), `pack_own_block_promotions`, `pack_promoted_bytes`, `pack_slot_pad_bytes` | Tenants packed into a shared open pack block (the engagement instrument); promotions whose slot exceeded `pack_max_slot_bytes` and took their own block (0 on a ≤ `block_size/2` population); image bytes packed and the LBA-grain pad those slots carried (`slot − image`) — `pad ÷ bytes` is the 4 KiB-grain internal-fragmentation tax |
| `pack_blocks_opened`, `pack_blocks_sealed_{full,dismount,drain,batch}`, `pack_blocks_abandoned`, `pack_slots_abandoned`, `pack_reservation_refills`, `pack_refill_storage_full`, `pack_arm_stopped` (word, `true`/`false`), `pack_arm_stopped_promotions` | The open-block life cycle: blocks opened by a refill (`reservation_refills` ≈ `blocks_opened`, single-flighted), sealed because FULL (the overflowing reservation), at DISMOUNT, by the drain mover (an open pack on a draining volume would keep taking tenants the drain then has to move again), or at a co-writer batch's frame reply; ABANDONED = a seal whose pin release was terminal with no committed tenant (≈ 0); `slots_abandoned` = slots whose tenant commit was refused or whose DMA failed (dead space until compaction). `refill_storage_full` counts refills that met `StorageFull` — the pack arm then STOPS for the batch (`pack_arm_stopped`) and promotions are answered resident-and-counted (`arm_stopped_promotions`) with one aggregated WARN |
| `pack_open_blocks` (gauge), `pack_open_block_age_ms`, `pack_open_block_occupancy` (gauges) | Open pack blocks right now, the oldest one's age and its occupancy (the census `pack_open_blocks_census` names them) |
| `pack_partial_frees`, `pack_terminal_frees`, `pack_release_untracked_noops` (tripwire on a single-writer mount) | Tenant releases through the router ladder: nonterminal (the block stays allocated for its siblings) vs the block's LAST (`partial + terminal ≡ tenant releases`); `release_untracked_noops` = a release on an entry already retired — legitimate only on a co-writer whose tenants were all deleted between the group landing and the seal |
| `pack_mapping_clips` | Passthrough truncate-shrinks of a size-carrying mapping re-described in place (`base:off:len'`) with no data-plane I/O — the durable population and the RAM refcount unchanged (the transformed re-encode arm keeps counting on `staged_truncate_durable_clips`) |
| `pack_mover_{open,resident}_defers` | The VL4 mover (`move_one`) deferring an OPEN pack block (the packer is still filling it — a drain victim's open pack is then SEALED, `pack_blocks_sealed_drain`) / a staged-layout tenant whose `file_id` still has a RESIDENT ring entry (a newer image is about to supersede the durable tenant) |
| `pack_cowriter_{batches,frames,group_refusals,group_splits,group_unavailable}`, `pack_batch_{frames,tenants,volume_splits}`, `served_pack_group_{frames,splits,unavailable}`, `served_publish_free_block_refusals` (tripwire) | **The co-writer batch pack (PK4)**: promotion batches the group driver ran and the `pack_group` frames shipped; frames whose group LANDED and the tenants they committed (`tenants ÷ frames` = the live pack width — a frame per tenant means the driver regressed to per-file commits); batches split per `(owner endpoint, home meta volume)` (counted when > 1); promotions that fell back to one-block-per-file because the owner refused a frame `UNAVAILABLE` (the `SQUEEZEFS_PUBLISH_CONVEYOR_GROUP=0` arm or an older owner), refused `SPLIT` a second time (a `migrate-meta-slot` in flight), or because the membership grant does not advertise pack groups. The owner's faces: frames received / refused SPLIT / refused UNAVAILABLE, and the served-publish screen refusing a publish that adopts a block the authority holds released |
| `packed_reads`, `packed_read_bytes`, `packed_mapping_refusals` (tripwire) | Reads of size-carrying `bk:off:len` mappings through the one ranged, routed read funnel (`DataRouter::read_mapping_window` — every promoted staged file's device read, packed or not; a packed-read row is INVALID unless `packed_reads` accounts for its reads), the device window those reads cost (`≤ Σ ceil(image)` is the amplification bound), and the malformed-mapping refusals |
| `ipc_direct_packed_{serves,bytes}`, `ipc_direct_ineligible_packed_shape` | **PK8** — the LD_PRELOAD direct-drive READ prelude's packed-tenant arm (`SQUEEZEFS_IPC_DD_PACKED`, default on): direct-drive serves whose binding was a packed tenant (⊆ `ipc_direct_drive_serves`; 0 under `=0`), the request bytes they completed, and requests refused to the handler because the window was not strictly inside the tenant image (short-read / zero-fill semantics) |
| `frag_d1_pack_blocks`, `frag_d1_pack_occupancy` / `frag_d1_pack_occupancy_mean` (`null` until measured), `pack_reclaimable_bytes`, `pack_blocks_below_half` | **The D1 pack face** (walk-priced — refreshed by `defrag --report-only` / `--pack` and after every compaction pass): pack blocks measured (every block whose referencers are all staged-layout tenants, the legacy one-tenant `bk:0:len` block included), the least-occupied block's and the mean occupancy (live slot bytes ÷ block size; a clone/prefix share counts its slot once), `Σ (block − live)` over them, and how many sit at or below the half-block victim line — what `defrag --pack` can reclaim |
| `pack_compactions`, `pack_compaction_{tenants_moved,windows_copied,bytes_copied,bytes_reclaimed,blocks_freed,deferred}` | **The compaction mover** (`JobType::DefragPack`, `defrag --pack`): passes that executed a plan; tenants republished into fresh slots; distinct windows copied (`≤ tenants_moved` — both share classes copy once per offset); image bytes copied vs `Σ (block − live)` over the victims freed (`copied ≤ reclaimed` on every pass — the break-even law); victims freed through the terminal ladder at population 0; victims deferred (an open pack, a ring-resident tenant, an unstable word, a stopped pack arm) |
| `fsck_pack_ledger_exempted`, `fsck_tenant_overlap_findings` (tripwire), `fsck_repair_classC12` (structurally 0) | fsck's pack-open ledger shield (the +1 pin of an OPEN pack block exempted from C2/C3 — the tenants' transient references ride `fsck_inflight_exempted`) and class C12 (report-only: two tenants overlapping at different offsets means at least one is wrong and nothing on the volume says which) |

#### Write path

| Keys | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `put_obj`, `get_obj`, `del_obj`, `fuse_ops` | Raw op counts (`get_obj` counts ranged reads by design) |
| `write_through_{blocks,bytes,fallbacks}`, `write_through_inplace_{overwrites,rewrites}` | Complete-block write-through past staging; never-lossy staging fallbacks; in-place full-block overwrites (`SQUEEZEFS_INPLACE_OVERWRITE`) and the brim in-place rewrite taken only at genuine space pressure |
| `write_path_seed_read_bytes`, `flush_seed_read_bytes`, `overwrite_seed_{materialized,skipped,deferred}` | Seed reads on the write path (**`write_path_seed_read_bytes` must stay 0** — a fully-covered buffer never pays a seed) and the overwrite-seed decision ledger |
| `active_block_ooo_runs`, `active_block_cow_copies`, `active_block_memset_elided_bytes`, `write_block_revisits`, `striped_block_concurrency` (gauge) | Kernel-split out-of-order WRITE segments (normal), CoW copies of a shared active buffer, zero-fills elided, block revisits at checkout, and the derived striped-I/O concurrency cap in force (`clamp(cores × 2, 4, 64)`) |
| `write_enospc_refusals` | WRITE replies refused `ENOSPC` — the synchronous face of exhaustion (a promotion or sparse-stripe mint the write itself owns; custody the never-lossy ladder ACKed reports at `fsync`, not here). A write-path allocation refusal is TERMINAL for that write since 2026-09-06 (`.benchmarks/2026-09-06-cowriter-enospc-wedge.md`): the park on grace-held space is bounded in wall time, then `ENOSPC` stands |
| `patch_{writes,write_bytes,dma_errors}`, `patch_edge_rmw_reads` (0 by definition), `patch_ineligible_{unmapped,decorated,unaligned,overlay,device_overlay,shared,range_shared,transform,adjacent,oversize,posture,foreign_custody}` | The sole-owner in-place patch and its decision ledger — growth of an `ineligible_*` arm on a shape that should patch is predicate rot ([Random-small-write path](#random-small-write-path-sole-owner-patch--extent-overlay-design-docsdesign-random-small-writesmd)); `foreign_custody` (symmetric PR 9) is the one arm whose growth is a PRICED cost, not rot — a file whose custody this mount holds from its slot holder pays a whole-block CoW per sub-block overwrite until the grant carries a per-block reference summary ([PR 9](#symmetric-metadata-program--custody-by-the-slot-holder-pr-9-dark)) |
| `parked_extent_bytes`, `parked_full_buffer_bytes` (gauges), `extent_{parks,escalations,implicit_escalations,spills,spill_bytes,record_absorbs}`, `fold_{passes,seed_reads,extents_folded}`, `fold_fill` (histogram, median ≥ 16), `staged_rider_{extent_writes,folds}`, `extent_records_{recovered,stale_discarded,torn_discarded,future_refused,foreign_scope}` | The extent overlay, batched fold and staged extent records (recovery classes kept distinct; `foreign_scope` = another writer's records skipped by the mount sweep) |
| `overlay_{stores,store_bytes,short_stores,store_fallbacks,store_submits_qids}`, `overlay_ack_early_{stores,bytes,retries,lost}`, `overlay_governed_stores`, `overlay_{installs,overwrite_installs,overwrite_bytes}`, `overlay_{publishes,published_bytes,unpublished_at_fsync}`, `overlay_{epoch_feeds,feed_fallbacks,supersessions,superseded_by_merge,claim_conflicts,window_escalations,enospc_declines,mover_skips,teardown_waits}`, `overlay_ineligible_{sub_cap,shadow_bound,range_shared}`, `overlay_gap_{seeds,seed_bytes,seed_old_bytes,seed_ranged_bytes,seed_read_bytes}`, `overlay_read_{serves,serve_bytes,drains,gap_serves,gap_bytes}`, `overlay_dma_{passthrough,pool_copy}_bytes`, `overlay_inflight_bytes` (gauge), `overlay_open` (gauge), `overlay_fence_drops` (tripwire), `overlay_superseded_by_merge`, `overlay_superseded_by_served_publish`, `overlay_superseded_dead_old_binding` | The device-backed write overlay: stores and their ACK-early half, admission through the depth governor, the overwrite arm, publication, supersession, the length floor (`ineligible_sub_cap` ≈ per sub-cap aligned write on an armed mount), old-image seeding at settle (`gap_seed_old_bytes` growing on a rand-4k row is predicate rot; W-6: `seed_ranged_bytes` ⊆ `seed_old_bytes` is the `SQUEEZEFS_GAP_SEED_RANGED` lever's engagement — the seed read exactly the uncovered span — and `seed_read_bytes` the device bytes those seeds cost, the amplification numerator), reads served from unpublished overlay bytes, and the DMA vehicle split. The three `superseded_*` arms: `by_merge` is the discarding-class belt (a `TruncateFrom`/`RemoveBlocks` reaching the primitive un-drained; a FOREIGN un-marked `Merge` additionally trips `invariant_tripwires` under the label `overlay_foreign_merge` — that pair must stay 0, it is the 2026-09-10 data-loss class `.benchmarks/2026-09-10-overlay-vs-growth-merge-data-loss.md` whose guard-less writer was deleted), `by_served_publish` is finding 51's served-publish screen (an authority's open overlay record superseded at a co-writer's served commit that displaced its block — a LEGAL peer publisher, so never the tripwire), and `dead_old_binding` its belt (a settle that found its captured old binding naming a dead lifetime — 0 while the screen catches every displacement) |
| `write_pipeline_{inflight_blocks,inflight_bytes,depth_target,depth_target_base,admission_waits,admission_tick_wakes,depth_probe_ups,depth_probe_backoffs,supersessions,superseded_bytes,fence_drops}`, `write_pipeline_phase_ns` (exact-sum: `admit_wait`, `detach_lag`, `lock_wait`, `crypto`, `allocate`, `dma`, `publish`, `displaced_free`, `inval_tail`, `total`; plus `dev_queue` / `dev_service`, the DMA phase's split at the NvmeBlockDev funnel) | The write-pipeline depth governor (the runtime bandwidth-delay target and its probe-up multiplier; `admission_waits` is the honest-backpressure gauge) and the ten-phase residence histogram |
| `rewrite_{blocks,user_bytes,device_write_bytes}`, `rewrite_shadow_{swaps,bytes,parked_bytes,fallbacks,superseded,fence_drops,open_epochs,close_retries}` | The rewrite program: `device_write_bytes ÷ user_bytes` is the vehicle-blind amplification (gate ≤ 1.05); shadow swaps with `open_epochs` → 0 at quiesce; `close_retries` = epoch closes whose token was superseded by a process-local lease rotation and that converged by re-presenting the inode's current generation (the multi-handle fsync-racing-sibling shape; one extra fencing revalidation per close) |
| `rewrite_shadow_supply_closes` / `rewrite_shadow_supply_close_blocks` (co-writer side) | the supply-coupled epoch close (`SQUEEZEFS_REWRITE_SUPPLY_CLOSE`; finding 15's parked-supply term, `.benchmarks/2026-09-07-rewrite-epoch-supply-close.md`): epoch closes the lane refill tick fired because the lane's reachable supply sat below its watermark (⊆ `rewrite_shadow_swaps`), and the parked A keys they released into the recycle loop. **A rewriting co-writer whose `alloc_lane_enospc_refusals` grow while these stay flat is parking its displaced supply to the iteration boundary** — read the decline ledger next. Both 0 on every single-writer / authority mount by construction |
| `rewrite_shadow_supply_close_declined_covered` / `rewrite_shadow_supply_close_declined_no_parked` / `rewrite_shadow_supply_close_bounded` | the close's decision ledger, one entry per refill tick: `covered` = the lane held at least one loop transit's supply (`reachable ≥ watermark`, or a quiet writer's watermark 0) — the healthy co-writer's per-second beat, and growing BESIDE lane ENOSPC refusals means the watermark is not reading the starvation; `no_parked` = starving with no epoch parking anything — nothing to inject, the KD-1.7-only shape; `bounded` = candidate epochs a tick left open because larger ones already covered the deficit (the per-tick bound engaging on many small epochs) — growing beside lane ENOSPC refusals means the deficit under-reads the need. Nothing moves with the lever off |
| `write_lock_wait`, `write_lock_wait_{exclusive,shared}` (histograms), `write_lock_scope_{entire,metaprep,shared}`, `write_lock_scope_shared_upgrades`, `write_lock_candidate_{entire,metaprep,shared}` | Inode write-guard acquisition: waits by mode, the scope each write took vs the scope it was classified for, and shared-to-exclusive upgrades |
| `write_lock_hold_{shared,metaprep,entire}` (histograms, exact `sum_ns`/`count`) | Inode write-guard HOLD per final scope — acquisition → drop (W-2 write-stream-guard, 2026-09-05). Closure: Σ counts ≡ Σ `write_lock_scope_*`. `shared`/`metaprep` are meta-prep only (µs — the P1-8 tripwire: a device-length value there means the guard is held across a DMA); `entire` legitimately spans the inline/staged commit and layout promotions |
| `block_lock_wait` (histogram) | Per-block flush-lock waits (`block_lock_wait_by_site`, the per-call-site attribution, rides `SQUEEZEFS_OP_PROFILE=1` — see the FUSE transport table) |
| `nt_copy_bytes`, `numa_{local,remote}_bytes`, `numa_nodes`, `aligned_pool_{hits,misses}` | Non-temporal-store engagement at the DMA-destined copy sites; the NUMA-crossing estimate for daemon copy passes (`numa_nodes` = 1 ⇒ structurally inert); aligned-buffer pool recycling (misses ÷ total ≈ 0 is healthy) |
| `writeback_queue_depth` (gauge), `writeback_enqueued_{drain,flush,teardown,wt_fallback}`, `writeback_{superseded_noops,stale_token_retries,fence_noops,orphan_discards,retry_exhaustions}`, `writeback_errors_{latched,reported}` | The never-lossy writeback queue: admissions by driver, supersession-aware outcomes (fencing-stale ⇒ no-op, reclaimed-ino ⇒ orphan discard, transient ⇒ retry), and close-time error reporting (`latched − reported` = failures still waiting for an fsync to consume them) |
| `durable_upload_bytes_{writeback,self_flush,escalation}` | Durable block uploads by driver: the per-block flush unit, the memory-budget-Red self-flush, staging-refusal escalations |
| `staging_put_bytes_{drain,flush,teardown,wt_fallback}`, `staging_drain_barriers`, `spill_{seed_reads,seed_read_bytes,staging_puts,staging_put_bytes,pass_cacheless_skips}`, `restage_churn_{removes,bytes}`, `staging_sibling_{probes,hops_elided}`, `staged_rmw_pooled_seeds`, `staged_spill_escalations`, `staged_identity_retries`, `staged_payload_lost_reads`, `staged_truncate_{inplace_shrinks,durable_clips}` | Local NVMe staging: puts by driver, inline victim spills (the foreground writer paying a victim's seed), same-key re-stage churn, the staged-sibling probe (latch-free; hops elided when the occupancy index says absent), never-lossy StorageFull escalations, and truncate-shrink correctness arms. **`staged_payload_lost_reads` nonzero after a crash remount = acked-unfsynced staged data was lost and served as zeros (degraded, never errored)** |
| `staging_generation_discards`, `staging_writer_scope` (word), `staging_scope_upgrades`, `staging_foreign_scope_{records,refusals,discards}` | Staging roots bound to the filesystem generation and the node's writer scope: dead-generation wipes, the scope word, one-time scope upgrades, and the foreign-scope tripwires |
| `nvme_staging_{current,max}_bytes`, `nvme_staged_write_file_count`, `nvme_staged_write_file_ids` (census), `nvme_unaligned_write_fallbacks`, `active_write_block_count`, `active_writes` (census) | Staging tier occupancy, unflushed staged writes (`squeezefs umount`'s check), unaligned staging fallbacks, live active-write blocks |
| `bg_admit_capacity`, `bg_admit_available_permits` (gauges), `bg_spawn_{admitted,rejected}` | Best-effort background-task admission |
| `block_free_{discards,discard_bytes,file_punches,punch_bytes,trim_discards,trim_bytes,reclaim_skipped}`, `block_free_reclaim_{queued,queue_bytes,batches,commands,sync_drains,supply_drains,cap_parks,cap_overflow,elided,fence_halts,drain_rate,arrival_rate,queue_cap,park_bound_ms,park_tick_wakes}`, `block_free_elided_debt_bytes`, `block_free_debt_{drain_passes,pressure_drains}`, `alloc_trim_window_parks` | Device deallocation: discards/punches issued (blocks ÷ `commands` = the coalesce factor), the background reclaim queue (`sync_drains` must stay 0 except under real space pressure; `supply_drains` = fill-coupled pacing engaging at high fill; `cap_parks` = at-cap enqueues parked, `cap_overflow` ≈ 0 while the drain keeps pace — the norm when displacement outruns the target's deallocate service, each costing the write path one `park_bound_ms`; W-4: `drain_rate`/`arrival_rate` blocks/s are the derivation's measured inputs, `queue_cap`/`park_bound_ms` its live outputs, `park_tick_wakes` ≈ 0 = parks resume on room edges, not the tick), the discard-elision debt ledger (`queued + elided ≡ terminal frees`) and its drainer's pacing (bounded growth at idle); `alloc_trim_window_parks` = allocations that found the free list entirely inside a trim CLAIM WINDOW and parked for its return edge instead of refusing `StorageFull` — each one a spurious ENOSPC that was not (≈ 0 except on a full store under rewrite) |
| `data_device_sync_requests`, `data_device_syncs` | Data-device barrier requests vs barriers issued (the difference is coalesced work; a ratio collapsing to 1.0 under concurrent fsync means coalescing stopped) |
| `fsync_phase_ns` (exact-sum: `intent_barrier`, `data_flush`, `staged_promote`, `data_barrier`, `meta_publish`, `meta_barrier`, `extent_barrier`, `total`), `fsync_calls`, `fsync_noop_clean`, `fsync_data_namespaces_{touched,flushed}`, `fsync_write_through_skips`, `fsync_touched_unresolved`, `fsync_parallel_joins` | The fsync ladder's decomposition (every phase records once per fsync, 0 ns for an absent leg — `staged_promote` is the `SQUEEZEFS_FSYNC_PROMOTE_STAGED` leg, ≈ 0 with the lever off; `Σ legs ≡ total`) and the touched-namespace ledger: `flushed ÷ touched ÷ calls` against the namespace count (`data_volume_write_cache`) is the measured "flushes ALL namespaces" ratio; `noop_clean` = fsyncs whose data legs found nothing to barrier (the meta barrier still runs); `write_through_skips` = touched namespaces whose cache is write-through; `touched_unresolved` (a stamp that named no listed device — the all-namespace fallback) should stay 0; `parallel_joins` = barrier steps that joined ≥ 2 legs |
| `data_read_lanes`, `data_write_lanes`, `data_write_lane_submits` | Per-device read/write submission lanes armed at mount and the write lanes' per-lane engagement |
| `dev_{enters,fills,submit_enters,wake_batches,wake_writes,wakes_elided}` | The NvmeBlockDev lane worker: `io_uring_enter` calls vs fills (enters ÷ fills ≪ 1 under concurrency), request-arrival wakes coalesced |
| `compress_stored_raw` | Incompressible blocks stored raw (best-effort compression) |
| `evacuate_{blocks_moved,bytes_moved,shared_blocks_moved,inflight_bytes,stale_token_noops,deferred_staged_blocks,replans,needed_bytes,avail_bytes,transient_bytes}`, `volume_preflight_refusals` | Volume drain/evacuation (a drain row is invalid unless `evacuate_bytes_moved` accounts for the victim's used bytes) and capacity-preflight refusals |

#### Fabric, zcrx lane

| Keys | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `fabric_controllers`, `fabric_ctrl_not_live`, `fabric_ctrl_reconnects` | NVMe-oF initiator state sampled from sysfs ([Fabric observability](#fabric-observability)) |
| `zcrx_lane_armed`, `zcrx_{fills,fill_bytes,gather_bytes,dest_gather_bytes,hdr_copy_bytes,fill_fallbacks}`, `zcrx_area_bytes`, `zcrx_area_admission_waits`, `zcrx_{recv_parks,recv_failovers,conn_errors,degraded_bypasses,structural_teardowns,starved_ms}`, `zcrx_parks_{cq_full,pool_dry,rq_empty}`, `zcrx_frame_violations`, `zcrx_lane_poisoned` | The opt-in zero-copy-receive read lane (`SQUEEZEFS_ZCRX_LANE`; engagement law `gather ≡ fill`; `lane_poisoned` is the tripwire — `docs/design-zcrx-read-lane.md`) |

### `df` / statfs semantics

A mounted SqueezeFS reports honest, cheap numbers to `statfs(2)` (`df`): **total** is the formatted capacity — the summed data-backend size, or the lower explicit `--capacity` quota chosen at format (the effective limit you experience); **used/free** track the bytes currently allocated on the striped block backends, maintained by the block allocators at alloc/free time (no metadata transactions or device I/O on the statfs path). Tiny inline payloads live in the metadata volume and staged-but-unpromoted small writes in the local NVMe staging dirs, so those transient bytes appear in `df` as their blocks promote via writeback rather than instantaneously; deletes return space after background reclaim completes. Inode columns (`df -i`) report the format inode quota against the v3 monotonic, no-reuse inode watermark — `IFree` is remaining create headroom, and deleting files does not raise it.

The **`squeezefs df`** verb answers the same accounting **offline** — read-only probes over the volume set, no mount required (`squeezefs df -g sqmeta://<meta_dev> [--json]`; see above). It reports the durable point-in-time state: beside a live mount, bytes still in flight through staging/journal deferral appear once durable.

### Fabric observability

Target side — **`nvmeof target status [--json]`** is the kernel-nvmet health verb: module presence (`nvmet`/`nvmet-tcp`), the configfs mount, and the subsystem / namespace / port counts with the count of namespaces carrying `resv_enable=1` (the enforcement-grade PR posture); `nvmeof list [--json]` carries the ledger reconciliation (`managed/down/pending/removing/foreign`, plus `spdk — RETIRED target stack` for a record the retired SPDK stack left behind). (The retired SPDK stack's RPC-liveness / version-drift / reactor-busy / hugepage / `ptpl_files` gauges left with it — R-SYM-8.) Initiator side — mounts whose meta/data devices are fabric-attached expose the **`fabric_*`** family on the `.stats` inode, sampled from sysfs at the stats cadence: `fabric_controllers`, `fabric_ctrl_not_live` (controllers in `connecting`/`resetting` — the reconnect-storm detector; measured storms run at 10 s cadence for ~10 min), and `fabric_ctrl_reconnects` — a **sampled-transition counter**, not a kernel counter: sysfs exposes only instantaneous controller state, so this counts observed `live → connecting/resetting` transitions at the sampling cadence and **undercounts flaps faster than it** (fine for a storm detector; do not "fix" it against a nonexistent kernel counter). Controller identity is renumbering-stable (transport + subsysnqn + target endpoint — `nvme3` can die and reattach as `nvme7` and still counts as a reconnect, not a new controller). `squeezefs status <sqmeta-uri>` reports the same data as a per-volume `"Fabric"` section when the backing device is fabric-attached. Guard signals stay `writer_guard_{mode,fenced,pr_reacquires}`: across a kernel-nvmet target power cycle `pr_reacquires` growth is *expected* (nvmet has no PTPL; the heartbeat re-check law covers it), while growth on a target that never restarted is the signal to read (a registration the guard had to re-establish under a serving target).

## NVMe-oF operations

> **SPDK retired as an NVMe-oF target — 2026-09-12, owner ruling R-SYM-8** ([`docs/design-symmetric-metadata.md`](design-symmetric-metadata.md) §5.8.1, KD-SYM-23; forward-only). **The kernel `nvmet` target (the sqz-kernel target) is THE target.** SPDK's compiled-in `SPDK_NVMF_MAX_NUM_REGISTRANTS = 16` was the only hard registrant ceiling SqueezeFS ever shipped — the fencing-group machinery that existed to fit 12,500 hosts under it left the program with it; nvmet's registrant list is unbounded. What refuses now, loud and naming nvmet + the re-share sequence: `--target-stack spdk`, `SQUEEZEFS_NVMEOF_TARGET_STACK=spdk`, `nvmeof target install` (SPDK-only by definition — a retired verb naming `target setup`/`target start`), and the retired knobs `SQUEEZEFS_SPDK_TGT_BIN` / `SQUEEZEFS_NVMEOF_RUN_DIR`. What is deleted: the pinned v26.05 build/install, hugepage management, the pidfile lifecycle, the JSON-RPC client, and the SPDK-only flags (`--accept-version-drift`, `unshare --force`, `--hugemem-mb`, `--restore-prior`, `--core-mask`, `--cores`, `--dpdk-mem-mb` — they die on clap, never a silent accept). **An SPDK share still in the share ledger stays decodable and is never re-presented**: `nvmeof list` shows it as `spdk — RETIRED target stack` with the sequence below, `nvmeof restore` reports it `skipped` **and exits nonzero until the record is removed** (so `target start` and the nvmet oneshot unit stay red), `nvmeof unshare` removes ONLY its ledger record, and the ledger's duplicate-backing guard holds its backing until that record is gone.
>
> **The re-share sequence** (the operator's path off every SPDK share):
> 1. `sudo squeezefs nvmeof unshare <subnqn>` — removes the SPDK ledger record only (no `spdk_tgt` is driven) and releases the backing for step 3. The verb prints the state-dir residue the record leaves (below); `nvmeof list` prints the same `Residue:` line for every SPDK record still present.
> 2. If an `spdk_tgt` still serves the old subsystem, tear it down yourself: `rpc.py nvmf_delete_subsystem <subnqn>`, `rpc.py bdev_aio_delete <bdev>` — SqueezeFS no longer speaks SPDK RPC.
> 3. `sudo squeezefs nvmeof share <backing> --ip <ip> [--subnqn <nqn>] [--ns-uuid <uuid>]` — nvmet is the default (`--target-stack nvmet` is optional). Re-use the old `--ns-uuid` if initiators must reattach under the same namespace identity.
>
> **Upgrading a host that ran the SPDK target** — three things the pre-retirement lifecycle left that the sequence above does not touch, in the order to take them:
> 0. **The old SPDK systemd unit** (emitted by `target systemd-unit --target-stack spdk`: `ExecStart=<spdk_tgt> -r <sock> -m <mask> -s <MiB>` + `ExecStartPost=squeezefs nvmeof restore --target-stack spdk`). On the upgraded binary its `ExecStartPost` refuses, so at the next boot systemd marks the unit failed and **stops `spdk_tgt`** — the SPDK shares go dark, loudly, with the refusal text in the journal. Before upgrading (or right after, if it already happened): `systemctl disable --now <unit>` and delete the unit file; the nvmet oneshot unit (`target systemd-unit`, default stack) takes its place once step 3 has re-shared. **`nvmeof restore` (and so `target start` and that oneshot unit) exits nonzero while any SPDK record remains in the ledger** — the unit stays red until step 1 has run for every SPDK share; a green unit over an unserved share is not a state this binary produces.
> 4. **The hugepage reservation** `target setup --hugemem-mb` made is now unmanaged: `--restore-prior` is gone with the stack. Undo it by hand from the value the retired verb recorded — `cat <state>/spdk/hugepages-prior` (the pre-setup `nr_hugepages`), then `echo <prior> > /sys/kernel/mm/hugepages/hugepages-2048kB/nr_hugepages` once no `spdk_tgt` runs (the kernel refuses to lower below in-use pages).
> 5. **State-dir residue** under `<state>/spdk/` (`ptpl/<uuid>.json` per share, `tgt-config.json`, `hugepages-prior`; `<state>` = `SQUEEZEFS_NVMEOF_STATE_DIR`, default `/var/lib/squeezefs/nvmeof`): the binary never touches it — `rm -r <state>/spdk/` once every SPDK record is gone from `nvmeof list` and step 4 is done.
>
> A retired knob left in the environment — `SQUEEZEFS_SPDK_TGT_BIN` or `SQUEEZEFS_NVMEOF_RUN_DIR` in an old unit's `Environment=`, a rig box's shell profile, an `EnvironmentFile` — refuses **every** `squeezefs` verb at startup, `mount` included (the ENG-10 retired-knob law is process-wide); unset it wherever it was exported.
>
> The 2026-07 dual-stack A/B (`.benchmarks/2026-07-17-spdk-target-scoping.md`, `.benchmarks/2026-07-18-nvmeof-dual-stack-ab.md`) stands as history — it measured SPDK's queued-I/O wins (a dedicated busy-polling core as the entry price) against nvmet's QD1 latency and zero-install posture; the retirement is a registrant-ceiling ruling, not a re-measurement.

The top-level **`squeezefs nvmeof`** verb shares and dismantles NVMe-oF target subsystems on the kernel nvmet target, manages its readiness, and connects clients (runbooks: [QUICKSTART §4](../QUICKSTART.md#4-nvme-of-fabric-setup-remote-block-storage)). The **kernel-nvmet path** is the rebuilt configfs plumbing (reserved port-id range 53000–53999 with ownership checks, `resv_enable`+`device_uuid` stamped before enable, loop handling via the ledger) riding the write-ahead intent ledger, a working `restore`, and a **live-state duplicate-backing guard** (the same backing must never be double-served — refusals name the live holder and the exact removal steps). Target-stack selection is `--target-stack nvmet` (or `SQUEEZEFS_NVMEOF_TARGET_STACK=nvmet`), the default; the retired `spdk` refuses loud (above).

```bash
squeezefs nvmeof share <backing> --ip <ip>[,...] [--port 4420] [--subnqn <nqn>] \
    [--target-stack nvmet] [--ns-uuid <uuid>] \
    [--create-size <sz>] [--allow-host <hostnqn>]...
squeezefs nvmeof unshare <subnqn>         # stack resolved from the share ledger;
                                           # a retired-SPDK record is removed ledger-only
squeezefs nvmeof list [--json]             # managed/down/pending/removing/foreign/retired-spdk
squeezefs nvmeof restore [--target-stack nvmet]
                                           # replay ledger; reconcile intents; SPDK records skipped loud
squeezefs nvmeof adopt <subnqn> [--target-stack nvmet]  # absorb a live foreign share
                                           # into the ledger — target state never touched
squeezefs nvmeof target setup [--target-stack nvmet]     # modprobe + configfs checks
squeezefs nvmeof target start [--target-stack nvmet]     # readiness + ledger restore (configfs IS the running target)
squeezefs nvmeof target stop [--target-stack nvmet]      # always refuses: the kernel target is not a process
squeezefs nvmeof target status [--json] [--target-stack nvmet]  # modules, configfs, subsystem/namespace/port counts
squeezefs nvmeof target systemd-unit [--target-stack nvmet]     # the oneshot restore unit, emitted to stdout, never installed
squeezefs nvmeof connect --ip <ip> --subnqn <nqn> [--port 4420] \
    [--host-traddr <addr>] [--host-iface <if>] [--nr-io-queues <n>] \
    [--hostnqn <nqn> --hostid <uuid>]      # per-connection PR identity, pair-or-neither
squeezefs nvmeof disconnect <nqn>
```

`connect`'s optional flags: `--host-traddr` / `--host-iface` pin the source address / interface when several local interfaces share a subnet (routing alone cannot pick the path); `--nr-io-queues` caps the I/O-queue request at a target that offers fewer than the initiator asks for by default (min 1); `--hostnqn` + `--hostid` present a **per-connection host identity** — each distinct pair is its own PR registrant on the target (the co-located distinct-identity fencing row in [the guarantee tables](#write-custody--visibility-by-mount-posture-the-54-guarantee-tables)), configured pair-or-neither because a mismatched pair is how registrants alias; the default is the `/etc/nvme` convention.

Flag semantics: `--ns-uuid` seeds the recorded namespace identity (generated once when absent; stamped as nvmet `device_uuid`; re-presented by `restore` so initiators reattach). The kernel-nvmet namespace index is structurally fixed at 1 — the hidden `--nsid` flag survives only so a value ≠ 1 refuses loud (never a silent flag-ignore). Missing backing paths refuse loud (`--create-size` is the explicit sparse-create opt-in; NoCOW-guarded on btrfs). `--allow-host` (repeatable) restricts a share to named host NQNs; the default is allow-any — the trusted-fabric posture. `unshare` resolves the stack from the ledger; unmount → `disconnect` → unshare is the sequence. Target-verb semantics: `target setup` loads `nvmet`/`nvmet-tcp` and checks the configfs mount; `target start` is the same readiness check plus a ledger `restore` (idempotent — configfs is the running target, so a second start reports the shares as verified no-ops); `target stop` refuses (there is no process — tear shares down with `unshare`); `target status` reports module presence, configfs and subsystem/namespace/port counts; `target systemd-unit` emits a `Type=oneshot` unit whose `ExecStart` is `squeezefs nvmeof restore --target-stack nvmet` (values baked at emission; the operator installs it).

**`adopt` semantics** (design §6.10): an **explicit operator action** — never automatic, never another verb's fallback — that absorbs a live **foreign** (unledgered) share into management by **writing only the share ledger; the live target object is untouched**, so data keeps serving with zero interruption (the pre-rebuild-share and ledger-loss recoveries both ride it). It probes the kernel nvmet target's live state (`--target-stack nvmet` is the only admissible value — the retired `spdk` refuses loud). The live object's identity is read as-is — backing, listeners (out-of-range nvmet port ids recorded verbatim; teardown removes them only when link-free), namespace UUID, allow-host list — with **loud nulls** where the object exposes nothing (a missing `device_uuid` records null with a note that a re-share upgrades it). Adopt refuses loud on five named classes: `adopt_not_live`, `adopt_already_ledgered` (NQN **or** backing, any intent state — `restore`/`unshare` territory; a record the retired SPDK stack wrote names the re-share sequence), `adopt_backing_duplicated` (the duplicate-backing guard applies verbatim), `adopt_harness_owned` (test-fabric NQNs/ports are never absorbed), `adopt_shape_unsupported` (namespace index ≠ 1 / multi-namespace / listener-less shapes — removal-first + re-share is the remediation). Absorption rides the write-ahead intent protocol with `adopted_from` provenance (surfaced by `list`) and re-verifies live state just before finalizing (drift aborts loud and leaves nothing behind). After adoption the share is fully managed — `list`/`restore`/`unshare` treat it like any other.

Target-side health and initiator-side fabric signals (`target status`, `fabric_*`, the guard readings): see [Fabric observability](#fabric-observability).

## Performance records

> **fio engine policy (user ruling 2026-08-07, `.benchmarks/2026-08-07-fio-engine-policy.md`):** every throughput/IOPS row runs `ioengine=libaio --direct=1` with a stated iodepth on BOTH lanes (kernel and il — the il lane rides the v1.1 aio interposers), every A/B comparison uses the SAME engine both sides, and psync survives only as explicitly-labeled §5.5.1 sync-lane coverage rows (never a headline, never cross-lane compared); `io_uring` is a labeled kernel-lane-only extra.

Every number traces to a committed `.benchmarks/` note (box/substrate/method inside each). Two venues carry the record: the **field** (squeeze-test — the fabric the product is built for) and the **reference dev box** (the 2026-07 programs' controlled-latency substrate). The README's four hero numbers are the first field row below.

### Field records (squeeze-test)

**Venue:** 5 storage nodes over nvme-tcp, memory-backed (nullblk) NVMe targets; client 32-core Xeon 6426Y, 251 GB RAM, 2×200 GbE; cacheless mount with `--interception`. Two lanes per row: **kern** (kernel FUSE-over-io_uring) and **il** (`LD_PRELOAD=libsqueezefs_il.so`, same-commit pairing). Tier: measured-real. 4 KiB rows are 24 jobs × iodepth 8, closed-loop (≈ 190 ops in flight), so every il-vs-kern delta is a latency delta at equal depth.

### The multi-reference scoreboard (release gate)

`tests/run_scoreboard.sh` is the standing **multi-reference scoreboard** — the proof surface for the top-3-fastest-FUSE-filesystems directive (PERFORMANCE IS PRIMARY, 2026-07-18). It measures SqueezeFS against the reference fast-FUSE field on one substrate with matched budgets and identical elbencho drivers: **JuiceFS** (meta engine + `file://` objstore), **SeaweedFS native** (`weed server` master+volume+filer, `weed mount`), and **geesefs** + **mountpoint-s3** over one shared local **RustFS** S3 store (object store held constant so those two rows differ only by client) — all pinned releases with recorded checksums, fetched into a non-repo tools dir. The grid is unchanged from the vs-JuiceFS lineage it absorbed: **three regimes** — R1 as-deployed (all cache layers live, dataset 2–4× cache), R2 device-true (sqz `-o direct_device_true` verified by counters; refs by cache-off knobs + tight cages on the page-cache-serving process, verified by device-byte evidence per row), R3 cold-cache (full drops + client cache wipes, first pass) — × the 6-shape workload grid (seq write/read 1 MiB, rand read/write 4k at `t16 iodepth16 --direct`, stat storm, del storm).

**RW6 durability-leveled timing:** write-family rows carry two modes. *Relaxed* is each system's native ACK semantics (published, labeled, never gating). *Durable* — which **governs the write-family verdicts** — is fsync-inclusive at matched depth: the harness times fdatasync on every dataset file through the mount, then syncfs on the mount, then syncfs on the substrate (every system's backing store flushed to the device before the clock stops). elbencho's `--sync` cannot deliver this (verified from v3.1-9 source: it is a separate post-phase syncfs step, excluded from the WRITE row, and several FUSE clients no-op `FUSE_SYNCFS`), so the pass is the harness's own, identical for every system. **RW6-del** applies the same pattern to the delete family: del rows carry relaxed (labeled) + *durable* (governs) modes, where durable adds a timed pass of tree-dir + mount-root fsync (the client flush lever — geesefs documents dir-fsync flushes all pending changes, deletes included, and its syncfs is not FUSE-wired) then mount + substrate syncfs (store settle). The historical `R1/R3.seq_write_1m` allowlist (JuiceFS's page-cache-ACK artifact) is retired, and the inaugural run's three adjudicated entries (`R1/R2.del_storm.gee`, `R2.seq_write_1m.jfs`) were deleted the same day their follow-ups landed (RW6-del + the `direct_device_true` write-path-inertness pin — see the baseline report's 2026-07-18 addendum) — **the standing allowlist is ∅ (bare run)**.

**Capability matrix:** where a reference does not support a workload *by design*, the cell reads **N/S (not supported by design)** — neutral, never "0 IOPS", never a LOSS, excluded from rank denominators. The matrix is declarative in the harness (one-line reason per cell) and verified empirically at mount time (the refusal errno is recorded in `capabilities.tsv`; a probe that succeeds un-declares the cell loudly). Current matrix: `mps3.rand_write_4k` (sequential-upload semantics; EBADF verified).

```bash
tests/run_scoreboard.sh                      # full scoreboard (~2–4 h, quiet-gated)
tests/run_scoreboard.sh teardown             # kill owned daemons + wipe stores
SQUEEZEFS_SB_SMOKE=1 tests/run_scoreboard.sh # ~10 min micro-grid plumbing proof (per-commit tier)
```

**The gate:** the run emits the primary kernel-FUSE table (per-reference W/L/TIE at ±5%, SqueezeFS rank per row, per-row-family **top-3 adjudication**) plus a labeled relaxed-write table, machine TSV, and per-row raw evidence (elbencho output + CSV, `.stats`/metrics counter snapshots, diskstats deltas, durability-pass splits, honesty lines), and **exits nonzero on any unattributed LOSS or any INVALID SqueezeFS cell** in the primary table. `SQUEEZEFS_SB_ALLOW_LOSS="R1.foo,R1.foo.jfs,..."` names attributed losses (row-wide or per-reference); a reference whose own setup fails 3× becomes an n/a-with-reason column (named residual), never a run abort — the SqueezeFS side always gates hard. Legacy `SQUEEZEFS_VS_*` env spellings are honored. Cadence: **per-release** and after any perf-relevant landing.

**Current standing (inaugural multi-reference run, 2026-07-18): see `.benchmarks/2026-07-18-multi-reference-scoreboard.md`** — provenance, the full grid, per-family top-3 adjudication, and the honest-anomalies ledger. JuiceFS-only lineage: 13 W / 3 TIE closing standing `.benchmarks/2026-07-17-rand-write-program-closing.md` §2, inaugural baseline `.benchmarks/2026-07-15-vs-juicefs-scoreboard.md` (both measured with the retired `tests/run_vs_juicefs.sh` protocol this harness absorbed).

### Built-in benchmark (`squeezefs bench`)

A **bare invocation is the full saturation suite**: over one auto-sized dataset it runs write seq `1m` `--direct` → read seq `1m` `--direct` → read rand `4k` `--direct` (30 s box) → write rand `4k` `--direct` (30 s box) → stat → del (timed; leaves the mount clean), and prints one table with a row per pass (THROUGHPUT / IOPS / coverage / latency min/avg/p99/max) plus the daemon's `.stats` metrics delta.

```bash
squeezefs bench /mnt/squeezefs
```

Auto-sizing (the default for `-t`/`-n`/`-s` everywhere; explicit flags always override): threads = `min(CPUs, 16)`, 1 file per thread, total = `max(16 GiB, 2 GiB × threads)` capped at 25% of the mountpoint's free space (loud error if even 4 GiB does not fit), per-file rounded down to 1 MiB. The computed shape — with `(auto)`/`(explicit)` provenance per value — is printed loudly in the header of **every** run.

*Explicit phases* run over the same **persistent, reusable dataset** at `<mountpoint>/squeezefs-bench/t{tid}/f{fid}.bin` and inherit the identical auto defaults, so single-phase numbers are directly comparable to the matching suite pass:

```bash
# reproduce the suite's rand-4k read pass against an existing dataset:
squeezefs bench /mnt/squeezefs -r --rand -b 4k --direct
# write then read 1 GiB/file across 4 threads at 1 MiB ops:
squeezefs bench /mnt/squeezefs -t 4 -w -r -s 1g -b 1m
# re-read the SAME dataset later at a different I/O size (no rewrite):
squeezefs bench /mnt/squeezefs -t 4 -r -s 1g -b 128k
```

*Phases* (any combination, always executed in this fixed order; none given ⇒ the full suite):
- `-w, --write`: create/overwrite the dataset, timed (includes create/open; each file is fsync'd before the clock stops — durable write numbers).
- `-r, --read`: read it back, timed (reuses the dataset from an earlier `-w`; loud shape-mismatch error otherwise — never silently creates files).
- `--stat`: stat every file, timed.
- `--del`: delete the dataset, timed (doubles as cleanup).

*Shape* (applies to all phases; `-t`/`-n`/`-s` auto-size when omitted):
- `-t, --threads <N>`: workers (default: auto = `min(CPUs, 16)`).
- `-n, --files <N>`: files per thread (default: auto = 1).
- `-s, --size <SZ>`: file size, human units `4k`/`128k`/`4m`/`10g` or plain bytes (default: auto-sized from free space, see above).
- `-b, --block <SZ>`: I/O size per operation, same units (default: `1m`; explicit phase runs only — the suite fixes `1m` seq / `4k` rand).
- `--rand`: random offsets (shuffled full-coverage block list — every block exactly once; explicit phase runs only).
- `--direct`: O_DIRECT (`-b` must be a multiple of 4096 and `-s` a multiple of `-b`); the suite's I/O passes are always O_DIRECT.
- `--time <SECS>`: wall-clock box for rand read/write passes (default: 30 for `--rand`, unlimited for sequential; `0` forces full coverage). Partial coverage is honest — reported over actual elapsed/bytes and stated in the row.
- `-i, --iterations <N>`: repeat the selected pass set (default: 1).

Write phases fill blocks with a deterministic non-zero pattern seeded per `(thread, file, block)`, so transparent compression cannot fake throughput numbers.
